# Chef App — Project Plan & Grounding Notes

Living document. Read this first in any new session to recover context. Update it whenever a phase completes, a decision is made, or something is discovered that the next session needs to know.

## What this is

A locally hosted, AI-driven, web-connected meal planning, food inventory, and recipe management app for a household (default 2 people). Runs via Docker Compose. Ships on GitHub so others can pull it down and self-host. Uses Ollama for local LLM inference and Tavily for web search grounding.

## Origin note

This project supersedes an earlier prototype built inside a personal "Local AI" Open WebUI stack (Ollama + mcpo + ComfyUI + n8n + a `chef-api` container, Excel workbook as datastore). That prototype is not part of this repo. Some of its design patterns (interactive tool surface, autonomous weekly meal-plan job, avoiding spreadsheet-as-database) were salvaged and are reflected in the decisions below.

## Author's environment

- CPU: Intel 6700K, Z170A chipset motherboard
- GPU: dual GTX 1080 Ti (second card runs headless — usable for compute/second Ollama model or ComfyUI-style workloads, not display)
- Ollama: already available in the environment (assume host-installed unless told otherwise)
- Tavily: API key already held by the author
- Docker host: WSL2 Debian. The project folder (`C:\Users\JBentley\Claude\Projects\Chef` on Windows) is reached from the WSL Debian docker host at:
  ```
  /mnt/c/Users/JBentley/Claude/Projects/chef
  ```
  Run `docker compose` commands from that path inside WSL, not from a Windows shell.

## Architecture decisions (confirmed 2026-07-30)

| Decision | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Backend | Python + FastAPI | Best local-LLM/Ollama ecosystem support, async, auto API docs, matches prior prototype's language |
| Frontend | React (Vite) SPA | Needed for a chat panel that stays alive across page navigation; richer interactivity for inventory/recipe editing |
| Database | SQLite (file-backed, via SQLAlchemy + Alembic) | "Lightweight local DB" per project brief; avoids the Excel file-locking problem hit in the prior prototype |
| Styling | Single centralized CSS (CSS variables / design tokens) imported app-wide | Project brief requires one-source theming |
| Containerization | Docker Compose: `backend`, `frontend` services, SQLite + uploaded images on a named volume for persistence across rebuilds | Required by project brief |
| LLM | Ollama, base URL configurable via `.env` / Settings GUI (defaults to `http://host.docker.internal:11434`, override for a containerized Ollama) | Author already runs Ollama in his environment |
| Web search | Tavily API, key entered via Settings GUI or `.env`, never hardcoded | Author has an existing key |
| Settings/secrets storage | Database (`AppSetting` table), not `.env`. `.env` only for infra bootstrap (`DATABASE_URL`, ports) plus optional first-run seed convenience. Secrets encrypted at rest with Fernet, key rotation supported | Author's explicit preference (2026-07-30), matching the pattern already validated in the sibling Fiduciary project — see "Settings & secrets storage" section below |

Open question for a future session: does the author want Ollama itself running inside this repo's Docker Compose (with GPU passthrough configured for the 1080 Tis), or does it stay a separate/host service that this app just points at? Scaffolding currently assumes the latter (external Ollama) since "Ollama is available in the environment" was stated as a given. Revisit if the author wants it bundled.

## Repo layout

```
Chef/
  PROJECT-PLAN.md        <- this file, keep current
  README.md               <- setup instructions for external users
  .env.example
  docker-compose.yml
  backend/
    Dockerfile
    requirements.txt
    app/
      main.py            <- FastAPI app entrypoint
      config.py          <- settings (pydantic-settings, reads .env)
      database.py        <- SQLAlchemy engine/session
      models/            <- ORM models (Phase 1+)
      routers/           <- API routers (Phase 1+)
      services/          <- business logic, Ollama/Tavily clients (Phase 2+)
  frontend/
    Dockerfile
    package.json
    index.html
    vite.config.js
    src/
      main.jsx
      App.jsx
      styles/theme.css   <- centralized design tokens
      pages/
      components/
  data/                  <- gitignored; SQLite file + uploaded images live here, volume-mounted
```

## Author's stated dietary/health context (for grounding meal-plan design, Phase 5/6)

- Gluten-free, celiac-friendly focus
- Quick preparations preferred, leftovers welcome
- Sedentary lifestyle, goal to reduce LDL/bad cholesterol
- Weight loss not a core goal, but both household members ~20 lbs over ideal weight
- Household size default: 2

## Phase 3 notes: inventory

Backend (`backend/app/routers/inventory.py`, `app/services/inventory_service.py`, `app/schemas/inventory.py`):

- Full CRUD: `GET/POST /api/inventory`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/inventory/{id}`, with `category`/`is_priority`/`search` filters on the list endpoint.
- **Urgency scoring** (`compute_urgency`): points for expired (100) / expiring ≤3d (80) / ≤7d (50) / ≤14d (20) days, staleness (unused >60d: 30, >30d: 15), and the `is_priority` flag (25) — plus human-readable reasons. `GET /api/inventory/priority-suggestions` returns items ranked by this score; the meal-planning engine (Phase 5) is meant to consume the same function directly, not just the endpoint.
- **Vision photo intake**: `POST /api/inventory/vision-intake` sends the uploaded image + a JSON-array-only prompt to the configured Ollama vision model and returns a *preview* (nothing written to inventory yet). `POST /api/inventory/vision-intake/confirm` bulk-creates from a (user-reviewed/edited) item list. Response parsing (`parse_vision_response`) is defensive — tries strict JSON, falls back to extracting the first `[...]` block — because real vision-model output often wraps JSON in prose/markdown fences; unit-tested against both cases plus garbage input and an invalid category value.
- **Deduction primitive** (`deduct_by_name`): case-insensitive exact-then-substring match against inventory by name, decrements quantity floored at 0, stamps `last_used_date`. This is what Phase 5 (confirming a meal-plan entry) and Phase 7 (chat: "we made X" / "we're out of Y") will call — matching recipe ingredient names to inventory names is inherently fuzzy; a smarter matcher (aliases, unit conversion) is a documented future improvement, not attempted here.

Frontend (`frontend/src/pages/InventoryPage.jsx`, `components/InventoryItemForm.jsx`): category filter, add/edit form (shared component), delete, and a photo-upload flow that shows the detected-items preview before anything is committed. Rows are color-coded by urgency score using the same reasons the backend computed. Switched to `HashRouter` (was a single static page) with a nav bar for Home/Inventory.

**Quantity model, redesigned 2026-08-02 (B4.5) -- see that section's own notes below for the full rationale.** `quantity`/`unit` now mean "current on-hand amount, in a real convertible measurement unit" (not the compound "8 oz bag"-style string this app originally used). Three new purely-descriptive package fields (`package_quantity`, `package_count`, `package_descriptor`) capture container info without it ever being read by deduction/cost arithmetic, and `purchased_quantity` is an immutable purchase-time snapshot B6.1's cost math divides by instead of the live, depleting `quantity`. `app/services/package_parsing.py` is the shared best-effort text-to-structured-fields parser feeding this from barcode/receipt/order-import sources.

**Known simplification: frontend/backend origins.** Docker Compose runs `frontend` (5173) and `backend` (8095) as separate containers with no reverse proxy between them. In dev (`npm run dev`), the vite proxy in `vite.config.js` makes `/api/*` and `/health` same-origin. In the production Docker build, `frontend/src/api.js` falls back to hitting the backend directly at `${hostname}:8095` (hardcoded port, matching `.env`'s `BACKEND_PORT` default) using the backend's wide-open CORS. This works for the common single-host case but is fragile if `BACKEND_PORT` is changed without updating `api.js` too, and doesn't support serving over HTTPS/a real domain cleanly. A shared reverse proxy (nginx/Caddy sidecar, single external port) is a reasonable Phase 9 packaging improvement.

**Verification:** unit tests for `parse_vision_response` (4 cases) and `deduct_by_name` (exact match, fuzzy match, no match, floor-at-zero) run directly against the service module; full CRUD + priority-suggestions + bulk vision-confirm exercised via curl against a live local uvicorn instance; `vision-intake` itself confirmed to fail cleanly with a 502 (no live Ollama in the sandbox) rather than crashing. Frontend: `npm run build` succeeds; ran the real Vite dev server against the real backend together and hit `/health` and `/api/inventory` (including a POST) through the actual proxy path the browser will use, not just each side in isolation.

## Operational note: bash sandbox / native file coherence

Claude's bash sandbox sees this folder through a FUSE mount that can cache stale file sizes -- if Claude edits a file with the Read/Write/Edit tools and then reads/git-adds/executes it via bash in the same session, the bash side can occasionally get truncated/stale content instead of the true file (documented and verified in the sibling Fiduciary project, `docs/COWORK-FUSE-COHERENCE.md`). Checked on 2026-07-30: every file that had been through this mixed edit pattern so far (`config.py`, `main.py`, `.gitignore`, `README.md`, `.env.example`, `seed.py`, this file) was verified intact -- Read tool content matched `git show HEAD:<path>` exactly, no corruption found. Going forward, prefer a single writer surface (bash heredoc/sed, or the Edit/Write tool) per file for its whole edit-test-commit cycle, and spot-check with the Read tool before trusting a bash-side commit of a file that was just edited natively.

## Notes / gotchas

- Do not reintroduce a spreadsheet as the live datastore — caused file-lock issues in the prior prototype. SQLite file lives under `data/`, only the backend container writes to it.
- Keep secrets (Tavily key, etc.) out of version control — `.env` is gitignored, `.env.example` documents required vars.
- Fiduciary Project (a sibling app the author built, `C:\Users\JBentley\Claude\Projects\Fiduciary`) holds reusable patterns -- connected and read directly for `secrets_crypto.py` (Phase 1/2) and the knowledge/RAG design (this session, 2026-07-31). Not connected by default each session; request it again if a future session needs it.

## Backlog / planned enhancements

Repopulated 2026-07-31 from the capstone competitive-research pass (see "Capstone research" section near the end of this file for the landscape findings these items derive from), then extended the same day with a group of author-requested items (B10). Items are grouped by theme and ordered roughly by value-per-effort within each group. Groups B1-B9 are candidates, not commitments; **B10 items are stated author wants and carry higher priority weight than a same-effort item elsewhere in this list.** The two earlier backlog items (recipe dish images, RAG upgrade) are done -- see their own notes sections.

**Dependency notes worth reading before sequencing any of this.** Three items are load-bearing for others and are cheaper to do early than to retrofit: **B1.1** (food-database resolution) unblocks accurate nutrition (B1.2-B1.4), diet-quality scoring (B2.2), barcode intake (B4.1), and the weight-based unit mode (B10.5). **B5.3/B10.5** (one shared unit-conversion layer, two surfaces) unblocks accurate inventory deduction and grocery aggregation as well as the recipe unit toggle. **B10.4** (responsive UI) is a prerequisite for the cook-mode/timer/PWA cluster (B7.1-B7.3) being usable on the device people actually cook with. Two items also absorb earlier ones outright: **B10.2** supersedes B4.4 and B9.4, and **B10.3** partly delivers B6.1 and B4.3 as side effects.

### B1 -- Nutrition data integrity (highest-value gap)

The single largest correctness gap in the app today: `Recipe.nutrition` is a free-form JSON dict populated entirely by whatever the LLM estimated at import/generation time. Nothing in the codebase references an authoritative food-composition source. Every downstream feature the brief cares about (cholesterol steering, calorie counts, BMI-aware portioning) currently rests on a hallucination-prone number that is never checked. Published research on LLM nutrition advice makes the same point: models that do not encode population reference-intake data can produce recommendations without appropriate nutritional constraints.

- [x] **B1.1 -- Ingredient-to-food-database resolution layer.** Done 2026-07-31 -- see notes section below. New service that maps a `RecipeIngredient` name+quantity+unit to a canonical food record and returns per-100g nutrients. Primary source: USDA FoodData Central (free, government-backed, ~300K+ foods, API key required, no barcode/branded coverage). Secondary: Open Food Facts (free, crowd-sourced, ~2.5M+ products, has barcodes) for packaged/branded items USDA lacks. Both are free and self-host-friendly, which matters for a project meant to be pulled down by external users -- Edamam/Nutritionix/Spoonacular are all paid past a low free tier and would put a per-user API bill in front of anyone cloning the repo. Store the resolved FDC id / OFF barcode on the ingredient row so a resolution is done once, not per request.
- [x] **B1.2 -- Computed vs. estimated nutrition, visibly distinguished.** Done 2026-07-31 -- see notes section below.
- [x] **B1.3 -- Expand the nutrition dict, and reconcile the two prompts that disagree about it.** Done 2026-07-31, together with B1.2 -- see notes section below. Added saturated fat and total sugars (both cleanly available from USDA/OFF); added_sugars_g and soluble_fiber_g were investigated and deliberately NOT added -- neither source cleanly distinguishes them from total sugars/fiber, and this app doesn't mislabel one nutrient as another.
- [x] **B1.4 -- Daily and weekly nutrient roll-up.** Nothing currently sums a day or a week. Add a per-day and per-week nutrition view over a `MealPlan`, compared against DRI-derived targets computed from each `HouseholdMember`'s age/sex/height/weight/activity (all already in the schema and unused for this). This is the feature that turns logged body metrics into an actual feedback loop rather than a chart nobody acts on. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**

### B2 -- Diet-quality scoring and guideline grounding

- [x] **B2.1 -- Ship curated guideline knowledge files as repo defaults.** The knowledge/RAG plumbing exists and works, but the repo ships with an empty corpus, so every external user starts ungrounded and the "grounded in nutritionist knowledge" requirement is technically unmet out of the box. Bundle a small set of public-domain/openly-licensed reference documents seeded as inactive-by-default knowledge files the user can toggle on: Dietary Guidelines for Americans, DRI reference tables, cholesterol-specific patterns (Portfolio diet, DASH, Mediterranean), **and official food-allergy/allergen research** (author-requested 2026-07-31) -- e.g. NIAID's Guidelines for the Diagnosis and Management of Food Allergy, and FDA/FALCPA-and-FASTER-Act major-allergen reference material (the nine major allergens: milk, eggs, fish, crustacean shellfish, tree nuts, peanuts, wheat, soybeans, sesame). This corpus doubles as reference grounding for B3.1/B3.2's structured allergen/cross-contact model, not just free-text dietary advice. Check licensing per document before bundling; US federal/NIH government works are generally public domain but verify each. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B2.2 -- Diet-quality score on a generated plan.** HEI-2020 (Healthy Eating Index, 0-100, 13 components: 9 adequacy, 4 moderation) is the standard, published, computable US diet-quality metric -- the national average is 58/100, so there is a real reference point to show against. Scoring a generated week gives the planner an objective quality signal instead of "the model said it was balanced," and gives the user a single number that moves. Requires B1.1/B1.3 first (needs real food-group and nutrient data). **Completed 2026-08-01 as an HONEST APPROXIMATION, not the certified index -- see notes section below for exactly what that means and why.**
- [x] **B2.3 -- Portfolio-diet-aware LDL steering.** The author's stated goal is LDL reduction. The Portfolio diet is the pattern explicitly engineered for that (plant sterols, viscous/soluble fiber, nuts, soy protein), overlapping but more targeted than DASH or Mediterranean. Add it as a selectable dietary goal preset that concretely biases ingredient selection, rather than leaving "reduce cholesterol" as free text in the goals field for the model to interpret differently every week. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**

### B3 -- Celiac and allergen safety

- [x] **B3.1 -- First-class allergen/restriction model.** Dietary restrictions are currently free-text JSON on `HouseholdPreferences`, interpreted by the LLM per prompt. Nothing structurally prevents a generated plan or an imported recipe from containing gluten. Add a structured allergen/restriction taxonomy and a deterministic (non-LLM) check that flags any recipe or plan entry containing a restricted ingredient, run at import, at plan generation, and at plan confirm. **Completed 2026-07-31 -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B3.2 -- Cross-contact awareness, not just ingredient matching.** Best-in-class celiac tools (Fig, Spoonful) go beyond the label: Fig lets the user set an observance level (must avoid gluten and cross-contamination not ok / gluten only / trying to avoid), and Spoonful flags cross-contact risks such as non-certified oats. Chef should carry the same observance-level setting and flag known cross-contact ingredients (oats, shared-equipment items, bulk-bin staples) rather than treating "no wheat in the ingredient list" as safe. This is a genuine safety feature for the stated primary user, not a nicety. **Completed 2026-07-31 -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B3.3 -- Recall awareness.** The USDA FSIS Recall API and the openFDA food enforcement API are both free and public; USDA's own FoodKeeper app pushes recall notifications. A daily/weekly check of current inventory item names and brands against active recalls is a differentiator essentially no consumer meal planner offers, and is cheap given inventory is already structured. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**

### B4 -- Inventory capture friction

Inventory quality determines whether every other feature works, and manual entry is where these apps die. Chef currently has manual entry, vision photo intake, and chat -- but not the two mechanisms that dominate the category.

- [x] **B4.1 -- Barcode scanning to Open Food Facts.** Tandoor does exactly this (scan, look up in OpenFoodFacts, get name and nutrition). A browser-based scanner plus an OFF lookup gives near-zero-friction addition of packaged goods. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- built with ZXing (`@zxing/browser`), not the originally-scoped `BarcodeDetector` API with a JS fallback: verified live that Safari and every iOS/iPadOS browser (WebKit-only there) do not implement `BarcodeDetector`, with no Apple timeline to add it, and the author explicitly needed phone/iPad support with no physical scanner -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B4.2 -- Grocery receipt OCR intake.** The category standard is now a receipt photo that populates inventory automatically with quantities and prices; the leading apps claim 95%+ line-item accuracy. Chef already has a vision model wired up and an established preview-then-confirm intake pattern, so this is largely a new prompt plus a review screen, not new architecture. Biggest win per unit of effort in this group. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- author-requested directly (receipt photo/PDF, or a plain-text/file list of purchased items), generalized beyond photo-only OCR. See notes section below.**
- [x] **B4.3 -- FoodKeeper-backed default shelf life.** USDA publishes FoodKeeper as a machine-readable data feed covering storage guidance for 500+ food and beverage items. Use it to auto-suggest an expiration date when the user adds an item without one -- directly serving the brief's "keep expiration information as much as reasonably possible" and the stated forgotten-pantry-item problem, which currently depends on the user remembering to type a date. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B4.4 -- Expiration notifications.** Every competitor in the food-waste space notifies ahead of expiry (typically 7 days out and on the day) with color-coded status. Chef computes urgency server-side already but only surfaces it passively when the user opens the Inventory page. Add a digest (in-app banner at minimum; optional email/webhook) so the app reaches out rather than waiting to be visited. **Completed 2026-08-01 as part of B10.2 -- the in-app banner (the stated minimum); push/email deliberately not built, see B10.2's notes section.**
- [x] **B4.5 -- Inventory quantity/packaging model redesign (author-requested 2026-08-02).** Author-reported design gap: `quantity`/`unit` conflated "how many packages" with a compound package-size-plus-container string (e.g. `unit="8 oz bag"`), which isn't a real convertible unit -- this silently broke recipe-confirm inventory deduction (a partial-package use could zero out the whole row) and B6.1's cost-per-serving math (which divided by the live, depleting on-hand quantity instead of a stable purchase-time snapshot, so cost-per-serving drifted upward as a package got used). Also author-requested: separate "on hand" quantity (what recipe confirmation should deduct from) from "at time of purchase" quantity (what cost math should divide by). **Completed 2026-08-02 -- see notes section below.**

### B5 -- Meal-plan mechanics

- [x] **B5.1 -- Explicit leftovers modeling.** The brief says "leftovers welcome" and the author cooks for two; the plan engine has no concept of one cook event feeding multiple slots. Plan to Eat schedules leftovers as first-class calendar items and Eat This Much merges cook events to cut stove time. Add a leftover/portion link between plan entries so a Sunday cook can legitimately fill Monday lunch, with inventory deducted once rather than twice. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B5.2 -- Prep-day / batch-cooking mode.** Mealime Pro's "Meal Prep Day" batch-cooks proteins and grains for mix-and-match assembly; MealPrepPro builds whole weeks around a single cooking afternoon. Add a generation mode that clusters shared components into one prep session, then composes weekday meals from them. Fits the author's stated quick-preparation preference better than seven independent cook events. **Completed 2026-08-02 as a generation-PROMPT heuristic, not a new component/inventory-linking data model -- see notes section below for the honest scoping rationale.**
- [x] **B5.3 -- Unit conversion layer.** Core conversion table built 2026-07-31 (`unit_conversion_service.py`) and wired into `inventory_service.deduct_by_name` and `meal_plan_service.aggregate_ingredients`/`subtract_inventory` the same day. The two remaining pieces -- the B10.5 recipe-detail-page toggle UI, and density-sourcing for the volume<->mass ("weight mode") case -- were completed together with B10.5 on 2026-08-01 (see that item's notes section). Already documented as a known simplification in the Phase 5 notes: "2 cup flour" and "1 lb flour" stay separate grocery-list lines and inventory deduction is name-match-only when no density is available. Public recipe-scaling APIs exist that return smart culinary fractions and unit promotion, but a local density/unit table was the right call for an offline-first app.
- [x] **B5.4 -- Grocery list grouped by store aisle.** `GroceryListItem.category` was added in Phase 5 explicitly "for a future group-by-aisle view" and is still unused. Tandoor implements supermarket aisle mapping with per-store section ordering. Low effort, high daily-use payoff. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B5.5 -- Pantry-staple exclusion from the grocery list.** Aggregation currently only omits a quantity-less ingredient ("salt to taste") when something matching exists in inventory. A user-maintained "always on hand" staple list would stop obvious noise (salt, pepper, oil) from appearing every week. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**

### B6 -- Cost and budget

- [x] **B6.1 -- Cost per recipe and cost per serving.** Tandoor does meal-cost calculation; the notable 2026 critique of AI recipe apps is that they are price-blind. Even a manually-entered or receipt-derived (B4.2) price-per-unit on inventory items would let Chef show cost per serving and a projected weekly grocery spend -- a metric households actually make decisions on. **Partially unblocked 2026-08-01**: `InventoryItem.unit_price` now exists and is populated by the B10.3 order-history importer (and editable manually) -- the price DATA this item needs is now capturable; the actual cost-per-recipe/weekly-spend computation and display is still not built. **Fully completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**
- [ ] **B6.2 -- Live retailer pricing (investigate only, do not commit).** Kroger publishes a public developer API but only for its own stores; Walmart publishes none; Instacart pricing is available through third-party/scraper services. Scraping is out of scope for a self-hosted app shipped to strangers. Recommendation: treat B6.1 (user/receipt-sourced pricing) as the real deliverable and keep retailer APIs as an optional per-user configured integration at most.

### B7 -- Cooking-time UX

- [x] **B7.1 -- Cook mode.** A full-screen, large-type, step-at-a-time view with the Screen Wake Lock API keeping the display on, step check-off, and inline unit/temperature conversion. This is table stakes in the category and Chef has none of it -- recipes currently render as a static page. Requires a user-initiated toggle (wake lock requires user activation) and should warn about battery impact. **Completed 2026-08-02 -- see "B7.1/B7.2/B7.3 notes" section below.**
- [x] **B7.2 -- Step-linked timers.** Parse durations out of instruction steps and offer a tap-to-start timer per step, running in the same background-persistent way the chat widget already does. **Completed 2026-08-02, same session as B7.1 -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B7.3 -- PWA packaging.** Manifest plus service worker so the app installs to a phone home screen and recipe/plan views work when the kitchen has bad wifi. The frontend is a Vite SPA with no manifest, no service worker, and no offline story today; this is also what makes B7.1/B7.2 usable on the device people actually cook with. **Completed 2026-08-02 -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B7.4 -- Accessibility pass (WCAG 2.1 AA).** Grep of `frontend/src` found essentially no ARIA attributes. Contrast check against the `theme.css` tokens, keyboard navigability, focus states, form labels, text alternatives on icons, and screen-reader labeling on the chat widget and action cards. Also relevant to B7.1, where large type and high contrast are the point. **Completed 2026-08-02 -- see "B7.4 notes" section below.**

### B8 -- Health data integration

- [x] **B8.1 -- Bloodwork import instead of hand entry.** Metrics are typed in one field at a time. A CSV/PDF lab-panel import (the app already extracts text from PDFs for recipes and knowledge files) would make trend tracking realistic rather than aspirational. **Completed 2026-08-02, extended beyond the backlog text's own CSV/PDF scope to also accept a photo of a printed report and pasted text -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B8.2 -- Wearable/health-platform import (opt-in, file-based first).** Apple Health, Google Health Connect, and Withings all export; aggregator APIs (Terra and similar) cover 500+ devices but are paid and cloud-dependent, which fits this project's local-first stance badly. Start with an Apple Health / Health Connect export-file importer for weight and activity, and treat live API sync as a later optional integration. **Completed 2026-08-02 -- Apple Health export.xml/.zip parsed deterministically; Health Connect has no verified stable export format (researched live, see notes section), so routed through the same AI free-text extraction pipeline B8.1's bloodwork import already established instead of guessing at one.**
- [ ] **B8.3 -- Glucose data as an optional signal.** CGM access went mainstream in 2026 -- Withings now ingests Abbott Lingo data via Apple Health / Health Connect and a direct API. Worth designing the health schema so a dense time-series metric is representable, even if nothing consumes it yet; the current `HealthMetricEntry` shape is built for occasional point readings.

### B10 -- Author-requested additions (added 2026-07-31, direct from the author)

These came from the author directly after reviewing the research above, not from the competitive scan. Treat them as carrying higher priority weight than a same-effort item elsewhere in this backlog -- they are stated wants, not inferred ones. Cross-references to related B1-B9 items are noted where implementing one should reuse or unblock the other.

- [x] **B10.1 -- Dining-out finder: restaurants near me that fit the diet.** The app currently assumes every meal is cooked at home; the author wants to eat out sometimes and still stay inside dietary requirements. Needs: a location input (manual entry and/or browser geolocation, with manual always available since geolocation needs HTTPS and this app is commonly served over plain HTTP on a LAN -- see B9.4), a **user-configurable search radius**, and results filtered against the household's dietary restrictions. Should also be able to slot a "eating out" entry into a meal plan so the week stays coherent and the grocery list does not over-buy for a meal that will not be cooked (this touches `MealPlanEntry` -- likely a new entry type alongside recipe-backed entries, similar to how `is_skipped` already exists). **Completed 2026-08-01, the last remaining B10 item -- see notes section below.**

  **Data source guidance, in recommended order.** There is no clean free API for celiac-grade restaurant safety data. Find Me Gluten Free is the category leader but publishes no public API (the only integrations that exist are scrapers, which are not an acceptable dependency for a repo other people clone). Picknic covers 58,000+ locations with allergen/diet attributes and is worth checking for partner API access. Recommended primary: **OpenStreetMap via the Overpass API** -- free, no key, no rate-limit contract to breach, and OSM has an established `diet:*` tagging namespace (`diet:gluten_free=yes|only|no`, plus `diet:vegan`, `diet:vegetarian`, and others) that maps directly onto Chef's restriction model. *Verify the exact tag values against the current OSM wiki before building against them.* Coverage is uneven and skews urban, so treat a missing tag as unknown, never as safe. Secondary enrichment: **Tavily**, which is already wired into this app and already has a key -- a targeted search on a candidate restaurant's name plus "gluten free celiac" is a reasonable way to surface menu pages and recent reviews the model can summarize. Optional, user-configured only: Google Places or Yelp Fusion keys entered through the existing Settings GUI, never bundled or required.

  **Safety framing matters here more than anywhere else in the app.** A restaurant recommendation for a celiac user carries real consequences and the underlying data is crowd-sourced and stale by nature. Present results as candidates to verify, surface the data's provenance and age, and never let the LLM assert a restaurant is safe -- it should summarize what the sources say and say plainly when it does not know. Related: B3.1/B3.2 (the structured restriction model and observance level this should filter against).

- [x] **B10.2 -- Notifications and authentication, mirroring the Fiduciary project.** **Completed 2026-08-01, scoped down after confirming with the author -- see notes section below.** Two of this backlog's gaps -- B4.4 (expiration notifications) and B9.4 (no authentication at all) -- already have solved, author-approved implementations in the sibling Fiduciary project, and the author has asked to mirror them rather than invent new ones. This is the same pattern that already worked twice on this project: `secrets_crypto.py` was ported from Fiduciary in Phase 1/2, and the knowledge/RAG layer was ported from it on 2026-07-31.

  **Fiduciary is not connected by default.** It lives at `C:\Users\JBentley\Claude\Projects\Fiduciary` and must be requested as a connected folder before it can be read. Read the actual implementations before designing anything -- do not assume the shape from this note. Port with the same discipline used for the RAG upgrade: adapt where Chef's domain genuinely differs, port verbatim where the logic is domain-neutral, and write down in the module docstring what was deliberately *not* ported and why. Supersedes/absorbs B4.4 and B9.4 -- when implementing, fold those items in rather than building two notification paths or two auth paths.

- [x] **B10.3 -- Walmart integration and order-history import.** The author does most grocery shopping through the Walmart mobile app, so this is the highest-value retailer integration for this household specifically. Research finding, verified this session: **Walmart publishes no public consumer API for personal purchase history.** The developer APIs at developer.walmart.com (Order Management, On Request Reports, DSV) are seller/supplier-side and require a Marketplace account -- they are the wrong surface entirely. Walmart's own site has no built-in export button either; the working consumer paths today are third-party browser extensions that export an order page to XLSX/CSV, or a manual data request. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**

  **Recommended build, which deliberately does not depend on any of that.** Build a **generic order-history / receipt CSV+XLSX importer** with a per-retailer column-mapping profile, and ship a Walmart profile as the first one. The user brings a file from whatever export path works for them today; Chef parses it into inventory with quantities, prices (which unblocks B6.1 cost tracking for free), and purchase dates (which feed the FoodKeeper shelf-life estimation in B4.3). This is format-agnostic, survives any single extension or export path breaking, and generalizes to Kroger/Costco/Instacart users who clone the repo. Pair it with B4.2's receipt-image OCR as the fallback for in-store paper receipts -- both paths should land in the same review-then-confirm intake screen the vision flow already established, not two separate UIs.

  **Do not build scraping into the app.** Automating a logged-in Walmart session, storing Walmart credentials, or shipping a scraper are all out of scope for something distributed to strangers -- and Chef's own convention (established in the GitHub notes above) is that Claude never handles the user's credentials. If a Walmart product-lookup/affiliate API turns out to be available and openly registerable, it is worth a look as an optional user-configured key for price and product metadata, but verify its current status first rather than trusting any secondhand description of it, including this one.

- [x] **B10.4 -- Responsive UI: real mobile layout alongside the desktop one.** First pass done 2026-07-31 -- see notes section below. Covers the surfaces the backlog text named explicitly (nav, forms, meal-plan grid, chat widget, inventory list) via one centralized breakpoint in `theme.css`, mobile-first per the project brief's one-source-CSS mandate, no component library or second stylesheet added. Not yet done: real-device/real-browser verification (nothing here was checked visually, only built and traced through the DOM/CSS output -- see the notes' verification caveat), and the still-open B7.1-B7.3 cluster (cook mode/timers/PWA) this item was meant to be a prerequisite for -- see the original backlog rationale immediately below, still accurate for what's left.

  Original rationale: the frontend is a desktop-assumption SPA today, and the phone is the device that is actually in the kitchen, at the store, and in front of the fridge, which makes this a prerequisite for several other items rather than a cosmetic pass. Closely related and best done together: B7.1 (cook mode), B7.2 (timers), B7.3 (PWA installability, which is what makes the mobile UI reachable from a home screen), and B7.4 (accessibility, where touch-target sizing and contrast overlap directly with mobile).

- [x] **B10.5 -- Unit system toggle on a viewed recipe: Imperial / Metric / Weight.** A per-view toggle (not a stored per-recipe property, and not a destructive conversion) that re-renders ingredient quantities in US customary, metric volume, or **weight** -- the third mode being the one serious cooks and most gluten-free baking actually wants, since GF flour blends are notoriously unreliable by volume. Should persist as a user preference and sit alongside the existing servings-scaling control, which already re-renders quantities, so there is an established pattern to follow. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**

  **This is the same underlying capability as B5.3 (unit conversion) and should be built once.** B5.3 is currently framed around grocery-list aggregation and inventory deduction ("2 cup flour" vs "1 lb flour" staying separate lines); B10.5 is the user-facing surface of the same conversion table. Build the conversion layer as a shared service and expose it to both. Volume-to-weight specifically requires per-ingredient density, which a generic unit table cannot supply -- but B1.1's food-database resolution layer (USDA FoodData Central publishes portion/gram-weight data per food) can, which makes B1.1 a natural prerequisite for the weight mode. Where density is unknown, show the toggle as unavailable for that ingredient rather than guessing a conversion, and never let the LLM invent one.

- [x] **B10.6 -- Print a recipe.** Done 2026-07-31 -- see notes section below. Implemented as a print stylesheet within the existing centralized `theme.css` (`@media print`), a `.no-print` utility class, and a "Print recipe" button, per the original scoping below.

  Original scoping: implement as a print stylesheet rather than a separate print view or a PDF generation dependency -- consistent with the one-source-CSS mandate and far less to maintain. Should hide app chrome (nav, chat widget, buttons), lay out ingredients and steps for paper, respect the currently-selected servings scale and unit system (B10.5 -- not yet built, so nothing to respect there yet) so what prints matches what was on screen, and include the source citation the recipe already carries. A "print" affordance on the recipe detail page plus browser print-to-PDF covers the export case too, which partially serves B9.2 without building anything extra.

### B11 -- Background job queue and page/navigation resilience (author-requested 2026-08-01)

- [x] **B11.1 -- Unify every AI-consuming endpoint behind one shared, visible, serial background job queue.** The author reported three related problems after uploading a PDF for inventory parsing: no visual busy indicator beyond a button label change, the Inventory page going blank (losing all indication of an in-progress parse) after switching tabs and back, and a direct question about whether Chef has the same failure mode the sibling Fiduciary project had, where starting one AI task while another was in flight would silently abort the earlier one. Root cause, found by inspecting each route's actual `def`/`async def` declaration rather than assuming: several `async def` FastAPI handlers (`inventory.vision_intake`/`import_inventory`, `recipes.import_recipe`, `chat.send_message`, `meal_plan.generate_meal_plan`, plus the knowledge-file indexing loop) called blocking synchronous I/O directly -- Ollama's `Client.chat()`/`describe_image()`/`embed()`, and (found only while converting `import_recipe`, not part of the original hypothesis) `trafilatura.fetch_url()` and a synchronous `httpx.Client` for URL/image fetching -- which freezes an `async def` handler's single-process asyncio event loop for the ENTIRE app, not just that one request, for the full call duration. This is a different, more severe bug than Fiduciary's client-side request-cancellation problem, though it produces a similar-looking symptom. Asked the author how far to take the fix (ingestion-only, everything unified, or something in between); answer was explicit: "comprehensively address this issue now before the project gets bigger." **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**

- [x] **B12.1 -- Google Calendar push sync: a dedicated, auto-created "Chef Meal Plan" calendar kept live in sync with the meal plan.** The author's own framing: B9.5's `.ics` export is a fine no-setup option, but a live-syncing, shareable calendar is "the least amount of user effort for Chef's benefits to be applied." Before building anything, reviewed the author's existing, working Google/iCloud calendar integration in a sibling "Local AI" Open WebUI project (`workspace-mcp` + `mcpo`, streamable-HTTP) as prior art, per the author's own suggestion -- and deliberately did NOT reuse it directly: that stack solves "let an LLM tool-call Calendar operations," a different problem than Chef's deterministic sync-on-change, and depending on it would tie Chef's distributable, `docker compose up`-able repo to a separate stack most people cloning Chef will never have running. Scoped to Google only for this pass (author confirmed via AskUserQuestion); iCloud (CalDAV + an app-specific password, no OAuth) is a natural, simpler follow-up using the same one-way-push architecture. **Completed 2026-08-01 -- see notes section below.**
- [x] **B12.2 -- iCloud Calendar push sync.** Same one-way-push architecture as B12.1, targeting CalDAV against `caldav.icloud.com` with an Apple app-specific password (no OAuth flow needed, so actually less setup complexity than B12.1 once the shared `sync_entry`/event-id-tracking pattern already exists). Deferred at the author's own explicit choice ("Google only first") on 2026-08-01; picked up 2026-08-02 as one of two "no preference which" next roadmap items. **Completed 2026-08-02 -- see notes section below.**

### B16 -- AI parsing reliability and prompt customization (author-requested 2026-08-03)

- [x] **B16.1 -- Fix the recipe-import parsing regression, and expose every AI import/extraction prompt as a GUI-editable advanced setting.** The author reported recipe PDF import going from "parses wrong" to "totally broken" ("Could not extract a recipe from that input" on a plain, well-formed two-page recipe PDF), and asked for two things: (A) fix it, framed as likely needing the same kind of prompt redesign the receipt importer got on 2026-08-02, and (B) stop needing a code change/rebuild every time local AI output doesn't parse -- expose the import prompts as editable settings. **Completed 2026-08-03 -- see notes section below.** The real root cause turned out to be neither a prompt-wording problem nor recipe-specific: `recipe_service._extract_json_object` -- the shared JSON-extraction function EVERY object-shaped AI response in this app funnels through (recipe import, recipe-chat edits, health/bloodwork parsing, meal-plan generation's `new_recipe` proposals) -- had never received the same defensive rewrite `inventory_service._extract_json_array` got on 2026-08-02 for the array-shaped receipt/vision responses. It was still a naive greedy `re.search(r"\{.*\}")` with no defense against a `<think>` reasoning trace landing inline in the response, which the household's current chat model (`qwen3.6:27b`, thinking-capable) does routinely despite `think=False` being requested on every call.

## GitHub repository

https://github.com/txAlphaMale/ai-chef-and-nutritionist (public; renamed from the initial `chef` slug on 2026-07-30 at the author's request — GitHub repo names can't contain spaces/`&`, so "AI Chef & Nutritionist" became this slug, with the full name kept in the README title and repo description)

The author has a "GitHub Integration" connector shown as Connected in Settings, but it did not surface any tools in the session where the repo was created (2026-07-30) — connectors load at session start, so it likely needs a fresh conversation to pick up. Until confirmed working, assume no GitHub connector is available: Claude's sandbox has no route to push authenticated git operations (no `gh` CLI available — not installable, no root — and Claude does not handle GitHub tokens/passwords on the user's behalf under any circumstances). Claude created/renamed the repo via browser automation (Chrome, already-authenticated session, no credentials entered) and configured the local `origin` remote + `main` branch. **The author must run the actual `git push` locally** — local git/credential manager handles auth, Claude never sees a token. Command, run from the `Chef` folder:
```
git push -u origin main
```
After that succeeds, future commits just need `git push`.

## Phase 1 schema notes

15 tables, defined under `backend/app/models/` (one file per domain area) and imported through `app/models/__init__.py` so Alembic autogenerate and relationship string-lookups both see the full set:

- **inventory.py** — `InventoryItem` (category, quantity/unit, location, purchased/expiration/last-used dates, `is_priority` flag + note for "use this up" boosting, source of entry: manual/vision/chat).
- **kitchen.py** — `KitchenProfile` (name, JSON equipment list, `is_active`) for home vs. camping/RV/rental setups.
- **recipe.py** — `Recipe` (default_servings for scaling, JSON instructions list, JSON nutrition dict, rating, `is_staple`, source), `RecipeIngredient`, `MealTag` + `recipe_tag_links` join table (seeded tags: quick, portable, non_refrigerated, dutch_oven_only, backpacking, one_pot, make_ahead, freezer_friendly, kid_friendly, gluten_free).
- **meal_plan.py** — `MealPlan`, `MealPlanEntry` (day/meal_type/recipe/servings, `is_confirmed` is the hook that should trigger inventory deduction in Phase 3, `is_skipped` for chat-driven skip handling), `GroceryListItem`.
- **household.py** — `HouseholdPreferences` (singleton row, household_size default 2, dietary_restrictions JSON, goals, indulgence_frequency), `HouseholdMember` (age/height/sex/activity_level for BMI-aware planning in Phase 6).
- **health.py** — `HealthMetricEntry` (weight, BMI, LDL/HDL/total cholesterol, triglycerides, blood pressure, glucose) tied to a household member, for Phase 6 trend charts.
- **chat.py** — `ChatMessage` (session_id, role, content, timestamp) backing the persistent/background chat in Phase 7.
- **settings.py** — `AppSetting` (key/value/is_secret, for the Settings GUI in Phase 8), `SystemPrompt` (prompt_key: `main_chef` / `dietary_onboarding`, editable content — Phase 2 wires these into the Ollama client), `KnowledgeFile` (imported nutritionist grounding docs, Phase 6).

**Seed data is deliberately generic**, not the author's personal profile — `HouseholdPreferences` seeds with household_size=2 and no dietary restrictions pre-filled, since this repo is meant for other households to pull down and configure for themselves via onboarding/Settings. The `main_chef` and `dietary_onboarding` system prompts are seeded with real content per the project brief (nutritionist-chef persona, confirm-before-write, expiring/priority-ingredient bias, occasional indulgence).

`backend/docker-entrypoint.sh` runs `alembic upgrade head` then `python -m app.seed` (both idempotent) before starting uvicorn, so the DB bootstraps itself on first `docker compose up` and stays current on every restart without manual steps.

## Settings & secrets storage

Ported from `Fiduciary/portfolio-api/secrets_crypto.py` (a validated, tested module in the author's sibling project, read directly on 2026-07-30 — not reinvented from scratch) into `backend/app/services/secrets_crypto.py`:

- Fernet (AES-128-CBC + HMAC, via the `cryptography` package) symmetric encryption for any `AppSetting` row where `is_secret=True` (currently just `tavily_api_key`).
- Key file at `/app/data/secrets.key` (same Docker volume as the SQLite DB), generated on first run, written atomically with 0600 permissions. **Losing this file makes every encrypted value permanently unrecoverable** — back it up alongside `./data`.
- Versioned envelope encryption from day one so key rotation is possible later without a bulk re-encrypt: unrotated deployments produce unprefixed ciphertext (the common case); `rotate_key()` adds a new key version to `/app/data/secrets_keyring.json`, all new encrypts use it, old ciphertext keeps decrypting under its original key forever.
- `decrypt_or_legacy()` never raises — falls back to returning the raw value if it isn't a valid token, for defensive handling of pre-encryption data.

`backend/app/services/settings_service.py` wraps this with a `SETTING_SPECS` registry (key, label, `is_secret`, default, description, optional `.env` fallback name) and `get_setting()`/`set_setting()`/`list_settings_for_display()` (secrets masked as `********` for API responses). `app/seed.py` uses this to seed each setting once — from an optional `.env` value if present, otherwise the spec default — and never overwrites a value that already exists, whether set via `.env` or later via the Settings UI (Phase 8).

`app/config.py` (pydantic `Settings`) now holds ONLY true infra bootstrap values (`database_url`, `backend_port`) that must exist before the DB is reachable. Everything else moved out of it: Ollama base URL/models and the Tavily key are DB-backed via `settings_service`; household size lives in `HouseholdPreferences` (already the case since Phase 1).

`app/services/ollama_client.py` and `app/services/tavily_client.py` are thin wrappers that pull their config from `settings_service` per-call (not cached at import time), so a Settings UI edit takes effect on the next request without a restart.

**Known sandbox-only quirk (not a bug):** importing the `ollama` package fails inside Claude's sandbox because ambient `ALL_PROXY=socks5h://...` env vars (from the sandbox's own network setup) break `ollama`'s eager default-client construction at import time. Confirmed this doesn't happen with those proxy vars unset, and the real Docker deployment won't have them set. Local verification of `ollama_client.py`/`tavily_client.py` therefore ran with `env -u ALL_PROXY -u ...`; no code changes were needed to work around it.

## Phase 4 notes: recipes

Backend (`backend/app/routers/recipes.py`, `app/services/recipe_service.py`, `app/schemas/recipe.py`):

- Full CRUD: `GET/POST /api/recipes`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/recipes/{id}`, with `is_staple`/`tag`/`search` filters. `POST /{id}/rating` validates 1–5 (422 outside that range).
- **Servings scaling**: `GET /api/recipes/{id}?servings=N` returns ingredient quantities scaled by `N / default_servings` (`scale_ingredients`) — nutrition is stored per-serving already (see Phase 1 schema notes) so it's returned as-is, not multiplied. `None`-quantity ingredients (e.g. "salt to taste") pass through unscaled rather than erroring.
- **Tags**: get-or-create by name (`resolve_tags`) — reusing an existing tag name (e.g. one of the 10 seeded in Phase 1) does not create a duplicate `MealTag` row, verified directly against the DB.
- **AI-assisted import** (`POST /api/recipes/import`, multipart): accepts a `text` form field OR an uploaded file, dispatching by content type — images go to the Ollama vision model, PDFs are text-extracted via `pypdf` then sent to the chat model, plain text files are decoded and sent to the chat model. Returns a *preview* (`RecipeImportResponse`), nothing is saved until the user reviews/edits and the frontend POSTs to the normal create endpoint — same preview-then-confirm pattern as Phase 3's vision intake. `parse_recipe_response` defensively extracts a JSON object from real-world model output (prose/markdown-wrapped or strict), returning `None` (→ 422) only if no usable title is found.
- **Bug caught and fixed during testing**: the `/import` endpoint mixes an `UploadFile` with a plain `text` parameter, which requires `text: str | None = Form(None)` — without explicit `Form()`, FastAPI silently treats `text` as a query parameter and never reads it from multipart form data. Caught by an actual failing curl test (400 "provide text or file" despite sending text), not by inspection.

Frontend (`frontend/src/pages/RecipesPage.jsx`, `RecipeDetailPage.jsx`, `components/RecipeForm.jsx`): list with search/staple filters, a shared add/edit form (dynamic ingredient and instruction rows, comma-separated tags, optional per-serving nutrition), an import panel (paste text or upload photo/PDF, review the parsed preview before saving), and a detail page with a live servings input that re-fetches scaled quantities, a rating dropdown, and a staple toggle. Routing extended with `/recipes` and `/recipes/:id`.

**Verification:** unit tests for `parse_recipe_response` (strict JSON, markdown-wrapped, garbage, missing-title) and `scale_ingredients` (up-scale, down-scale, no-op, `None` quantity untouched) run directly; full CRUD, rating validation, staple/tag filters, and tag deduplication exercised via curl against a live backend; the `Form()` bug found and fixed via a live request, then re-verified; frontend build succeeds; ran the real Vite dev server against the real backend and created/scaled a recipe through the actual proxy path.

## Phase 4.1 notes: recipe chat, citations, URL import, tips

Added after Phase 4 shipped, in response to a specific follow-up request: an ephemeral chat for substitution questions while cooking, source citation capture for copyright respect, URL import, and a way to retain genuinely useful asides (substitutions/variations) from imported sources while discarding ads/stories/boilerplate.

**Schema** (migration `957de2f3c51d`): `Recipe` gained `source_url` (String 1000, nullable), `source_name` (String 200, nullable), `source_author` (String 200, nullable), and `tips` (JSON list, default empty). `RecipeBase`/`RecipeUpdate` schemas extended to match; `RecipeIngredientRead.id` changed to `int | None` since scaled (non-persisted) ingredient views don't have a real row id.

**URL import**: `recipe_service.extract_url_content(url)` splits into `fetch_html()` (network call via `trafilatura.fetch_url`) and `extract_content_from_html()` (pure function over HTML using `trafilatura.bare_extraction`, `favor_recall=True`) so the extraction logic is unit-testable without a live network call. `trafilatura` does the mechanical boilerplate removal (ads/nav) and captures byline/sitename metadata in the same pass — that metadata becomes the citation (`source_url`/`source_name`/`source_author`) rather than being discarded. `POST /api/recipes/import` now accepts `text`, `file`, or `url` (exactly one; `url` is checked first) as multipart form fields.

**Copyright-respecting parsing**: `RECIPE_IMPORT_PROMPT` explicitly instructs the model to extract only factual/functional recipe data (what to buy, what to do, timing) and NOT reproduce narrative prose, personal stories, ads, or other copyrightable writing — summarizing functionally instead of quoting at length. A new `tips` array in the prompt output captures genuinely useful asides (substitutions, optional variations, storage/make-ahead notes, equipment alternatives) as short paraphrases, omitted entirely if the source has nothing like that. Verified with a synthetic HTML fixture containing a fake ad, nav, personal story, and an embedded substitution tip: ad/nav were stripped by trafilatura, the substitution text survived into extracted text, and title/author/sitename metadata were captured correctly.

**Bug fixed**: `parse_recipe_response` previously hardcoded `"source": "import_text"` in its return dict regardless of the actual import method, mislabeling image/PDF/URL imports. Fixed by removing that key from the service function entirely; the router now sets `parsed["source"]` based on which import branch actually ran (`import_text` / `import_file` / `import_image` / `import_url`).

**Ephemeral recipe-scoped chat** (`POST /api/recipes/{id}/chat`): for in-the-moment questions like "I'm out of buttermilk, what can I use?" while cooking. Deliberately NOT persisted to the `chat_messages` table — that's reserved for the Phase 7 persistent/background chat system, a separate concern. The client (`RecipeChat.jsx`) holds conversation history only in React state and resends it each turn to this stateless endpoint. `recipe_service.build_recipe_chat_context()` builds a context block (title, ingredients at the currently-viewed serving size, numbered instructions, known tips) that's appended to the active `main_chef` system prompt, so suggestions are grounded in the actual recipe rather than generic. Widget is collapsed by default on the recipe detail page, Enter-to-send (Shift+Enter for newline).

**Frontend**: `RecipeForm.jsx` gained editable tips list and source fields (auto-filled on import, editable manually); `RecipesPage.jsx` gained a URL input alongside the existing text/photo/PDF import controls, with a hint explaining the ad/story filtering behavior; `RecipeDetailPage.jsx` displays tips and a source citation (linked if a URL is present) below the nutrition block, and mounts `RecipeChat` at the bottom of the page; `theme.css` gained matching styles for the chat widget and citation text.

**Verification**: backend — unit tests for `extract_content_from_html` against the synthetic HTML fixture described above, `build_recipe_chat_context` against a mocked recipe (asserts title/ingredient text present in the built context), and the full import/chat flow via curl against a live local uvicorn instance (mocked `ollama_client.chat`/`describe_image` since no live Ollama reaches the sandbox) covering url/text/file import dispatch and the new `source`/citation labeling; a nested-f-string Python 3.10 SyntaxError in `build_recipe_chat_context` was caught by `py_compile` before runtime testing and fixed by extracting a plain-concatenation helper (`_format_ingredient_line`). Frontend — `npm run build` succeeds cleanly; ran the real Vite dev server against a real backend (fresh SQLite DB, real Alembic migration + seed, mocked Ollama chat response) together and exercised `/health`, recipe create, servings-scaled GET (confirmed tips/citation fields and scaled quantities round-trip correctly), and `POST /{id}/chat` all through the actual browser-facing Vite proxy path — not just each side tested in isolation.

## Phase 5 notes: meal planning engine

Backend (`backend/app/routers/meal_plan.py`, `app/routers/kitchen.py`, `app/services/meal_plan_service.py`, `app/schemas/meal_plan.py`, `app/schemas/kitchen.py`):

**Schema** (migration `67a6d7e5b6e5`): `MealPlanEntry` gained `requested_tags` (JSON list -- the guiding tags a slot was planned against, e.g. `["portable", "non_refrigerated"]` for a picnic) and `is_indulgence` (marks the week's occasional treat meal, separate from `HouseholdPreferences.indulgence_frequency` which just sets the target cadence). `GroceryListItem` gained `category` (nullable, mirrors `InventoryItem` categories, for a future group-by-aisle view). `MealPlan`/`MealPlanEntry`/`GroceryListItem` tables themselves already existed from the Phase 1 schema but had never been wired to an API until now.

**Kitchen profiles** (`routers/kitchen.py`, new): simple CRUD the project brief calls for but no earlier phase built -- `GET/POST /api/kitchen-profiles`, `PATCH/DELETE /api/kitchen-profiles/{id}`. Only one profile is active at a time; setting one active clears the flag on the others (same singleton-ish pattern as `HouseholdPreferences`). This is what lets the meal planner adapt to a home kitchen vs. camping/RV/rental with limited equipment.

**Generation** (`POST /api/meal-plans/generate`): same preview-then-confirm pattern as recipe import -- returns a `MealPlanCreate`-shaped preview, nothing persisted until the user reviews/edits and `POST`s to `/api/meal-plans`. `meal_plan_service.gather_generation_context()` pulls together everything the model needs to ground a sensible plan: household size/dietary restrictions/goals/indulgence frequency, the active (or requested) kitchen profile's equipment list, `inventory_service.get_priority_suggestions()` (the same urgency-scoring function built in Phase 3, reused directly rather than reimplemented) for what to prioritize using up, and a compact recipe catalog (staples and highly-rated first) so the model can reuse an existing recipe instead of inventing one for every slot. The prompt instructs the model to strongly prefer an existing catalog `recipe_id` and only propose a `new_recipe` (same shape as recipe import, via the now-shared `recipe_service.coerce_recipe_fields()`) when nothing fits. Per-slot guidance (e.g. "Saturday lunch needs to be portable for a picnic") and free-text notes (e.g. "going camping this weekend") can steer generation.

**Defensive parsing carries a validation step recipe import didn't need**: `parse_meal_plan_response()` coerces fields the same defensive way as recipe import (strict JSON first, then extract the first `{...}` block), but a meal plan additionally references *existing* recipe ids the model was shown -- and models sometimes hallucinate one that wasn't in the catalog. `validate_entries_against_catalog()` nulls out any `recipe_id` not actually present in the catalog that was sent, rather than trusting it; the slot is then left recipe-less for the user to fix in the review step, same as any other AI-preview-then-confirm flow here. Verified with a live curl test using a mocked Ollama response that deliberately includes a fabricated id (999) alongside two valid slots (one reusing a catalog recipe, one proposing a `new_recipe`) -- confirmed the hallucinated id came back `null` while the other two passed through correctly.

**Grocery list derivation**: split the same way as recipe import's URL extraction -- a pure aggregation/subtraction pair (`aggregate_ingredients`, `subtract_inventory`) that's unit-testable without a DB, plus a thin `compute_grocery_list()` wrapper that loads a persisted plan's entries/inventory. Ingredients are scaled to each entry's servings (reusing `recipe_service.scale_ingredients`), summed across all non-skipped entries keyed by `(name, unit)`, then matched against current inventory the same case-insensitive exact-then-substring way as `inventory_service.deduct_by_name` and subtracted; only the remaining (still-needed) quantity is listed. An ingredient with no stated quantity (e.g. "salt to taste") is only listed if nothing matching exists in inventory at all, rather than always appearing. Recomputation happens automatically on plan creation, on any entry edit/skip (`_persist_grocery_list()` replaces `source="auto"` rows only, leaving manually-added items alone), and on-demand via `POST /{plan_id}/grocery-list/regenerate` (e.g. after a shopping trip). Verified: confirming a meal deducts its scaled ingredients from inventory (reusing `inventory_service.deduct_by_name` once per ingredient) and a subsequent grocery-list regeneration correctly reflects the now-lower on-hand quantity -- i.e. the grocery list always answers "what do I still need to buy for everything not yet skipped," not a frozen snapshot from generation time.

**New-recipe creation on confirm**: `recipe_service.create_recipe_from_parsed()` (new, shared) creates a `Recipe` + ingredients + tags from a `coerce_recipe_fields()`-shaped dict without duplicating `routers/recipes.py`'s creation wiring wholesale -- used when `POST /api/meal-plans` persists an entry whose slot had no catalog match, tagging the new recipe `source="ai_generated"`. Verified live: a generated plan's `new_recipe` entry actually appears in `GET /api/recipes` afterward with the correct source label and is immediately available for future weeks' catalogs.

**Confirm/skip**: `POST /{plan_id}/entries/{entry_id}/confirm` marks a meal as made and deducts its scaled ingredients from inventory (best-effort -- an ingredient with no inventory match is silently skipped rather than failing the whole confirmation); `/skip` marks it skipped with no deduction. Both are blocked once an entry is already confirmed (400), and skip is blocked on an already-confirmed entry, verified via live curl including the double-confirm 400 case. Deleting a plan cascades to its entries (existing `cascade="all, delete-orphan"` relationship from Phase 1), verified live.

Frontend (`frontend/src/pages/MealPlanPage.jsx`, `components/MealPlanEntryRow.jsx`, `components/GroceryListPanel.jsx`): a plan picker (dropdown over existing plans by week), a generate form (week start date, household size override, kitchen profile select, meal-type checkboxes, free-text notes, and a collapsible per-day tags/notes guidance section), an editable review step after generation (swap any slot between the AI's proposed new recipe and any catalog recipe, adjust servings/tags/indulgence flag, nothing saved until confirmed), a weekly grid for the persisted plan (recipe swap, servings, "we made this"/skip actions, linking through to the recipe detail page), and a self-contained grocery list panel (checkbox to mark purchased, manual add, delete, recompute-from-inventory). Nav/routing extended with `/meal-plan`. `theme.css` gained matching styles for the grid, preview rows, and grocery list, following the existing "single source of truth" CSS-variable convention.

**Verification**: unit tests for the pure functions (`parse_meal_plan_response` against strict/markdown-wrapped/garbage/invalid-day input, `validate_entries_against_catalog`, `aggregate_ingredients`, `subtract_inventory`, `build_generation_prompt`) run directly against the service module; a comprehensive live curl sequence against a fresh local backend (real Alembic migration + seed, mocked `ollama_client.chat` returning a canned plan with a catalog-reuse slot, a new-recipe slot, and a deliberately hallucinated recipe id) covering generation, plan creation (including new-recipe auto-creation), grocery-list aggregation/subtraction math (hand-verified the exact expected quantities at each step), confirm-triggered inventory deduction, skip, manual grocery items, regeneration, entry PATCH re-syncing the grocery list, 404s, and cascade delete; kitchen-profile CRUD and active-exclusivity verified live; frontend `npm run build` succeeds cleanly; a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass exercised health, kitchen-profiles, recipe creation, plan generation, plan creation, grocery-list retrieval, and entry confirmation all through the actual browser-facing proxy path.

**Known simplification, documented for a future pass**: ingredient aggregation/subtraction matches by name (case-insensitive exact-then-substring, same approach used throughout this app since Phase 3) and does not attempt unit conversion -- "2 cup flour" and "1 lb flour" stay as separate grocery-list lines rather than being reconciled. A real unit-conversion layer is a reasonable future improvement once real-world usage surfaces how much it actually matters.

## Phase 6 notes: health & nutrition tracking

Backend (`backend/app/routers/household.py`, `app/routers/health.py`, `app/routers/knowledge.py`, `app/services/health_service.py`, `app/services/knowledge_service.py`, `app/schemas/household.py`, `app/schemas/health.py`, `app/schemas/knowledge.py`):

**Schema** (migration `a4a23dff4e5e`): `KnowledgeFile` gained `content` (Text, nullable -- extracted plain text, populated once at upload rather than re-parsed on every request) and `is_active` (Boolean, default true -- only active files ground generation). `HouseholdPreferences`, `HouseholdMember`, and `HealthMetricEntry` already existed from the Phase 1 schema but had no API until now.

**Household preferences & members** (`routers/household.py`, new): `GET/PATCH /api/household/preferences` for the singleton row (household size, dietary restrictions, goals, indulgence frequency), `GET/POST /api/household/members` + `PATCH/DELETE /api/household/members/{id}` for per-person profiles (age, height, sex, activity level) that both BMI calculation and meal-plan generation draw on.

**Health metrics & BMI** (`routers/health.py`, new): `GET/POST /api/health/metrics` (optionally filtered by member) and `PATCH/DELETE /api/health/metrics/{id}`. BMI is deliberately never accepted from the client -- `_compute_bmi_for_entry()` always computes it server-side from the submitted `weight_kg` and the associated member's `height_cm` (`health_service.compute_bmi`), so it can't drift out of sync if the member's height is corrected later, and is recomputed on any `PATCH` that changes `weight_kg` or `household_member_id`. Weight is stored canonically in kg (matches the existing model); the frontend (`HealthPage.jsx`) converts to/from pounds for display and entry, since a US household is the primary near-term user, without baking imperial units into the schema.

**Trend computation** (`GET /api/health/trends?household_member_id=`): `health_service.compute_metric_trend()` compares the most recent data point for a field against the oldest point still within a rolling window (default 90 days), falling back to the earliest point overall if none fall inside the window -- e.g. "down 3.5kg over the last 90 days" rather than a naive first-vs-last-ever comparison that could span years. Returns `null` for a field with fewer than 2 data points rather than a misleading zero-delta. Covers weight, BMI, LDL, HDL, and total cholesterol in one response.

**BMI/health-aware meal planning**: `health_service.build_health_context_summary()` builds a compact per-member line (e.g. "Jason: weight 94.5kg, BMI 29.2 (overweight), LDL 128.0 mg/dL ... weight down 3.5kg over the last 90 days") from the same trend function, and `meal_plan_service.gather_generation_context()` (Phase 5) now includes it as `health_summary`, injected into the generation prompt via a new section instructing the model to let it quietly bias toward lower-cholesterol, appropriately-portioned choices without mentioning the numbers directly to the user. This directly closes the loop the project brief asked for: body metrics actually steering plan generation, not just being logged and forgotten.

**Nutritionist knowledge files** (`routers/knowledge.py` + `services/knowledge_service.py`, new): `POST /api/knowledge-files` (multipart upload) stores the raw file on disk under a UUID-based name (`knowledge_service.save_file`, same atomic-write pattern as `secrets_crypto.py`'s key file, avoiding filename-collision/traversal issues from user-supplied names) and extracts plain text once at upload time (`knowledge_service.extract_text` -- reuses `recipe_service.extract_pdf_text` for PDFs rather than duplicating pypdf plumbing; `.txt`/`.md` decoded directly; anything else stored but left ungrounded, `has_content: false`, not an upload error). `GET /api/knowledge-files` never returns the full extracted text (`content_excerpt` is a 300-character preview only); `PATCH` toggles `is_active`/edits the description; `DELETE` removes both the DB row and the file from disk.

**Knowledge-file grounding**: `health_service.build_knowledge_context()` concatenates every *active* file's extracted text (truncated to a combined 3000-character budget via `_format_knowledge_excerpts`, each chunk labeled with its filename) and `gather_generation_context()` includes it as `knowledge_context`, injected into the generation prompt as reference material the model should follow where it doesn't conflict with stated dietary restrictions. **Documented simplification**: this is simple concatenation-with-truncation, not retrieval -- fine for a handful of short reference documents (a doctor's guidance sheet, a specific diet plan) but would need real chunking/embedding-based retrieval to scale to a large knowledge base. Noted here for a future pass rather than over-built now.

Frontend (`frontend/src/pages/HealthPage.jsx`, `components/TrendChart.jsx`, `components/KnowledgeFilesPanel.jsx`): household preferences editor, member add/remove list, a per-member metric-logging form (date, weight in lbs, LDL/HDL/total cholesterol, triglycerides, blood pressure, glucose) with a history table (delete per entry), two `TrendChart` sparkline-style panels (weight, LDL) built as plain inline SVG rather than adding a charting library dependency -- the app has stayed dependency-light through five phases and the data volumes here (a handful of log entries) don't warrant one; gracefully shows "not enough data yet" below 2 points instead of erroring. `KnowledgeFilesPanel` (upload/list/activate-toggle/delete) is self-contained like Phase 5's `GroceryListPanel`. Nav/routing extended with `/health`. `theme.css` gained matching styles for member rows, trend charts, and the knowledge file list.

**Verification**: unit tests for the pure functions (`compute_bmi`, `bmi_category`, `compute_metric_trend` against within-window/outside-window/fallback-to-earliest/insufficient-data cases, `format_member_health_line`, `_format_knowledge_excerpts` truncation) run directly against the service modules; a live curl sequence against a fresh local backend covering preferences update, member CRUD, metric logging with server-computed BMI verified against a hand-calculated expected value, the trends endpoint, knowledge-file upload/list/deactivate/delete (including confirming the file is actually removed from disk), and 404s; **directly verified the generation prompt itself contains the injected health and knowledge grounding text** (not just that the context dict was built) by calling `gather_generation_context` + `build_generation_prompt` against the live test DB and asserting the member's name/BMI/trend and the uploaded file's content appear in the resulting prompt string; frontend `npm run build` succeeds cleanly; a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass exercised preferences, member creation, metric logging, and knowledge-file upload/list through the actual browser-facing proxy path. Also fixed, in passing: a confusing name collision in `main.py` where the `/health` endpoint function was named `health`, shadowing the newly-imported `health` router module within the same file (worked by accident due to Python's top-to-bottom execution order, but fragile) -- renamed the endpoint function to `health_check`.

**Known limitation, not fixed in this pass**: deleting a household member does not null out `household_member_id` on their existing `HealthMetricEntry` rows (the FK is nullable but SQLite doesn't enforce it by default in this setup, so the rows are left with a dangling reference rather than erroring or cascading). Harmless today since nothing dereferences a deleted member's id, but worth a real cascade or explicit nulling if member deletion becomes a common operation.

## Phase 7 notes: persistent chat system

Backend (`backend/app/routers/chat.py`, `app/services/chat_service.py`, `app/schemas/chat.py`):

**Schema** (migration `5c07836d9339`): `ChatMessage` (already existed from the Phase 1 schema, unused until now) gained `actions` (JSON, nullable) -- structured action proposals attached to an assistant message, so history replay after a page reload can still show actionable cards, not just the reply text. Always null on user messages.

**Sessions are implicit, not a separate table**: a session is just a distinct `session_id` value already present in `chat_messages`. `GET /api/chat/sessions` derives a summary (message count, last-message time, a truncated preview) with a `GROUP BY`, ordered most-recent-first; `DELETE /api/chat/sessions/{id}` deletes all rows for that id. The frontend generates its own `session_id` (`crypto.randomUUID()`, persisted in `localStorage`) rather than calling a `POST /sessions` endpoint -- a session only "exists" server-side once its first message is sent.

**Context grounding**: `chat_service.build_chat_context()` pulls the currently-relevant meal plan (the `active`-status one if there is one, else the most recently created plan at all, so chat is still useful before a plan is manually activated) and up to 80 inventory items, then `build_chat_system_prompt()` appends a formatted block -- household size/dietary restrictions, meal-plan entries with their real `entry_id`s and status, inventory items with their real `id`s -- to the `main_chef` system prompt. The prompt explicitly instructs the model to never invent an `entry_id`/`meal_plan_id`/ingredient reference not listed, asking a clarifying question instead of guessing. Verified directly (same technique used in Phase 6): called `build_chat_context` + `build_chat_system_prompt` against a live test DB and asserted the actual inventory ids, entry id, household size, and entry status appear in the literal resulting prompt string.

**Actions are proposals only, never executed server-side**: `chat_service.parse_chat_response()` defensively extracts `{"reply", "actions"}` from the model's output (reusing the same `_extract_json_object` helper recipe import and meal-plan generation use), coercing each action against `ALLOWED_ACTION_TYPES` (`inventory_deduct`, `inventory_update`, `inventory_add`, `meal_plan_confirm_entry`, `meal_plan_skip_entry`) and dropping anything malformed. Unlike the structured-extraction tasks in earlier phases, chat is free-form conversation and far more likely to break a "JSON-only" instruction -- if no JSON object with a `reply` key is found at all, the raw text is used as the reply verbatim with an empty actions list, rather than showing the user nothing. `routers/chat.py` persists the proposal alongside the assistant message but does not act on it; the frontend calls the same pre-existing endpoint the rest of the app already uses for that change when the user clicks confirm (`POST /api/inventory/deduct`, `/api/inventory/update-by-name`, `POST /api/inventory`, or the Phase 5 `/api/meal-plans/{id}/entries/{id}/confirm`/`/skip`) -- deliberately avoiding a second, duplicate action-execution layer. This also means a hallucinated id isn't specially validated in chat code at all; it just 404s from the real endpoint like any other bad id would, verified live by proposing a confirm action against a nonexistent `meal_plan_id`/`entry_id` and confirming the real endpoint rejects it.

**New small endpoints, not new machinery**: name-based `POST /api/inventory/deduct` and `POST /api/inventory/update-by-name` were added to `routers/inventory.py` (static paths, correctly ordered before `/{item_id}`) because chat needs to resolve a natural-language ingredient name to a row without knowing its id -- the underlying matching logic (`inventory_service.find_by_name`, extracted from what was inlined in `deduct_by_name`) already existed and is now shared by both `deduct_by_name` and the new `update_by_name`. No new endpoints were needed for meal-plan actions since `/confirm`/`/skip` already existed from Phase 5.
