Frontend (`frontend/src/components/ChatWidget.jsx`): mounted once in `App.jsx` at the app-shell level, outside `<Routes>`, so it stays alive (history, in-flight sends, open/closed state) across route navigation -- the core requirement for "chat keeps running/visible in the background while navigating the app." A floating toggle button docked bottom-right expands into a panel with message history, action cards (each rendering the model's `description` with its own Confirm button that calls the corresponding real endpoint per the mapping above, tracking pending/done/error state per action so a card can't be double-applied), and a text input. History is fetched once on mount from `GET /api/chat/messages?session_id=`, so a full page reload restores the conversation (not just in-memory persistence across navigation). `theme.css` gained a `.chat-widget*` block reusing existing design tokens and the `.chat-msg`/`.card`/`.btn` classes already established by Phase 4.1's ephemeral recipe chat, rather than inventing a parallel style vocabulary.

**Verification**: unit tests for `parse_chat_response` (strict JSON, malformed-fallback-to-raw-text) and `_coerce_action` (all five action types, invalid type, missing required field) run directly against the service module; a live curl sequence against a fresh local backend (real Alembic migration + seed, mocked `ollama_client.chat` returning different canned responses keyed off trigger substrings in the message text) covering a plain reply with no actions, an `inventory_deduct` proposal, an `inventory_update` proposal, a `meal_plan_confirm_entry` proposal against real ids, the malformed-JSON-falls-back-to-raw-text case, a deliberately hallucinated meal-plan id, full history round-trip (including the `actions` JSON field surviving persistence), session listing/multi-session/deletion, and the empty-message 400 case; then confirming each proposed action for real through its actual endpoint (`/api/inventory/deduct`, `/api/inventory/update-by-name`, `/api/meal-plans/{id}/entries/{id}/confirm`) and hand-verifying the resulting inventory/meal-plan state, plus confirming the hallucinated-id action correctly 404s against the real endpoint instead of silently succeeding; directly inspected the generated system prompt text (same technique as Phase 6) to confirm real inventory/meal-plan ids are actually injected, not just present in an intermediate context dict. Frontend: `npm run build` succeeds cleanly; a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass through the actual proxy path exercised `/api/chat/sessions`, `/api/chat/messages` (send + reply), and `/health`, and confirmed `ChatWidget.jsx` transforms without error through Vite's dev pipeline and that `App.jsx` mounts it outside `<Routes>` as intended (checked in the served, unbundled dev source, not just the source file on disk).

## Phase 8 notes: theming, settings GUI, persistence

Centralized CSS theming was already in place as a running convention since Phase 0 (every phase's frontend work has added to the single `frontend/src/styles/theme.css` design-token file rather than inlining styles or introducing a second stylesheet), so Phase 8's theming scope was already satisfied going in -- this phase's new work was the Settings GUI itself and confirming persistence.

**Backend** (`backend/app/routers/system.py`, `app/schemas/system.py`, `app/services/settings_service.py`): Phase 2 had already built read-only `GET /api/system/settings` (secrets masked) and `GET /api/system/prompts`, plus `settings_service.set_setting()` -- Phase 8 just adds the write side the GUI needs: `PATCH /api/system/settings/{key}` (body `{value}`, 404 on an unrecognized key via new `settings_service.is_known_key()`) and `PATCH /api/system/prompts/{prompt_key}` (body `{content?, is_active?}`, 404 if the prompt key doesn't exist). No schema migration needed -- `AppSetting`/`SystemPrompt` have existed since Phase 1.

**Frontend** (`frontend/src/pages/SettingsPage.jsx`, new): three cards -- connection status (Ollama reachable / Tavily configured, from the existing `GET /api/system/status`, with a manual Refresh button since there's no push mechanism), AI services & secrets (one row per `SETTING_SPECS` entry, each with its own Save button so updating the Tavily key doesn't require re-entering the Ollama URL; secret fields render as a blank password input with a "set -- enter a new value to change" placeholder rather than ever showing the masked value in an editable field, and the Save button is disabled while that field is empty so a blank submit can't accidentally wipe a configured secret), and system prompts (a textarea + Active checkbox per prompt, matching what the schema already exposed). Household size and dietary restrictions are deliberately not duplicated here -- they're annotated with a link to the Health page's existing "Household preferences" card (built in Phase 6), since that's where they're tied to member profiles and BMI-aware planning; a second edit surface for the same data would risk drifting out of sync. Nav/routing extended with `/settings`. `theme.css` gained a `.settings-row`/`.settings-status-grid`/`.status-dot` block reusing existing tokens (`--color-secondary` for the "ok" dot, `--color-danger` for "not reachable/configured", matching the palette already used for trend-chart up/down coloring in Phase 6).

**Persistence verification**: no Docker daemon is available in Claude's sandbox (same limitation noted in every prior phase), so this was verified by tracing every write path's default location rather than literally rebuilding a container -- `app/config.py`'s `database_url` (`sqlite:////app/data/chef.db`), `secrets_crypto.py`'s `SECRETS_KEY_FILE`/`SECRETS_KEYRING_FILE`, and `knowledge_service.py`'s `KNOWLEDGE_FILES_DIR` all default under `/app/data`, which `backend/Dockerfile` declares as `VOLUME ["/app/data"]` and `docker-compose.yml` mounts as the named volume `chef-data`. Settings and prompts themselves are ordinary rows in the same SQLite file, so they persist through the identical mechanism -- no separate persistence path to verify for the new GUI.

**Verification**: backend -- a live curl sequence against a fresh local uvicorn instance (real Alembic migration + seed) covering GET/PATCH for both settings and prompts, the unknown-key/unknown-prompt-key 404 cases, and a direct SQLite read confirming the Tavily key is stored Fernet-encrypted (not plaintext) after a PATCH; `py_compile` clean. Frontend -- `npm run build` succeeds cleanly (run from a scratch copy outside the FUSE-mounted project folder after hitting the documented FUSE write-permission issue -- see the operational note above -- trying to clear a stale `dist/` in place; the build itself is unaffected, only where it was run from); a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass through the actual browser-facing proxy path (run from the real project folder, which has a working `node_modules`) exercised `/health`, `GET`/`PATCH /api/system/settings`, `GET /api/system/status`, and `PATCH /api/system/prompts/{key}` all through `http://127.0.0.1:5173`, and confirmed `SettingsPage.jsx` transforms without error via Vite's dev pipeline (fetched the served, unbundled dev source directly, same technique as Phase 7's `ChatWidget.jsx` check).

**New FUSE-mount data point** (extends the existing operational note above): `vite build`'s `dist/` cleanup (`rm -rf`-equivalent) and a plain `rm -rf dist` from bash both failed with `EPERM: operation not permitted, unlink` against files in the mounted project folder that a *previous* Vite build had written -- distinct from the previously-documented stale-read-after-native-edit issue, this is a write/delete permission gap on files the bash sandbox didn't itself just create. Worked around by building from a scratch copy under `/tmp` for that one verification step; no source files were affected, and the real `frontend/dist/` is gitignored anyway so the stale files there don't matter. If a future session hits `EPERM` deleting build output in this folder, this is why -- build/verify from a `/tmp` copy rather than fighting the mount.

## Phase 9 notes: packaging & GitHub distribution

Two architectural questions were resolved with the author before doing this work (2026-07-31), rather than assumed:

- **Ollama stays external by default.** Confirmed the Phase 2 assumption rather than containerizing it into the main stack -- containerizing would change the default setup for every external puller, including anyone without an NVIDIA GPU. Instead, added `docker-compose.ollama.yml` as an opt-in override (`docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.ollama.yml up --build`): a `chef-ollama` service (official `ollama/ollama` image) with `deploy.resources.reservations.devices` GPU passthrough (`driver: nvidia`, `capabilities: [gpu]`), an `ollama-data` named volume for pulled models, and a `backend.depends_on: [ollama]` merge addition. Extensive comments cover NVIDIA Container Toolkit setup (including the WSL2-specific driver note, since that's the author's actual Docker host per the earlier environment note above) and the author's specific dual-GTX-1080-Ti case -- `count: all` by default, with a documented `device_ids: ["1"]` alternative to dedicate only the headless second card if the primary is wanted for a display workload, plus a caution that GPU index order isn't guaranteed to match physical slot order (check `nvidia-smi -L` first). Switching a running deployment to use the containerized Ollama is a one-line Settings-page edit (`http://chef-ollama:11434`) thanks to Phase 8 -- no `.env` edit or rebuild required, a concrete payoff of that phase's DB-backed settings design.
- **The two-port/no-reverse-proxy simplification (documented since Phase 3) stays as-is.** Confirmed with the author rather than silently fixing or silently leaving it -- a shared nginx/Caddy sidecar remains a valid future improvement but wasn't done this pass; instead it's now called out explicitly in the README's own "Known limitation" section so an external user hits the explanation before the surprise, not after.

**LICENSE**: the repo had none despite being public -- meaning, under default copyright, external users technically had no legal right to reuse/modify/redistribute it, which directly contradicts the project's stated goal of "external users could pull down and run the app themselves." Asked the author rather than assuming; MIT chosen. Added `LICENSE` (MIT, Jason Bentley, 2026) at repo root and a matching README section.

**README rewrite**: replaced the Phase-0-era "Early scaffolding" status with a features overview reflecting everything Phases 1-8 actually shipped (inventory/recipes/meal-planning/health/chat/settings), a step 5 pointing first-time users at the Settings page for anything they skipped in `.env`, the new optional-GPU-Ollama section, the known-limitation callout, and a License section. Setup steps and the no-Docker dev instructions were kept as-is (already accurate).

**Verification**: no Docker daemon reaches Claude's sandbox (same constraint as every prior phase), so `docker compose config`-style validation wasn't literally runnable -- both compose files were checked for valid YAML via `yaml.safe_load`, and a manual Python re-implementation of Compose's override-merge semantics (dict deep-merge, list-append-unique) was run against the two files together to confirm the merged result is exactly what's intended: `ollama` service added with its GPU reservation intact, `backend.depends_on` correctly gains `ollama` via list-merge (rather than replacing anything, since the base service had no `depends_on` of its own), and both named volumes (`chef-data`, `ollama-data`) survive the merge. README reviewed for internal consistency (port numbers, file names, and commands all cross-checked against the actual `docker-compose.yml`/`docker-compose.ollama.yml`/`.env.example` contents rather than re-typed from memory).

## Recipe dish images (backlog item, completed 2026-07-31)

Implemented as its own unit of work per the backlog scoping above, not bolted onto a phase.

Backend (`backend/app/services/recipe_image_service.py`, new; `backend/app/routers/recipes.py`; `backend/app/services/recipe_service.py`): a new `recipe_image_service.py` mirrors `knowledge_service.py`'s UUID-named-file-on-disk pattern (own `RECIPE_IMAGES_DIR`, default `/app/data/recipe_images` -- same Docker volume as everything else, separate directory since the lifecycle differs: 1:1 with a recipe row, deleted on replace/remove/recipe-delete rather than an independent library). `Recipe.image_path` (existed since the Phase 1 schema) needed no migration. Three new endpoints on the recipes router: `POST /{recipe_id}/image` (multipart upload, replaces any existing file only after the new one is safely written), `DELETE /{recipe_id}/image`, and `GET /{recipe_id}/image` (`FileResponse` with content-type guessed from the stored extension). `DELETE /{recipe_id}` (existing) now also cleans up the image file, which it didn't before. Auto-capture wired into `POST /api/recipes/import`: a photo import's raw bytes are saved immediately (before the recipe row even exists) and the path rides along in the preview's `image_path` field; a URL import gets a new `fetch_image_bytes()` helper in `recipe_service.py` that best-effort downloads whatever `extract_content_from_html()` now also extracts as `image` (trafilatura's og:image/twitter:image metadata field, confirmed present via a live test against synthetic HTML with an `og:image` meta tag). Both auto-capture paths are non-fatal on any failure (unsupported content-type, fetch error, oversized response capped at 8MB) -- the import itself never breaks because a photo couldn't be captured. Documented tradeoff: an auto-captured image is written to disk at preview time, before the user confirms the import, so a cancelled import can leave an orphaned file -- accepted as consistent with the app's existing local-single-user pragmatism (best-effort deletes elsewhere) rather than building preview-session cleanup machinery for a low-stakes case.

Frontend (`frontend/src/components/RecipeForm.jsx`, `pages/RecipeDetailPage.jsx`, `pages/RecipesPage.jsx`): the dish photo is handled outside the JSON create/update payload since it's a separate multipart endpoint. In edit mode (`initial.id` present) a new photo uploads immediately on file selection rather than waiting for the rest of the form to submit; in add mode / the import-review step (no id yet) the selected `File` is instead handed to the parent via `onSubmit`'s second argument and uploaded right after `POST /api/recipes` returns an id. One case needed care: when an import's source already had an auto-captured image, that file already exists on disk (from the import-preview step) with no separate `File` object to upload -- `RecipeForm` detects this (`hasImage && !pendingImageFile && initial.image_path`, no `initial.id` yet) and includes `image_path` directly in the create payload so the existing file gets attached rather than silently orphaned. `RecipeDetailPage.jsx` shows the image above the description when present; `RecipesPage.jsx` shows a thumbnail in the recipe list. `theme.css` gained a `.recipe-thumb`/`.recipe-detail-image`/`.recipe-image-preview` block.

**Verification**: backend -- a live curl sequence against a fresh local uvicorn instance (real Alembic migration + seed, mocked `ollama_client.chat`/`describe_image`, and a real local `http.server` serving a synthetic recipe page with an `og:image` meta tag so the URL-import capture path was exercised against a real HTTP fetch, not a mock) covering manual upload/serve/replace (old file confirmed removed from disk)/remove (confirmed 404 after)/reject-bad-content-type, photo-import auto-capture (file written pre-confirm, correctly attached on create, served byte-identical), URL-import auto-capture (fetched og:image confirmed byte-identical to the source file), and recipe deletion cleaning up the image file from disk. Frontend -- `npm run build` succeeds cleanly (built from a `/tmp` scratch copy per the FUSE-mount note above); a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass through the actual proxy path confirmed the recipe list/image endpoints respond correctly through `http://127.0.0.1:5173` and that `RecipeForm.jsx`/`RecipesPage.jsx`/`RecipeDetailPage.jsx` all transform without error via Vite's dev pipeline.

## Knowledge/RAG upgrade (backlog item, completed 2026-07-31)

Replaced Phase 6's "concatenate every active knowledge file, truncate to a combined 3000-char budget, inject into every prompt" with real embedding-based retrieval, ported from the Fiduciary project's `portfolio-api/knowledge.py` + `store.py` (connected and read directly for this) at the author's request. Full design rationale, including what was deliberately adapted or not ported and why, is written up in `backend/app/services/knowledge_service.py`'s module docstring rather than duplicated here -- summary follows.

**Schema** (migration `5ee4a7ad013b`): new `KnowledgeChunk` table (`knowledge_file_id` FK with cascade-delete via the ORM relationship, `chunk_index`, `text`, `vector` as a JSON `list[float]` -- no vector DB needed at this corpus size, matching Fiduciary's validated approach). `KnowledgeFile` gained `indexed_embed_model` (nullable `String`) -- compared against the current `ollama_embed_model` setting to decide whether a file needs (re)indexing, covering both "never indexed" (null) and "the configured embed model changed" in one check.

**Adapted, not 1:1 ported**: Fiduciary's design is a watched-folder with content-hash-keyed cross-file dedup (`knowledge_docs`/`knowledge_paths`/`knowledge_removed` tables) because files can appear/disappear/rename on disk outside the app's control. Chef's `KnowledgeFile` rows are explicit, user-managed uploads via the existing UI with content set once and never edited -- there's no "did the bytes change under this path" case to detect, so the simpler `indexed_embed_model` comparison covers everything that can actually go stale here without needing hash-keyed storage tables. Chunking (`chunk_text`, 1400 chars/200 char overlap, paragraph/sentence-boundary-aware) and cosine similarity (`_cosine_similarity`) are ported essentially verbatim -- these are pure algorithms with no folder-vs-DB-identity question attached.

**Deliberately not ported**: `search_knowledge` as a model-invoked tool call (unverified whether Chef's target Ollama models support reliable tool-calling, and nothing else in this codebase uses native tool-calling -- every other AI-structured-output feature here, including chat's action-proposal system, uses prompt-engineered JSON instead, so retrieval results are injected directly into the prompt, the simpler fallback the backlog note itself allowed for); background-thread reindexing with a pollable progress endpoint (indexing is synchronous and fast enough at Chef's realistically small corpus size -- revisit only if real usage shows otherwise).

**New setting**: `ollama_embed_model` (default `bge-m3`, same as Fiduciary) added to `settings_service.SETTING_SPECS` -- editable from the existing Settings page with no frontend change needed, since that page already renders whatever `SETTING_SPECS` contains. New `ollama_client.embed()` wraps the `ollama` package's `Client.embeddings()`.

**Two integration points, one of them a real gap closed, not just an upgrade**: `meal_plan_service.gather_generation_context()` now builds a synthetic retrieval query from the household's dietary restrictions + goals (there's no single natural user question during generation the way chat has one) and passes it to `health_service.build_knowledge_context(db, query)`. More significantly: **persistent chat (`chat_service.py`/`routers/chat.py`) previously injected no knowledge-file grounding at all** -- Phase 7's notes only mention meal-plan generation using it. This session added it: the user's actual message is passed as the retrieval query (`build_chat_context(db, query=message_text)`), the natural best-fit use case for retrieval since a real question already IS a query, unlike generation's synthetic one. A new `{knowledge_section}` slot in `CHAT_CONTEXT_TEMPLATE` stays empty (and costs nothing) when nothing relevant is retrieved.

**Knowledge-file UI**: upload now eagerly (best-effort, non-fatal) indexes a file immediately via `knowledge_service.ensure_indexed(db, knowledge_file=kf)` rather than waiting for the first generation/chat call to trigger it lazily -- nicer feedback in the UI. `KnowledgeFileRead` gained `chunk_count`; `KnowledgeFilesPanel.jsx` shows an "N chunks indexed" / "indexing..." tag per file and its description text was updated to reflect that only relevant excerpts are retrieved now, not the whole file every time.

**Verification**: a from-scratch verification script run directly against a live test DB (not just curl) since this needed to inspect actual internal state and prompt text, the same technique Phase 6/7 used for grounding verification -- 32 checks covering: `chunk_text`/`_cosine_similarity` as pure functions (empty input, boundary splitting, identical/orthogonal-vector cosine values); indexing a 5-file corpus with deliberately topically-distinct content (gluten-free/celiac, cholesterol/fiber, camping equipment, wine pairing, grocery budgeting) via a deterministic bag-of-hashed-words stand-in for `ollama_client.embed` (real semantic embeddings aren't available in the sandbox, same constraint as every other Ollama-dependent verification in this project); confirming a targeted query's top-k results come from the topically relevant files and exclude an irrelevant one at a small k, and that a camping-specific query correctly surfaces the camping file; confirming both `meal_plan_service.gather_generation_context()`'s and `chat_service.build_chat_context()`'s resulting `knowledge_context` actually contains the retrieved text (inspected the literal built context and the literal generated prompt string, not just that a function returned something) and is meaningfully shorter than the full 5-file corpus rather than everything concatenated; confirming a chat call with no query gets an empty knowledge_context (existing non-retrieval call sites unaffected); model-change invalidation (changing `ollama_embed_model` correctly triggers re-indexing, verified via `indexed_embed_model` updating); deactivating a file removing it from retrieval; and cascade-delete removing a file's chunks. Separately verified via live curl against a real uvicorn instance: uploading a file returns a non-zero `chunk_count` immediately (eager indexing), and deleting a file leaves zero `knowledge_chunks` rows behind. Backend `py_compile` clean; frontend `npm run build` clean; a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass through the actual proxy path confirmed `chunk_count` renders in the knowledge files list and `ollama_embed_model` appears in the Settings page's settings list with no frontend code change required for that second part.

## Recipe variants: committing an AI-modified recipe (feature, completed 2026-07-31)

Added in response to a direct follow-up question, not a pre-queued backlog item: the author asked whether an AI-modified recipe (e.g. "take this imported recipe and make it gluten-free") could ever be *saved*, since chat modifications were previously always ephemeral. Confirmed via code inspection that this capability did not exist (`RecipeChatResponse` only carried `reply`; persistent chat's `ALLOWED_ACTION_TYPES` had no recipe-modification type) before building it. The author wanted both chat surfaces able to propose edits, and asked for a "version or variant system" if feasible, with an explicit fallback to safer overwrite-only behavior if not -- safety (never accidentally serving a recipe that's drifted out of sync with dietary needs) was the stated top priority.

**Schema** (migration `349c7fd3ed4c`): `Recipe` gained `parent_recipe_id` (nullable self-referential FK, `sa.ForeignKey("recipes.id")`, added via `op.batch_alter_table` since SQLite can't `ALTER TABLE` a raw FK constraint) and `variant_label` (nullable `String(100)`, e.g. "Gluten-Free"). A variant is deliberately just a normal, independent `Recipe` row that happens to point at the recipe it was derived from -- not a diff/patch/version-history entry -- the simplest structure that satisfies "keep the original safe from accidental modification" without over-building something not asked for. No cascade delete on the FK (same accepted SQLite-doesn't-enforce-FK-by-default tradeoff as `HealthMetricEntry.household_member_id` from Phase 6): a variant should survive its parent being deleted, since by that point it's a real independent recipe, not a dependent patch. ORM relationship: `Recipe.variants` (children) / `Recipe.parent_recipe` (backref, `remote_side="Recipe.id"`), verified bidirectionally against a live test DB.

**Recipe-scoped chat can now propose edits** (`recipe_service.RECIPE_MODIFY_INSTRUCTIONS`, `parse_recipe_chat_response`): the existing read-only "Ask the Chef" chat (Phase 4.1) is extended, not replaced -- every turn can still be a plain answer, but a message like "make this gluten-free" now gets a `proposed_recipe` (the entire recipe as it should look after the edit, same shape as recipe import, coerced through the existing shared `coerce_recipe_fields()`) and a suggested `variant_label`. `RecipeChatResponse` gained both fields. Same preview-then-confirm discipline as every other AI-structured-output feature in this app -- nothing is saved by chat code itself, only proposed.

**Persistent chat gained `recipe_update_proposal`**, initially variant-ONLY -- it always created a new `Recipe` row via `parent_recipe_id`/`variant_label`, with no in-place overwrite path, since the widget's confirm is a single click with no review form the way the recipe-scoped chat's `RecipeForm` step is. **Extended same day** after the author asked directly whether an overwrite path was available from chat at all, for either the original recipe or a specific variant. Answer: the recipe-scoped chat already supported this implicitly (it's scoped to whatever recipe/variant page the user is viewing, and already offered an overwrite choice there), but the persistent widget didn't. Added a `"mode": "variant"|"overwrite"` field to the action (default `"variant"`, so existing behavior is unchanged unless the user's phrasing clearly asks to update/replace/overwrite the existing recipe rather than save a copy -- `CHAT_CONTEXT_TEMPLATE` spells out the distinguishing phrases and instructs defaulting to `"variant"` when ambiguous). An unrecognized/missing `mode` value from the model falls back to `"variant"`, the safer default, rather than being trusted as-is (`chat_service._coerce_action`). `ChatWidget.jsx`'s `executeAction` branches on `mode` (POST a new recipe with `parent_recipe_id` set, vs. PATCH the target recipe directly), and `ActionCard` requires an extra native `window.confirm()` specifically for `"overwrite"` mode before calling through -- a deliberate speed bump matching the app's existing convention of a browser confirm for destructive actions (e.g. recipe delete), since this is the one action type in the persistent chat that can silently replace a recipe's content with no review step first. `chat_service.build_chat_context()` also gained a lightweight `recipe_catalog` (id, title only) so the model can reference an existing recipe by a real id; `CHAT_CONTEXT_TEMPLATE` explicitly forbids inventing a `target_recipe_id` not in that list, same discipline as the existing `entry_id`/`meal_plan_id` guard from Phase 7.

**New endpoint**: `GET /api/recipes/{id}/variants` lists a recipe's children. `_to_read()` (in `routers/recipes.py`) now populates `parent_recipe_title` (via `recipe.parent_recipe.title if recipe.parent_recipe else None`) and `variant_count` (via `len(recipe.variants)`) on every `RecipeRead`, so the detail page can show "Variant of X" / a variants list without extra round-trips for the common case.

**Frontend**: `RecipeForm.jsx` gained an optional `submitLabel` prop (default unchanged, "Save recipe") so the same shared form can be reused for non-edit submit flows with different button text. `RecipeChat.jsx` shows a review card when a turn's response includes `proposed_recipe`: a radio choice between "Save as a new variant" (default, with an editable label field pre-filled from the AI's suggestion) and "Update this recipe (overwrite)", reusing `RecipeForm` pre-filled with the proposal; saving as a variant POSTs to `/api/recipes` with `parent_recipe_id`/`variant_label` set and navigates to the new recipe, saving as overwrite PATCHes the current recipe and reloads it in place. The proposal is deliberately cleared (not accumulated) whenever a chat turn's reply isn't itself another proposal, since a fresh question implies the user has moved on from reviewing the pending one -- nothing is lost since it was only ever a preview. `RecipeDetailPage.jsx` shows a "Variant of [parent]" link when `parent_recipe_id` is set, a variant-label tag next to the title, and a "Variants" list (fetched from the new endpoint) when `variant_count > 0`. `ChatWidget.jsx`'s `executeAction()` gained a `recipe_update_proposal` case that branches on `action.mode` (POST a new variant, or PATCH the target directly for `"overwrite"`, guarded by the `window.confirm()` described above); its confirmed-action card labels itself "Recipe — new variant" or "Recipe — overwrite existing" and links to either "View saved variant" or "View updated recipe".

**Verification**: backend `py_compile` clean across all touched modules; the migration re-applied cleanly through the full chain against a fresh test DB (`PRAGMA table_info`/`PRAGMA foreign_key_list` confirmed the new column/constraint). End-to-end verification used a live FastAPI `TestClient` against a fresh seeded DB with `ollama_client.chat` monkeypatched (no live Ollama reaches the sandbox, same constraint as every prior phase) to return canned structured responses, covering: the recipe-scoped chat proposing a gluten-free ingredient swap and the resulting `proposed_recipe`/`variant_label` round-tripping correctly; saving that proposal as a new variant and confirming the parent's `variant_count` and `GET .../variants` both reflect it and `parent_recipe_title` resolves correctly on the child; the persistent chat's `recipe_update_proposal` action in both modes, including confirming the recipe catalog appears in the literal generated system prompt text (same "inspect the real prompt, not just the context dict" technique used since Phase 6/7), that a deliberately hallucinated extra action type in the same response is silently dropped, and that an unrecognized/missing `mode` value coerces to the safer `"variant"` default rather than being trusted as-is; simulating `ChatWidget.jsx`'s exact `executeAction` calls for both the POST-a-variant and PATCH-to-overwrite paths and confirming each lands correctly (the overwrite case confirmed against a recipe that already had two prior variants, verifying its own `variant_count`/`parent_recipe_id` were unaffected by the content update); and the recipe-scoped chat's "overwrite in place" PATCH path. Frontend `npm run build` succeeds cleanly (worked around the previously-documented FUSE-mount `EPERM` deleting a prior build's `dist/` by renaming it out of the way with `os.rename` rather than `rm -rf`, per the existing operational note).

## B1.1 notes: ingredient-to-food-database resolution layer (backlog item, completed 2026-07-31)

First implementation slice from the post-audit sequence agreed with the author (plumbing before user-visible features, per the dependency note at the top of the Backlog section). Scope was deliberately kept to resolution + caching only -- summing resolved nutrients into `Recipe.nutrition` with a computed/partial/ai_estimated provenance tag (B1.2) and reconciling the two generation prompts' nutrition key sets (B1.3) are separate, not-yet-built passes that this lays the groundwork for.

**New service** (`backend/app/services/food_data_service.py`): tries USDA FoodData Central first (only if a key is configured), falls back to Open Food Facts (no key needed) when USDA has no candidate or the candidate has no nutrients this app tracks, and returns `None` (not an error) when neither source has anything -- callers should treat that as "still AI-estimated only." Two pure parsing functions (`_parse_usda_nutrients`, `_parse_off_nutrients`) translate each API's native response shape into one shared per-100g dict; the key set is a superset of both existing AI-prompt nutrition fields plus saturated fat/sugars (anticipating B1.3, not yet consumed anywhere else). OFF's gram-denominated sodium/cholesterol fields are converted to milligrams to match USDA's and the rest of the app's convention. No fuzzy ranking beyond each API's own search -- the top result is taken as the match; documented as a simplification to revisit once real usage shows how often that's wrong (same category of tradeoff as the existing name-only ingredient matching in `inventory_service.py`/`meal_plan_service.py`).

**New setting**: `usda_fdc_api_key` (secret, optional, `SETTING_SPECS` entry + `.env.example` documentation) -- the author does not have a key yet (confirmed 2026-07-31); the integration degrades gracefully to Open Food Facts / AI-estimated nutrition until one is added via the Settings UI, no code change needed when that happens.

**Schema** (migration `30de1d21d026`): `RecipeIngredient` gained `fdc_id`, `off_barcode`, `resolved_food_name`, `resolution_source` (`usda`/`off`/`unresolved`/`None`), `nutrition_per_100g` (JSON snapshot at resolution time, avoids re-hitting either API on every recipe view), and `resolved_at`. `op.batch_alter_table` used per the established SQLite convention. A real match (`usda`/`off`) is cached and skipped on future calls unless `force=True`; a prior `unresolved` result is deliberately NOT sticky the same way, so ingredients that failed before a USDA key was added retry automatically on the next call rather than needing a forced re-resolve.

**New endpoint**: `POST /api/recipes/{recipe_id}/resolve-nutrition` (optional `force` query param) resolves every ingredient on a recipe and persists the result; `RecipeIngredientRead` and `_to_read()` (`routers/recipes.py`) surface the new fields so the frontend can eventually show a per-ingredient resolution badge (no frontend UI added yet -- this session was backend-only, matching the "plumbing first" sequencing decision). Deliberately not wired into recipe create/update automatically, so recipe creation stays fast and doesn't depend on two external APIs being reachable; explicit/on-demand for now, revisit if usage wants it automatic.

**Also started, opportunistically**: this is the first *committed* test in the repo (`backend/tests/`, new) -- B9.1 flagged that no phase's tests had ever been committed despite every phase being verified with real ones. Added `backend/tests/conftest.py` (redirects `DATABASE_URL`/`SECRETS_KEY_FILE`/`SECRETS_KEYRING_FILE` to throwaway temp paths before any `app.*` import, so pytest never touches the real `/app/data` volume paths) and `backend/tests/test_food_data_service.py` (25 tests: pure-function parsing edge cases, each network function with `httpx.get` monkeypatched, and the resolve-and-cache caching/force/retry-when-unresolved semantics). This is a first slice, not a claim that B9.1 is done -- no CI workflow yet, no `requirements-dev.txt`, and none of Phases 1-9's existing (verified-but-uncommitted) tests were reconstructed this session. `pytest` itself isn't yet in any requirements file; tests were run from a scratch venv (`/tmp/chef-b11-venv`) against the real `backend/requirements.txt` plus `pytest`, per the established "no Docker/live services in the sandbox" verification approach.

**Verification**: 25 unit tests pass (`pytest backend/tests/test_food_data_service.py`, run from a scratch venv since no project venv/CI exists yet). Live end-to-end pass: applied the new migration against a fresh SQLite DB on top of the full existing chain (`alembic upgrade head`, all 8 migrations run cleanly), confirmed the six new columns via `PRAGMA table_info`, ran `python -m app.seed`, started a real local uvicorn instance, created a recipe with three ingredients via `POST /api/recipes`, and called `POST /{id}/resolve-nutrition` twice. As expected (no route to api.nal.usda.gov/world.openfoodfacts.org from Claude's sandbox, the same network-allowlist constraint noted for every external-API-dependent feature in this project), all three ingredients landed as `resolution_source: "unresolved"` on both calls without any error -- confirming the graceful-degradation path works for real, not just in a mock, and that "unresolved" correctly keeps retrying rather than going sticky. Also confirmed `usda_fdc_api_key` surfaces correctly in `GET /api/system/settings` with zero frontend changes needed (same generic `SETTING_SPECS`-driven rendering as `ollama_embed_model`). `py_compile` clean across all touched backend files. **Not yet verified against a real USDA/OFF response** (sandbox has no route to either) -- the parsing functions are exercised against fixture payloads shaped like each API's documented schema, not a live call; worth a manual spot-check once the author has a USDA key and can reach the real APIs from wherever this actually runs.

## B5.3/B10.5 notes: shared unit-conversion layer, core only (started 2026-07-31)

Second plumbing item in the agreed sequence, done at the very end of a session with the token budget nearly exhausted -- **this is the conversion table only, not the full backlog item.** Nothing that currently does name-only ingredient matching has been touched yet.

**New service** (`backend/app/services/unit_conversion_service.py`): a pure, dependency-free conversion table (no DB, no network) covering volume<->volume (tsp/tbsp/fl_oz/cup/pt/qt/gal/ml/l) and mass<->mass (g/kg/oz/lb) conversions, plus a forgiving `normalize_unit()` that resolves free-text spelling variants ("Tbsp", "tablespoons", "fl. oz.") to one canonical key. Count-based units (clove, can, whole, etc.) are recognized but deliberately never convertible -- `convert()` returns `None` rather than guessing. Cross-family conversion (volume<->mass, the B10.5 "weight mode" case) is supported via an optional `density_g_per_ml` parameter, but this module never sources a density itself -- that's B1.1's job (USDA FoodData Central publishes gram-weight-per-portion data), consistent with the backlog item's explicit instruction to show a conversion as unavailable rather than let the LLM invent one.

**Not done yet, explicitly**: (1) wiring into `inventory_service.py`'s name-only matching (`find_by_name`/`deduct_by_name`) and `meal_plan_service.py`'s `aggregate_ingredients`/`subtract_inventory` -- these still compare by name only, "2 cup flour" and "1 lb flour" still won't reconcile; (2) the B10.5 recipe-detail-page Imperial/Metric/Weight toggle UI -- no frontend work happened this session; (3) sourcing real ingredient densities from B1.1 for the weight-mode case -- B1.1 stores per-100g nutrients, not density-per-mL directly, so a small additional helper (grams-per-100g plus a typical-serving-volume assumption, or a small hardcoded density table for common baking ingredients as a fallback) is likely needed and hasn't been designed yet.

**Verification**: 14 new unit tests (normalize_unit synonym/case handling, unit_family classification, same-family conversions in both directions, cross-family conversion with and without density, rejection of count units and non-positive density) -- all passing alongside the existing 25 from B1.1 (39 total in `backend/tests/`). `py_compile` clean. No live-server pass this time (session token budget was the binding constraint) -- nothing outside this one new pure-function module changed, so there was nothing yet to exercise end-to-end.

**Update, same day**: the wiring described above as "not done yet" is now done.

- `inventory_service.deduct_by_name()` gained an optional `unit` parameter (backward compatible, defaults `None`). When both `unit` and the matched item's `item.unit` are known and differ, the quantity is converted into the item's unit before subtracting -- fixing a real bug where e.g. "deduct 8 oz" against an item logged in pounds previously subtracted 8 from the raw pound quantity. Falls back to the old raw-number behavior when conversion isn't possible (count units, unknown units, or a volume<->mass gap with no density), so this is purely additive, never a regression. `POST /api/inventory/deduct` (`InventoryDeductRequest`) gained a matching optional `unit` field, and the meal-plan confirm endpoint (`routers/meal_plan.py`) now passes each scaled ingredient's real unit through instead of dropping it.
- `meal_plan_service.aggregate_ingredients()` now merges ingredient lines sharing a name across convertible units (e.g. "2 cup flour" + "8 tbsp flour" -> one 2.5-cup line) via a new `_merge_same_name_group()` helper, instead of only merging on an exact `(name, unit)` key. Genuinely incompatible units (count units, or volume vs. mass with no density) still stay as separate lines -- never guesses.
- `meal_plan_service.subtract_inventory()` now converts on-hand inventory quantity into the grocery line's unit before comparing, fixing a second real bug: a grocery line needing "2 lb chicken" against an inventory row logged as "500 g" previously compared `2 - 500` as raw numbers (silently treating the item as fully in stock). Falls back to the old raw-number comparison when no conversion is available, an unchanged known imprecision for that remaining case, not a new one.
- Deliberately NOT touched: chat's `inventory_deduct` action (Phase 7) still has no `unit` field in its prompt shape -- natural-language chat messages rarely state a precise unit anyway ("we're out of milk"), so this was left as a documented future enhancement rather than scope creep into prompt engineering this pass.

**Verification**: 15 new tests (`backend/tests/test_unit_conversion_wiring.py`) covering cross-unit aggregation merging, incompatible-unit fallback, exact-unit-still-merges (no regression), on-hand unit conversion in `subtract_inventory` (including a fully-covered case and an unconvertible-falls-back case), and `deduct_by_name`'s cross-unit/same-unit/no-unit/no-quantity paths against a real `db_session` fixture -- all 53 tests in the repo pass together. Live end-to-end: started a real local uvicorn instance, created a "butter" inventory row logged as `1 lb`, called `POST /api/inventory/deduct` for `8 oz`, and confirmed the item's quantity landed at exactly `0.5` (lb) through the actual HTTP layer, not just the unit-tested function in isolation. `py_compile` clean across all touched files.

**If a future session picks this up cold**: read this section, then move to B10.5's frontend toggle only after designing the density-sourcing helper (B1.1 stores per-100g nutrients, not density-per-mL directly -- a small additional helper is needed, e.g. deriving an approximate density from a food's per-100g mass/volume relationship or a small hardcoded fallback table for common baking ingredients) -- the toggle is pointless without a real weight-mode conversion behind it. B1.2/B1.3 (nutrition summation + provenance, prompt reconciliation) and B10.4 (responsive UI) remain the other next items per the sequence agreed with the author.

## B1.2/B1.3 notes: computed nutrition provenance + reconciled prompt key set (completed 2026-07-31)

Next items per the sequence agreed with the author (see the B5.3/B10.5 notes section above): B1.1 (done), the shared unit-conversion layer (done), then B1.2/B1.3. Both were done together in one pass since B1.2's summation needs a settled key set to sum into, and B1.3's key-set reconciliation has no real content of its own without B1.2 giving it a consumer.

**B1.3 -- one shared key set, not two.** `food_data_service.py` gained `NUTRITION_KEYS` (the 9 keys `_parse_usda_nutrients`/`_parse_off_nutrients` already produced: `calories, protein_g, carbs_g, fat_g, fiber_g, sodium_mg, cholesterol_mg, saturated_fat_g, sugars_g`) and a matching `NUTRITION_PROMPT_HINT` string. `recipe_service.RECIPE_IMPORT_PROMPT` (previously 7 keys), `recipe_service.RECIPE_MODIFY_INSTRUCTIONS` (previously no explicit list at all), and `meal_plan_service.MEAL_PLAN_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` (previously 4 keys) all now interpolate the same hint via a plain `str.replace()` pass at module-definition time -- deliberately not a second `.format()` kwarg, since both templates already use `.format()` for their own per-call placeholders (`{content}`, `{household_size}`, etc.) and `.format()` requires every `{...}` token to be filled in the same call. `added_sugars_g` and `soluble_fiber_g` were investigated per the backlog text's "if cleanly available" clause and left out on purpose: neither USDA FDC's Foundation/SR Legacy nutrient set nor Open Food Facts' core `nutriments` fields distinguish added from total sugars, or soluble from total fiber -- labeling one as the other would be a fabrication this app's existing discipline (never let the LLM/a parser invent a number a real source doesn't give it) rules out.

**B1.2 -- summation, with an honest three-way provenance label.** `Recipe` gained `nutrition_provenance` (migration `8f2c4b6a1d9e`, nullable `String(20)`: `computed` / `partial` / `ai_estimated` / `None` for a legacy never-touched row). New pure functions in `food_data_service.py`:

- `compute_ingredient_grams(ingredient)` -- converts one ingredient's `quantity`+`unit` to grams via `unit_conversion_service.convert(quantity, unit, "g")`. Returns `None` for no stated quantity, a count-based unit ("2 eggs"), or a volume-based unit ("1 cup") -- the last case is a real, documented limitation: nothing in this codebase sources a per-ingredient density yet (USDA FDC does publish portion/gram-weight data that could eventually fill this gap, `resolve_and_cache_ingredient` just doesn't parse it), so a recipe with only volume-unit ingredients can resolve 100% against USDA/OFF and still never reach `"computed"`. This is the same gap B5.3/B10.5's notes already flagged for the weight-mode toggle -- one missing piece blocks both.
- `compute_recipe_nutrition(recipe)` -- sums each ingredient's cached `nutrition_per_100g` scaled to its actual gram weight, divides by `default_servings` (falling back to 1 rather than dividing by zero), and returns `(nutrition_dict, provenance)`. Ingredients with no stated quantity (e.g. "salt to taste") are excluded from both the sum and the completeness check -- they're not a resolution failure, there's just nothing to weigh. `"ai_estimated"` is returned with the recipe's **existing** nutrition dict completely unchanged (never blanked, never overwritten with a fabricated zero-ingredient total) whenever nothing contributed real data; `"computed"` only when every quantified ingredient contributed; `"partial"` otherwise, with the dict being an honest sum over an incomplete ingredient list (documented in the UI, see below, as a likely undercount, not a final number).

**New endpoint** `POST /api/recipes/{id}/compute-nutrition` (`force` query param, same semantics as the existing `/resolve-nutrition`): resolves any never-attempted ingredient first (skips already-`"unresolved"` ones unless `force=True`, so this doesn't silently start retrying every failed lookup's network call on every click), then calls `compute_recipe_nutrition` and persists -- `Recipe.nutrition` is only overwritten when the result isn't `"ai_estimated"`, so an existing AI guess is preserved (just correctly labeled) rather than erased when resolution finds nothing to compute from. `RecipeRead` gained `nutrition_provenance` (output-only, like `RecipeIngredientRead.resolution_source` -- never accepted from `RecipeCreate`/`RecipeUpdate`, so a client can't just assert `"computed"`).

**Provenance on the normal create/update paths, and a bug avoided, not just fixed.** `create_recipe` (router) and `recipe_service.create_recipe_from_parsed` (used by meal-plan generation's `new_recipe`) both set `nutrition_provenance="ai_estimated"` whenever a non-empty nutrition dict comes in -- an AI guess and a manually-typed value share the same "not verified against a food database" bucket. `update_recipe`'s PATCH handler only resets provenance back to `"ai_estimated"` when the incoming `nutrition` dict actually **differs** from what's stored -- caught during design, not by a failing test: `RecipeForm.jsx` always resends whatever nutrition fields it loaded (see the frontend section below), so a same-value resend on an unrelated edit (retitling, toggling staple) would otherwise silently demote a `"computed"` recipe back to `"ai_estimated"` on every save with no nutrition change at all. Verified live (see below) that an unchanged-nutrition PATCH leaves `"partial"`/`"computed"` alone while a changed one correctly resets to `"ai_estimated"`.

**Frontend.** `RecipeForm.jsx`'s nutrition fieldset previously hardcoded exactly 4 of the (now 9) possible keys -- a real, pre-existing data-loss bug found during the B1.2/B1.3 code survey: editing any recipe that had `fiber_g`/`sodium_mg`/`cholesterol_mg`/etc. (from the 7-key import prompt) silently dropped those keys on save, since the submit builder only ever wrote back the 4 it knew about. Replaced the four hardcoded fields with a `NUTRIENT_FIELDS` config array driving state init/hydration/submit-building/JSX rendering from one list, so the three can't drift apart again the way the original 4-vs-7-vs-9 split happened in the first place. Added a hint above the fieldset warning the user that editing-and-saving a `"computed"`/`"partial"` recipe's nutrition here will mark it back to `"ai_estimated"` (true, and now the correct behavior per the paragraph above -- editing IS a real change). `RecipeDetailPage.jsx` gained a `PROVENANCE_INFO`-driven badge above the nutrition list (green "Computed from ingredient data" / amber "Partially computed..." / muted "AI estimate -- not verified..."), a `NUTRIENT_LABELS` map for friendlier display text (falls back to the raw key for anything unrecognized), and a "Compute from ingredients" button wired to the new endpoint. `theme.css` gained `.provenance-badge`/`.provenance-computed`/`.provenance-partial`/`.provenance-estimated` (reusing the existing secondary/accent/text-muted tokens, same traffic-light convention as the Settings page's `.status-dot` and the Health page's trend-chart coloring) and `.nutrition-field-grid` (a `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(140px, 1fr))` grid for the now-9-field fieldset -- deliberately given its own class rather than reusing `.form-row`, since `.item-form .form-row`'s existing `display: flex` rule has higher specificity than a bare `.nutrition-field-grid` class and would have silently overridden the grid layout).

**Verification.** 15 new unit tests (`backend/tests/test_nutrition_calc_service.py`, all 68 tests in the repo passing together) covering `compute_ingredient_grams` (mass-unit conversion, gram passthrough, no-quantity/no-unit/count-unit/volume-unit-all-return-None) and `compute_recipe_nutrition`'s every provenance branch (all-resolved-mass-units -> computed with hand-verified summed-then-per-serving-divided numbers; mixed resolved/unconvertible -> partial; mixed resolved/never-resolved -> partial; nothing resolved -> ai_estimated with the existing dict returned byte-for-byte unchanged; zero ingredients -> ai_estimated; only-unquantified-ingredients -> ai_estimated; an unquantified ingredient correctly excluded from the completeness denominator rather than forcing "partial"; rounding; zero-`default_servings` not dividing by zero). Full migration chain re-verified against a fresh SQLite DB (`alembic upgrade head` through all 9 revisions cleanly, `PRAGMA table_info` confirming the new column). Live end-to-end pass against a real local uvicorn instance: created a recipe via the actual `POST /api/recipes` (confirmed `nutrition_provenance` comes back `"ai_estimated"` immediately, matching the manual-nutrition-dict rule); hand-simulated a successful USDA/OFF resolution by writing directly to the ingredients table (no live network route to either API from the sandbox, same constraint as every B1.1 verification) and confirmed `POST /{id}/compute-nutrition` produces `"partial"` with correctly hand-verified summed-and-divided numbers when one ingredient's unit can't convert to grams (a real tbsp-of-olive-oil case, not a contrived one) and `"computed"` with correctly hand-verified numbers once that same ingredient is switched to a gram quantity; confirmed a same-value nutrition PATCH leaves `"partial"` provenance alone while a changed-value PATCH correctly resets it to `"ai_estimated"`; confirmed a 404 on a nonexistent recipe id. Frontend: `npm run build` succeeds cleanly (built from a `/tmp` scratch copy per the standing FUSE-mount note above); a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass through the actual proxy path confirmed `RecipeDetailPage.jsx`/`RecipeForm.jsx` both transform without error via Vite's dev pipeline and that `nutrition_provenance` round-trips through `GET /api/recipes/{id}` as served through the real browser-facing proxy.

**Known limitation, carried forward from B5.3/B10.5's own notes rather than fixed here:** a recipe whose ingredients are all volume-unit (cups, tablespoons, ...) will never reach `"computed"`/`"partial"` even after every ingredient successfully resolves against USDA/OFF, because nothing sources a per-ingredient density to convert volume to grams. Whoever picks up B10.5's weight-mode toggle (or a future density-sourcing pass) closes this gap for both features at once, per the dependency note already at the top of the Backlog section.

## Fix: BACKEND_PORT changeable without a frontend rebuild (2026-07-31)

Author-reported: tried to run the stack and port `8095` was already in use on their host. Changing `BACKEND_PORT` in `.env` alone didn't fix it -- the production frontend build talked to the backend at a hardcoded `8095` baked into `src/api.js` at build time (documented as a known simplification since Phase 3, and explicitly called out again in README's "Known limitation" section), so the two ports drifted apart the moment `BACKEND_PORT` changed. Fixing this properly (not just telling the author to edit `api.js` and rebuild) needed a way for the already-built static bundle to learn the current `BACKEND_PORT` at container *start*, not build time.

**Runtime-config injection, not a build-time constant.** Added `frontend/docker-entrypoint.sh` (mirrors the backend's own `docker-entrypoint.sh` pattern) which runs on every container start and writes `dist/config.js` containing `window.__CHEF_CONFIG__ = { backendPort: "<current BACKEND_PORT>" }` -- `BACKEND_PORT` is already present in the frontend container's environment via docker-compose's existing `env_file: .env`, no compose change needed. `frontend/Dockerfile` copies the script, `chmod +x`s it, and sets it as `ENTRYPOINT` (CMD unchanged: `serve -s dist -l 5173`). `index.html` loads `/config.js` via a plain classic `<script>` tag placed before the module-type app bundle script -- classic parser-blocking scripts always execute before deferred/module scripts regardless of final tag order in the document (module scripts are implicitly deferred per the HTML spec), which matters here because Vite's build always relocates the bundled module script into `<head>`, ahead of where `/config.js` sits in `<body>` -- confirmed the execution-order guarantee holds anyway by building and inspecting the actual output. `src/api.js`'s `BACKEND_PORT` constant now reads `window.__CHEF_CONFIG__?.backendPort || 8095` instead of a literal `8095`.

**A real placeholder file, not just an entrypoint-only fix.** First pass left `/config.js` served by nothing in `npm run dev` (no `dist/`, no entrypoint script running) -- Vite's dev server responded to the unmatched `/config.js` request with its SPA-fallback `index.html` (200, not 404), which the browser then tried to execute as JavaScript and threw a console error on (`<!doctype html>` isn't valid JS). Caught by actually curling `/config.js` against a live dev server rather than assuming a 404, not by inspection. Fixed by adding `frontend/public/config.js` (a real static file Vite serves directly in dev, and copies verbatim into `dist/` at build time) with the same default-8095 content -- so dev mode gets a real, harmless, valid script, and a production build's `dist/config.js` always starts from a valid file even before the entrypoint overwrites it with the real port at container start.

**Dev-without-Docker proxy target, too.** `vite.config.js`'s dev-server proxy (`/api`, `/health`) previously hardcoded `http://localhost:8095` as its fallback target (with `VITE_BACKEND_URL` as the only override). Now falls back to `http://localhost:${BACKEND_PORT}` (read via `loadEnv`, so a `.env`/`.env.local` in `frontend/` or a shell-exported `BACKEND_PORT` both work) before falling further back to the `8095` literal -- consistent with the same env var name used everywhere else in this project for this exact purpose, rather than introducing a second one. `VITE_BACKEND_URL` still takes precedence when set, for the different-host/scheme case that isn't just a port.

**README** updated: the "Known limitation" section no longer says changing `BACKEND_PORT` requires a frontend rebuild (it doesn't, anymore) and now states the actual fix path (change `.env`, `docker compose up`, no `--build`). `.env.example`'s `BACKEND_PORT`/`FRONTEND_PORT` comment expanded to say the same.

**Verification.** `sh -n` syntax-checked the new entrypoint script; ran it standalone against a scratch `dist/` with both a custom `BACKEND_PORT` and no env var set at all, confirming the generated `config.js` content in both cases. Built the frontend from a `/tmp` scratch copy (per the standing FUSE-mount note) and confirmed: `dist/index.html` retains the `/config.js` script tag in the right relative position; `dist/config.js` exists post-build with the placeholder content (copied from `public/`); running the entrypoint script against that same `dist/` afterward correctly overwrites it with a custom port (`9500` in testing); the built JS bundle's minified output actually references `window.__CHEF_CONFIG__` (grepped for the literal string) rather than having it constant-folded away. Served the built `dist/` for real with a plain HTTP server and curled both `index.html` and `config.js` to confirm the exact bytes a browser would receive. Ran a live Vite dev server against a live backend and curled `/config.js` before and after adding `public/config.js`, catching the SPA-fallback issue described above for real rather than assuming dev mode was fine.

## Fix: recipe-import review form usability + ingredient-parsing accuracy (2026-08-02)

Author-reported, with a screenshot and an attached PDF (a Bon Appétit "Pumpkin Chiffon Pie" recipe page saved to PDF) that reproduced all three complaints at once: "excessively small field sizes... too little to review and correct," "the ingredients seems to be amazingly wrong," and "the font (grey on white) makes it extremely difficult to see the content."

**Root-caused before touching any code.** Extracted the PDF's actual text with both `pypdf` (what `recipe_service.extract_pdf_text` uses) and `pdfplumber` as a cross-check -- both agree the raw text handed to the model was clean and complete, including the Unicode fraction glyphs (¼, ½, ¾, ⅔) and the real ingredient lines ("2 Tbsp. sugar" for the crust, "¾ (scant) cup plus 2 Tbsp. sugar, divided" for the filling, "1 envelope unflavored gelatin (2½ tsp.)"). So this was not a PDF-extraction bug. Comparing that ground truth against the screenshot's parsed rows found two genuinely separate problems bundled into one report:

1. **A layout bug, not a data bug, for most of what looked "amazingly wrong."** `RecipeForm.jsx`'s ingredient row renders 4 bare `<input>`s (name/qty/unit/prep_note) directly inside a `.form-row.ingredient-row` div, with no flex-basis/min-width of their own -- the generic `.form-row label { flex:1; min-width:140px }` rule only ever applied to `<label>` children, and these inputs aren't wrapped in one. Each input just took its browser-default width, and on a desktop viewport wide enough to stay off the 640px mobile breakpoint but too narrow to fit all 4 default-width inputs plus the delete button on one line, `.form-row`'s own `flex-wrap: wrap` silently wrapped the row's tail (almost always just the prep_note field + the ✕ button) onto its own line. That orphaned fragment then read as a whole separate, malformed ingredient -- exactly what the screenshot showed and what the author reasonably read as broken parsing. Fixed by giving each ingredient field an explicit className (`ingredient-name-input`/`ingredient-qty-input`/`ingredient-unit-input`/`ingredient-prep-input`) and a real flex-basis/min-width in `theme.css`, plus `flex-wrap: nowrap` on `.ingredient-row` above the mobile breakpoint (reset back to the normal stacked-column behavior below it, where `.form-row`'s existing mobile override already takes over). This is also the direct fix for the "excessively small field sizes" complaint -- fields now compress to fit the available width instead of wrapping mid-ingredient into illegible fragments.

2. **A handful of genuinely wrong ingredient values, confirmed against the ground-truth PDF text.** The model (a) invented a wrong unit conversion for the crust's sugar (source says "2 Tbsp.", parsed as quantity 0.5 / unit "cup" -- 2 Tbsp. is actually 0.125 cup, so this isn't even an internally-consistent conversion, just a hallucination); (b) bled the "divided" modifier from the *filling's* sugar ("¾ cup plus 2 Tbsp. sugar, **divided**") onto the *crust's* sugar, which has no such modifier in the source -- the two same-named ingredients in different sections got conflated; and (c) dropped quantity/unit entirely for the kosher salt and the gelatin, both of which do have stated amounts in the source (¼ tsp.; 1 envelope). Added explicit anti-hallucination instructions to `recipe_service.RECIPE_IMPORT_PROMPT`'s ingredients bullet: copy quantity/unit exactly as written, never convert between units, never guess a quantity/unit that isn't stated (leave both null instead), and when the same ingredient name appears more than once for different parts of a recipe, extract each occurrence as its own separate entry using only that occurrence's own stated amount/modifiers -- never merging one occurrence's modifier onto another. **This is a prompt-engineering mitigation, not a guaranteed fix** -- there is no Ollama instance reachable from this sandbox to re-run the actual extraction and confirm the model now gets this specific recipe right; the author should re-attempt this same PDF import after pulling the change and report back whether the sugar/salt/gelatin lines come out correctly.

3. **Placeholder/value legibility.** With ingredient rows frequently under-populated by the parser (per above), most of the visible fields in the screenshot were showing their *empty-state placeholder hint* ("qty", "unit", "prep note"), not real parsed values -- and with no explicit `::placeholder` rule anywhere in `theme.css`, the browser's own default placeholder rendering was the only thing distinguishing "empty, showing a hint" from "filled in," which reads as one undifferentiated wash of grey text on a form that's already partially empty. Added an explicit `::placeholder` rule (muted color, italic) to `.item-form`/`.settings-row` inputs and textareas so hint text reads as an unmistakable "needs review" cue rather than blending into real content, and made the input `background` explicit (`var(--color-surface)`) alongside the already-present `color: var(--color-text)` so a filled value's contrast doesn't depend on an unstyled/inherited background in some browser. Flagged honestly: if the author still sees genuinely low-contrast text in *filled* fields after this change (as opposed to placeholder hints), that would point to something outside this codebase's CSS -- e.g. a browser or OS-level forced-dark-mode override repainting page colors -- worth a follow-up report with which browser/OS is in use.

**Verification.** Ground-truthed the parsing complaint against the actual source PDF's extracted text (both `pypdf` and `pdfplumber`, see above) rather than guessing at what went wrong. `npm run build` run from a `/tmp` scratch copy (per the standing FUSE-mount note) confirms all 306 modules transform cleanly with the new `RecipeForm.jsx` classNames and `theme.css` rules. **Not verified**: actual on-screen rendering (no browser available from this sandbox) and whether the hardened prompt actually changes this specific recipe's extracted ingredients (no reachable Ollama instance) -- both worth the author's hands-on re-test.

## B10.4 notes: responsive mobile layout, first pass (2026-07-31)

Next item per the sequence agreed with the author, picked up right after B1.2/B1.3 and the BACKEND_PORT fix. Scoped to exactly what the backlog text named -- nav, every form, the meal-plan weekly grid, the chat widget, and the inventory list -- via one `@media (max-width: 640px)` block appended to `theme.css`, per the project brief's one-source-CSS mandate (no component library, no second stylesheet).

**A real bug found before the responsive work could even start.** `.form-row`'s flex/gap rule was scoped `.item-form .form-row` -- it only ever applied inside the two forms that happen to wrap themselves in an `.item-form`-classed `<form>` (`RecipeForm`, `InventoryItemForm`, and anything built on top of them like `HealthPage`'s member form). Every other `.form-row` in the app -- `MealPlanPage`'s generate form and preview rows, `ChatWidget`'s and `RecipeChat`'s input rows, `KnowledgeFilesPanel`'s upload row, `GroceryListPanel`'s add-item row, `RecipeDetailPage`'s servings/rating/staple row -- rendered with no flex layout at all. This mostly went unnoticed because `<input>`/`<select>`/`<button>` are inline-block by default, so they still sat roughly in a row via normal inline flow -- but with no `gap`, no wrapping control, and no way to cleanly collapse to a single mobile column. Generalized the rule to plain `.form-row`/`.form-row label` (dropping the `.item-form` requirement) -- checked all 10 files using the class first to confirm every usage is a short label/input/button row that only stands to gain from real flex+gap, not a case that was deliberately relying on block stacking. This is additive only: nothing that already had `.item-form` loses anything, and everything else gains the layout its own naming (`form-row`) already implied it should have had.

**Mobile breakpoint contents**, all under the one `@media (max-width: 640px)` block:

- `.form-row` (now universal, see above) switches to `flex-direction: column` -- covers every form in the app in one rule rather than one per page.
- `.data-table` (used only by the inventory list today) converts to a stacked-card layout with no JS: `thead` hidden, `tr`/`td` switched to `display: block`, and each `<td>` in `InventoryPage.jsx` now carries a `data-label` attribute (`Name`/`Category`/`Qty`/`Expires`/`Priority`/`Why it matters`/`Actions`) that a `td[data-label]::before { content: attr(data-label) ": " }` rule turns into a visible label -- the classic CSS-only responsive-table pattern, chosen over hiding columns since every column here is meaningful (expiration/priority in particular are the whole point of the urgency-scoring feature from Phase 3). The colSpan-7 inline-edit row (no `data-label`) is excluded from the label-prefix rule and keeps normal padding.
- `.meal-entry-row` (the meal-plan weekly grid -- turned out to already be a flex-wrap list of rows, not the literal 7-column `<table>` the backlog text assumed; verified by reading `MealPlanPage.jsx`/`MealPlanEntryRow.jsx` before writing any CSS, since the backlog description was inferred, not re-checked against current code) stacks its children full-width instead of wrapping several ~150px-min-width fields into an uneven multi-line jumble.
- `.chat-widget`/`.chat-widget-panel` become a full-screen overlay (inset-0, no border-radius) instead of a fixed 360px-wide docked panel that would otherwise cover most of a phone screen while still leaving unusable margins -- directly serves the "chat while cooking" use case, which is specifically a phone-in-the-kitchen scenario.
- `.app-nav` becomes horizontally scrollable (`overflow-x: auto`, `flex-wrap: nowrap`) rather than wrapping to a second line or overflowing the viewport.
- `.btn`/`.btn-link` get a modest touch-target size bump (min-height on `.btn`).

**Deliberately not touched in this pass:** Home/Health/Settings pages -- they lay out using the same shared primitives (`.card`, `.form-row`, `.btn`, `.data-table` isn't used there) that this pass already fixed, so nothing page-specific was needed; if real usage surfaces something page-specific later, it belongs in its own follow-up, not assumed here. The B7.1-B7.3 cluster (cook mode, timers, PWA installability) this item was flagged as a prerequisite for is still open -- B10.4 unblocks it, it doesn't include it.

**Verification, and its real limit.** `npm run build` succeeds cleanly (built from a `/tmp` scratch copy per the standing FUSE-mount note); grepped the built, minified CSS to confirm the `@media (max-width: 640px)` block and the `data-label` attribute selector both survived minification intact, and the built JS bundle to confirm nothing broke `InventoryPage.jsx`'s transform; a live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass confirmed `InventoryPage.jsx` and `theme.css` both transform without error and the inventory endpoint still round-trips through the proxy. **What this verification does NOT cover, and should be treated as open**: nothing here was checked in an actual browser at a narrow viewport width -- no screenshot, no devtools device emulation, no real phone. The CSS was written and reasoned through carefully (checked every `.form-row` usage site's actual children before generalizing the rule, checked the meal-plan grid's real markup before writing its override instead of assuming the backlog text's table description), but "the build succeeds and the selectors survived minification" is a much weaker claim than "this looks right on a phone." Treat this as a solid, reasoned first draft that should be eyeballed on a real device (or with browser dev tools) before being considered done.

## B10.6 notes: print stylesheet (2026-07-31)

Picked up as the next item after B10.4 -- lowest-effort remaining backlog item with zero unresolved dependencies (unlike B10.5, which needs a density-sourcing design first) and directly author-facing.

**`@media print` in `theme.css`, plus one new utility class.** `.no-print` is a plain class applied directly in JSX wherever a specific piece of app chrome shouldn't appear on paper -- `RecipeDetailPage.jsx`'s "All recipes" back link, the servings/rating/staple controls row, and the edit/print/delete button row. The app-wide chrome (`.app-header` -- title, subtitle, nav) and the recipe-scoped "Ask the Chef" widget (`.recipe-chat`) are hidden unconditionally rather than needing their own `.no-print` tags, since nothing in this app has a use case for printing them from any page, not just the recipe detail page. Also sets `.app-shell { max-width: none }` (the 960px cap exists for on-screen line-length readability, not for a printed page) and renders links in black instead of the screen-only-legible primary-orange brand color.

**Deliberately not done:** stripping card borders/background colors for a "cleaner" printed look -- browsers' own print dialogs already expose a "background graphics" toggle for that, and didn't seem worth forcing given this app's palette isn't heavy on ink. Respecting B10.5's unit-system toggle (Imperial/Metric/Weight) on print, since B10.5 itself doesn't exist yet -- whoever builds it should make sure the print stylesheet picks up whatever's currently selected, same as it already does for the servings scale (which prints correctly today since it's just the already-rendered `recipe.ingredients` list, no separate print-time re-fetch).

**New affordance:** a "Print recipe" button next to Edit/Delete calling `window.print()` -- the simplest possible trigger. It doesn't appear in its own output since its containing button row already carries `.no-print`.

**Verification:** built the frontend from a `/tmp` scratch copy (per the standing FUSE-mount note) and confirmed `npm run build` succeeds cleanly and the `@media print` block survives minification in the built CSS; a live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass confirmed `RecipeDetailPage.jsx` still transforms without error and the recipe endpoint still round-trips through the proxy. **Not verified**: actual print output was not visually inspected (no browser available to trigger a real print preview) -- same caveat as B10.4's responsive pass, this is a reasoned-through but visually-unconfirmed first cut. Worth a real print-preview check (Ctrl/Cmd+P on a recipe page) before considering this fully done.

## B3.1/B3.2 notes: allergen/restriction model, deterministic check, cross-contact flagging (2026-07-31)

Picked by the author over B1.4/Walmart-import/other options as the next area to tackle, flagged in the backlog as a genuine safety gap (not a nicety) given the stated celiac-friendly requirement. Dietary restrictions were previously free-text JSON (`HouseholdPreferences.dietary_restrictions`) interpreted only by the LLM per prompt -- nothing structurally prevented a generated plan or imported recipe from actually containing gluten if the model missed it. This adds a second, independent, deterministic layer that never trusts the model's own judgment about what it put in a recipe.

**New structured field, kept separate from the existing free-text one.** `HouseholdPreferences.restricted_allergens` (JSON list of fixed enum keys) and `.gluten_observance_level` (nullable string enum) are new columns (migration `c3d8a1f5b7e2`), deliberately not folded into the existing `dietary_restrictions` list -- that field is LLM-interpreted prose ("low sodium", "reduce cholesterol") and conflating it with a field that drives a hard code-level safety check would blur what each one actually guarantees. Restrictions stay household-level, matching every other preference in the data model; there's no per-member dietary concept anywhere in the schema today, and introducing one wasn't asked for.

**`app/services/allergen_service.py` -- the deterministic check itself.** `ALLERGEN_CHOICES` is a 10-entry taxonomy: the nine FDA/FALCPA-and-FASTER-Act major allergens (milk, eggs, fish, crustacean shellfish, tree nuts, peanuts, wheat, soybeans, sesame) plus "gluten" as its own tenth entry, since gluten (wheat + barley + rye + related grains) is broader than "wheat" alone and is the actually-relevant category for celiac disease. Each allergen maps to a keyword list (`ALLERGEN_KEYWORDS`); `find_allergen_matches()` regex-matches every keyword against every ingredient name with `\b` word-boundary anchoring (so "eggplant" doesn't false-positive on "egg") and returns every hit, not just the first, via `finditer` (a real bug caught in testing: "barley malt syrup" checked against "gluten" only reported "barley" with a naive `.search()`, missing "malt"). A `"-free"` negation pass (`_is_negated`) suppresses a match when the ingredient name itself declares the allergen absent ("gluten-free flour", "dairy-free wheat cracker" only flags wheat) -- the negation regex needed its own `\b` fix after a subtle bug where "nut-free" matched as a bare substring inside "peanut-free", incorrectly suppressing an unrelated, legitimate tree_nuts hit on "almond" in the same ingredient string.

**B3.2's cross-contact layer** is `find_cross_contact_matches()`: only fires when gluten is actually restricted AND the household's `gluten_observance_level` is `strict_no_cross_contact` (the other two levels -- `flexible`, `strict_gluten_only` -- don't care about cross-contact by definition), and matches a small `GLUTEN_CROSS_CONTACT_KEYWORDS` list (oats/oat/oatmeal/oat flour/oat milk/oat bran -- the single most common real-world cross-contact risk for celiac diets, since oats aren't gluten-containing but are very commonly processed on shared wheat/barley/rye equipment). Cross-contact matches never set `RestrictionCheckResult.has_conflict` on their own -- they're informational, not blocking, since "may share equipment" is a much softer signal than "contains the allergen."

**Wired into all three points the backlog named.** Import (`RecipeImportResponse.restriction_warnings`/`.cross_contact_warnings`, computed against the parsed ingredients before the recipe is ever saved), every recipe read (`RecipeRead` gains the same two fields, computed fresh on every `GET` rather than cached/persisted -- unlike nutrition resolution, this is a cheap string-match with no network call, and restrictions can change at any time, so a stale cached warning would be actively misleading), meal-plan generation preview (`meal_plan_service.attach_restriction_warnings()` runs against either the AI-proposed new recipe's ingredients or the matched catalog recipe's, informational only since nothing is saved yet), and meal-plan entry confirm (`POST /meal-plans/{plan_id}/entries/{entry_id}/confirm` now checks the actual recipe's ingredients against current household restrictions -- a plan can sit for days before a meal is actually made, during which restrictions could change, so generation-time warnings alone aren't enough). Confirm has real teeth: a hard allergen match returns **409** with the match details unless the request body sets `acknowledge_restriction_conflict: true`, at which point it proceeds and deducts inventory as normal. This is a deliberate, discussed trade-off -- block by default (a silent confirm on a conflicting meal is the failure mode that actually matters for a celiac household) while still leaving a human able to say "I know, do it anyway" for a false positive or an accepted one-off exception. Cross-contact warnings never block confirm on their own, matching their informational-only status everywhere else.

**Frontend.** `RestrictionWarnings.jsx` is a small shared component (red `.restriction-warning` banner for hard matches, amber `.cross-contact-warning` for the softer oats-type signal, matching the existing provenance-badge severity convention) reused in four places: `RecipeDetailPage.jsx` (below the description), `RecipesPage.jsx`'s import-review card (new `importWarnings` state alongside `importPreview`, since `RecipeImportResponse` carries the warnings at the top level, not nested under `.recipe`), `MealPlanPage.jsx`'s generation-preview entries (one banner per previewed slot), and `MealPlanEntryRow.jsx`'s confirm flow.
