Solved with a shared `chef-tls` Docker volume (backend writes, frontend reads read-only) and two independently-restarting processes watching the same cert files: the backend's `run_server.py` launcher (ported from Fiduciary, `os.execv` self-restart so Docker's restart policy never sees an exit) and a rewritten `frontend/docker-entrypoint.sh` supervisor loop (polls the cert file's mtime every 5s, restarts the `serve` child with `--ssl-cert`/`--ssl-key` flags when it changes) -- no reverse proxy added, since `frontend/src/api.js`'s `backendOrigin` already derives its protocol from `window.location.protocol`, so once the frontend page is HTTPS its own API calls follow automatically. New backend: `tls_service.py` (self-signed generation, CSR generate/import, `cryptography`-only, no new dependency), `routers/tls.py` (`/api/tls/status`/`self-signed`/`csr`/`import-cert`/`clear`/`restart`), new `BACKEND_HTTPS_PORT`/`FRONTEND_HTTPS_PORT` env vars (default 8446/5174). New frontend: a full "Certificate (HTTPS)" card on Settings > Security (status display, self-signed generate form pre-filled with the browser's own LAN address, an "Advanced" collapsible CSR/import flow, a clear-certificate button), and a new WIKI "HTTPS / secure context" entry walking through the single most important, easy-to-miss gotcha: a self-signed cert's browser trust warning has to be clicked through at BOTH the frontend's and backend's HTTPS addresses separately (they're different origins/ports), or the app looks silently broken (stuck "Loading...") because the background API fetch to the untrusted backend origin fails invisibly. Live-validated the `serve` npm package's `--ssl-cert`/`--ssl-key` flags with a real openssl-generated cert before writing any entrypoint-script code around the assumption, and separately ran the real (unmodified) `docker-entrypoint.sh` against a locally-installed `serve` binary through a full HTTP -> cert-appears -> HTTPS state transition, confirming the config.js backend-port rewrite and the correct listen flags at each stage. A same-session sandbox quirk worth recording for future debugging: `pkill -f`/`pgrep -f` patterns can match their OWN invoking command's argv text and self-terminate the calling shell -- cost several wasted tool calls before being identified; avoid `-f` pattern matching for cleanup in this sandbox, prefer `fuser -k PORT/tcp` or exact PIDs.

**B4.3 -- FoodKeeper shelf-life auto-suggestion.** Verified USDA FSIS's FoodKeeper dataset live before building against it: confirmed CC0/public-domain via data.gov's own catalog metadata, official JSON URL `https://www.fsis.usda.gov/shared/data/EN/foodkeeper.json`. Hit a genuine sandbox constraint -- this environment has no network route to any `.gov` domain (confirmed via both the web-fetch tool and direct `curl`; `github.com`/`npmjs.org`/`pypi.org` all work fine) -- and rather than substituting a stale third-party mirror without saying so, asked the author how to proceed (`AskUserQuestion`); the author's answer was to try the connected Chrome browser instead, which DOES reach `fsis.usda.gov`. Fetched and condensed the live file through that browser session (a `general-purpose` subagent handled the mechanical chunked-extraction-and-reassembly, since the browser-automation tool truncates any single returned string to ~950 characters, requiring the 64KB dataset to be pulled in ~80 chunks and verified byte-for-byte before saving). One honesty-relevant finding surfaced along the way: the dataset's own "Version" sheet shows version 128 (2018-09-06) as the current/latest entry -- so despite data.gov's catalog page showing "Dataset Last Updated: 2025-01-22," the actual FoodKeeper CONTENT hasn't changed since 2018; that 2025 date is a metadata re-harvest timestamp, not a content refresh. This is genuinely the current official file, just itself several years stale at the source -- documented plainly in `foodkeeper_service.py`'s module docstring rather than left for a future session to wonder about.

Shipped as `backend/app/data/foodkeeper_shelf_life.csv` (661 rows, pipe-delimited, condensed from FSIS's Product/Category sheets down to name/keywords/category plus Pantry/Fridge/Freezer/AfterOpening day-count ranges -- FoodKeeper's own Min/Max/Metric triples pre-converted to integer days at build time). New `foodkeeper_service.py`: a cached CSV parse, a scored fuzzy name-matcher (exact name > exact keyword > substring-of-name scaled by length > substring-of-a-sufficiently-specific-keyword), and `suggest_expiration_date()` anchoring on `days_min` (the sooner/more conservative end of the range) from `InventoryItem.purchased_date`. One interpretation choice documented in the module docstring: FoodKeeper publishes both a plain range and a `DOP_` ("date of purchase") range per storage location; since Chef only ever has a purchase date to anchor to, the CSV build preferred the DOP_ variant when populated (many common items, e.g. Butter, are ONLY populated on that side) and fell back to the plain variant otherwise. New `GET /api/inventory/shelf-life-suggestion?name=&category=&purchased_date=` endpoint (plain sync `def`, pure in-memory lookup, no DB access, no job-queue treatment needed). Frontend: `InventoryItemForm.jsx` now debounce-queries this endpoint as the household types an item name (only when the expiration field is still blank), showing an "Estimated (N-M days, USDA FoodKeeper match: X)" hint with an explicit "Use [date]" button rather than silently auto-filling the field -- same transparency pattern as Settings' redirect-URI suggestion buttons.

A real scoring bug was caught and fixed via failing tests during this pass, not assumed away: FSIS's own Keywords column splits compound words into their parts for FSIS's own app search (e.g. "Surimi seafood"'s keywords include "sea" AND "food" as separate entries), which meant a naive length-based keyword filter let a query like "leftover food item" falsely match Surimi seafood purely off the word "food." Fixed with a small stoplist of overly generic single-word keywords (`food`, `item`, `fresh`, `sea`, etc.) excluded from matching regardless of length, plus rescaling the substring-of-name score by the matched name's length so a short generic name (e.g. "Butter") can't outrank a longer, more specific one ("Buttermilk") on a tied substring hit.

**Verified**: 453 total backend tests passing (16 new for `foodkeeper_service`/the router endpoint, run in the sandbox's balanced ~45s-timeout-respecting batches, covering CSV parsing/caching, exact/keyword/substring matching, the no-confident-match floor, category-to-storage-location mapping including the "no FoodKeeper mapping for spice/other" case, date-anchoring math, and the router's blank-name/unparseable-date/unmatched-item branches), plus the pre-existing 18 `test_tls_service.py` tests re-confirmed clean and a new `test_zzz_main_import_check.py` smoke test asserting `/api/tls/status` registers on app startup. A real functional dry run of the (unmodified) `docker-entrypoint.sh` supervisor loop against a locally-installed `serve` binary confirmed the full HTTP -> HTTPS state transition end to end (described in B15.1's own paragraph above). Clean frontend build (`vite build` succeeds; a FUSE `dist/`-cleanup `EPERM` was worked around the same way as prior sessions, building to a scratch `--outDir` instead of the tracked `dist/`). **Not verified, and cannot be from this sandbox**: actual on-screen behavior of the new Certificate card, the browser trust-warning click-through flow, or the shelf-life suggestion's live typing-triggered UI, on a real device -- same standing limitation as every UI-only change in this project. The FoodKeeper CSV's fuzzy matcher was exercised against real (not synthetic) data via its own test suite, but hasn't been checked against the author's actual real-world inventory item names yet.

## Bug fix: Dining Out "Use my location" stuck on "Locating..." (2026-08-02)

Author-reported from a real iPad, after B15.1's HTTPS work: tapping "Use my location" under Dining Out never resolved, never errored, just sat on "Locating..." forever. Root cause, found by reading `DiningPage.jsx`'s `useMyLocation()` rather than assuming it was still the HTTPS/Secure-Context gate B15.1 already fixed: `navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition()` was called with no `options` argument, so its default `timeout` is `Infinity`. A WiFi-only iPad has no GPS chip and relies entirely on WiFi-based positioning -- if that fix never resolves (weak/unfamiliar network, indoors, Location Services still warming up), NEITHER the success nor the error callback ever fires, and the button is stuck with no way out short of reloading the page.

Two independent fixes, both in `frontend/src/pages/DiningPage.jsx`: (1) an explicit `{ enableHighAccuracy: false, timeout: 15000, maximumAge: 60000 }` options object, so the browser's own API gives up and calls the error callback instead of hanging forever; (2) a belt-and-suspenders 20-second JS-side fallback timer, since a small number of WebKit versions have shipped with geolocation callbacks that don't reliably fire at all in some states (Low Power Mode, a backgrounded tab during the permission prompt) -- if the browser's own timeout doesn't save us, this one still will. Also replaced the always-blames-HTTPS error message with one that reads the actual `GeolocationPositionError.code` (`PERMISSION_DENIED`/`POSITION_UNAVAILABLE`/`TIMEOUT`) and only mentions HTTPS when the page genuinely isn't in a secure context (`window.location.protocol !== "https:"`) -- HTTPS is no longer the likely culprit now that B15.1 shipped, and the old message was actively misleading for this exact bug.

**Verified**: a standalone Node script exercising `parseStepDuration`/`annotateTemperatures`-adjacent logic isn't relevant here, but the fix itself was verified via a clean `vite build` (302 -> no module-count change, this file already existed) with no transform errors; the underlying browser API behavior (timeout firing the error callback) is per the MDN-documented `PositionOptions` contract, not independently re-verified against a live device from this sandbox (no browser available, standing limitation).

## B7.1/B7.2/B7.3 notes: cook mode, step timers, PWA packaging (2026-08-02)

Picked up together per the author's prioritized list ("1, 3, 4, 2" -- cook-mode/PWA cluster first) after the geolocation bug fix above. All three are frontend-only; no backend/schema changes.

**B7.1 -- Cook mode** (`frontend/src/components/CookMode.jsx`, wired into `RecipeDetailPage.jsx` via a new "Cook mode" button): a plain full-screen overlay `<div>` toggled by React state, not a new route -- this app uses `HashRouter` with no server-side history-fallback need, and a route change would also make the browser back button exit cook mode by accident mid-recipe. One step at a time, large type, Prev/Next (Next also marks the current step done), a per-step "Done" checkbox, a collapsible ingredients panel and a "jump to step" list so the user never has to leave cook mode to check a quantity or skip around, and progress (current step + checked steps) persisted to `localStorage` keyed by recipe id so an accidental navigate-away mid-recipe doesn't lose your place -- same "don't lose in-progress state" discipline as B11.1's job-queue resume, just for a client-only concern. Screen Wake Lock API: acquired automatically on entering cook mode (a button click is a valid user-gesture context), a visible toggle checkbox to turn it off (with an explicit battery-impact hint, per the backlog's own ask), and re-acquired on `visibilitychange` if the browser auto-released it while the toggle is still on and cook mode is still open, per the API's own documented usage pattern. Feature-detected (`"wakeLock" in navigator`) with a plain hint shown on unsupported browsers rather than a broken control.

**Inline temperature conversion**: `frontend/src/utils/cookingText.js`'s `annotateTemperatures()` finds every degree-marked F/C mention in a step's text (`350°F`, `175 degrees C`) and appends the converted value in parentheses -- deliberately requires an explicit `°` or the word "degree(s)" before the letter, NOT a bare `\d+\s*[FC]\b`, since the latter would false-positive on things like "2 C water" (cup, not Celsius) in loosely-written instruction text.

**B7.2 -- Step-linked timers** (`frontend/src/hooks/useStepTimers.js`, `frontend/src/components/TimersBadge.jsx`, `frontend/src/utils/cookingText.js`'s `parseStepDuration()`): a step's parsed duration (regex-based -- handles "25-30 minutes" by anchoring on the lower bound, same "sooner, more conservative end" convention `foodkeeper_service.py` uses server-side, plus a combined "1 hour 15 minutes" form) surfaces a "Start timer" button in cook mode. Timer state lives in `localStorage` plus a tiny module-level pub/sub, NOT a server job (this is a pure client-side countdown, unlike the B11.1 job queue) -- remaining time is always computed from a stored wall-clock `startedAt`, never decremented in place, so it's correct immediately after a reload/tab-switch instead of drifting. `TimersBadge.jsx` is mounted in `App.jsx` outside `<Routes>` (same placement as `JobsBadge`/`ChatWidget`), so a timer started in cook mode stays visible while browsing elsewhere in the app -- the backlog text's own "background-persistent way the chat widget already does" requirement. On completion: a three-beep Web Audio chime (best-effort -- some browsers require a user-gesture call stack for audio, which a timer firing on its own isn't, so this can legitimately no-op) plus a browser `Notification` if permission was granted (requested lazily on the first "start timer" tap, not on page load).

**B7.3 -- PWA packaging**: `frontend/public/manifest.webmanifest` (name, icons, `display: standalone`, fixed `theme_color`/`background_color` -- a manifest can't read this app's dynamic `--color-primary` CSS variable, so both are pinned to the default theme's colors, a documented simplification) and `frontend/public/sw.js`, registered from `main.jsx` guarded by `"serviceWorker" in navigator` (a no-op on plain-HTTP LAN deployments until B15.1's certificate is set up -- the same secure-context gate camera/geolocation already hit, not a new limitation). Deliberately modest service-worker scope: network-first with a cache fallback for the app shell (HTML/JS/CSS/icons), and API calls (`/api/*`) are explicitly never cached -- inventory/recipes/meal-plan/chat all need live data every time, and a stale cached response would be actively misleading, not just a minor staleness nicety. This buys "the installed app shell still loads on bad kitchen wifi instead of a browser error page," not real offline CRUD/sync, which would be a much bigger feature than the backlog text asked for. Icons (`frontend/public/icons/`): a simple chef-hat silhouette generated programmatically (PIL, no external asset/license needed) in the app's brand color, at 192/512/512-maskable/180 (Apple touch icon, opaque per Apple's own no-transparency requirement) sizes.

**Verified**: `parseStepDuration`/`annotateTemperatures` exercised directly via a standalone Node script (6 duration cases including the false-positive-avoidance "2 C water" case, 3 temperature cases) before wiring into the UI. `manifest.webmanifest` confirmed valid JSON via `json.load`; a full `vite build` confirmed all new files (icons, manifest, sw.js, config.js) land correctly under `dist/` (Vite copies `public/` verbatim) and the build stays clean (306 modules, up from 302, across all three items). **Not verified**: on-screen appearance of cook mode/timers/the install prompt, the Wake Lock API's actual screen-on behavior, or the service worker's real offline fallback behavior on a real device -- no browser available in this sandbox, the same standing limitation as every other UI-only change in this project.

## B5.2 notes: prep-day / batch-cooking generation mode (2026-08-02)

**Scoping decision, made explicit before writing any code**: a literal version of Mealime Pro's "Meal Prep Day" (batch-cook shared components, mix-and-match them into several later meals) would need this app to model recipes as composed of reusable sub-components with their own inventory/quantity tracking -- a much bigger schema change than this backlog item asked for, and one nothing else in the app currently needs. Shipped the same underlying OUTCOME (fewer independent stove-heavy cooking sessions across the week) as a generation-PROMPT heuristic instead: a new optional `prep_day` field (0-6) on `MealPlanGenerateRequest` instructs the model, when set, to batch-cook 2-3 reusable base components (a grain, a protein, a roasted vegetable) on the chosen day in larger-than-needed quantities, then prefer fast-assembly recipes on other days that explicitly reuse them -- tagging the prep-day recipe(s) `make_ahead` and the reuse meals `quick` (both pre-existing seeded tags, no new taxonomy needed), and noting in a reuse meal's ingredient `prep_note` that a component is "already cooked from {day}" rather than listing it as a fresh purchase.

The persisted result is still ordinary `Recipe`/`MealPlanEntry` rows -- no new linking concept, so grocery-list and inventory-deduction logic needed zero changes and stays exactly as accurate (or inaccurate) as it already was for any other week. What this does NOT do, stated plainly in `meal_plan_service._format_prep_day_section`'s own docstring: verify the model actually produced fewer distinct cooking sessions, or enforce that a "reuses prep day" tag/note is truthful -- same "prompted, not enforced" honesty limit every other generation-steering feature in this prompt already has (dietary pattern, indulgence frequency, priority ingredients).

**Changed**: `schemas/meal_plan.py` (`MealPlanGenerateRequest.prep_day`), `services/meal_plan_service.py` (`gather_generation_context`'s new `prep_day` param, `_format_prep_day_section`, wired into `build_generation_prompt`'s template), `routers/meal_plan.py` (passes `payload.prep_day` through), and a new "Prep day / batch-cooking mode" dropdown on `MealPlanPage.jsx`'s generate form (off by default).

**Verified**: 10 new unit tests (`test_meal_plan_prep_day.py`) asserting the instruction text reaches the literal generated prompt string for every day value, not just an intermediate context dict -- same discipline the B2.3 dietary-pattern tests established. Full backend suite (473 tests) re-run in batches, all green. Clean frontend build. **Not verified**: on-screen appearance of the new dropdown, or real model adherence to the instruction (no live Ollama reaches this sandbox).

## B8.1 notes: bloodwork CSV/PDF/photo import (2026-08-02)

Same architecture as every other unstructured-document import in this app (recipe text/PDF/photo, inventory vision-intake, receipt OCR): extract text (or hand a photo straight to the vision model), ask Ollama for strict JSON, defensively parse whatever comes back, return a PREVIEW, let the user review/edit/assign-to-a-member, then confirm each accepted row through the EXISTING `POST /api/health/metrics` endpoint -- so BMI computation and validation stay on the one real code path instead of being duplicated. Extended past the backlog text's own "CSV/PDF" framing to also accept a photo of a printed report (the vision-intake pattern was already sitting right there) and pasted text, since a phone photo of a lab report is plausibly the single most likely real-world capture path for many users, more so than either a CSV export or a scanned PDF.

**Deliberately NOT a per-lab column-mapping profile system** the way B10.3's order-history importer is: a grocery receipt's column layout is far more consistent within one retailer than lab-report WORDING is even within one lab (LDL alone shows up as "LDL", "LDL Cholesterol", "LDL Cholesterol Calc", "LDL-C", etc.) -- free-text LLM extraction is the better-fitting tool here, the same conclusion recipe/receipt import already reached for their own free-text sources. Unit conversion (lbs->kg, mmol/L->mg/dL) is asked of the model directly in the prompt rather than attempted as a separate deterministic pass afterward, since which unit a report used has to be read from the same unstructured text being parsed anyway -- a weaker guarantee than the deterministic `unit_conversion_service` used elsewhere (B5.3/B10.5), flagged plainly in both the prompt and the import panel's own copy, not silently presented as exact.

**Backend**: `health_service.py` gained `BLOODWORK_IMPORT_PROMPT`, `extract_bloodwork_text()` (PDF via the existing `recipe_service.extract_pdf_text`, else plain UTF-8 decode for CSV/text), `parse_bloodwork_response()` (defensive JSON extraction, drops entries with zero actual metric values -- a common real-world model failure mode is a well-formed but entirely-null object), and `run_bloodwork_extraction()`. New `POST /api/health/import` (`routers/health.py`, multipart `text` or `file`) enqueues through the shared job queue (B11.1) like every other AI-consuming endpoint -- the Ollama call, and for a PDF the synchronous `pypdf` extraction, would otherwise block the whole app's event loop for the call's full duration, the exact bug class B11.1 fixed everywhere else; this is the first NEW AI-consuming endpoint added since B11.1 landed, so it went through the job queue from day one rather than needing its own follow-up conversion. New `HealthBloodworkEntryPreview`/`HealthBloodworkImportResponse` schemas (`schemas/health.py`) with every field nullable/unvalidated on purpose, since this is a preview for review, not a write.

**Frontend**: `HealthPage.jsx` gained a collapsible "Import bloodwork" panel (file upload + text paste, `useBackgroundJob`-driven) under the manual entry form, showing an editable preview table (member picker, date, and every metric field per row) with per-row "Add"/"Discard" and a bulk "Add all to log" action -- each confirm POSTs to the same `/health/metrics` endpoint the manual form already uses.

**Verified**: 14 new unit tests (`test_health_bloodwork_import.py`) covering text/CSV/PDF-dispatch extraction, strict/markdown-wrapped JSON parsing, multiple-draws-in-one-file parsing, dropping empty entries while keeping blood-pressure-only entries, garbage input, and three date-format variants. A live end-to-end pass against a freshly migrated+seeded local uvicorn instance: confirmed a 400 with no `text`/`file`, confirmed the job enqueues and runs, and confirmed it fails cleanly with a real, readable error ("Connection blocked by network allowlist" -- this sandbox's own network restriction, not a crash) rather than taking the server down, matching this project's established "the unhappy path is what's actually exercisable here" pattern for every Ollama-dependent feature. Full backend suite (473 tests) re-run in batches, all green. Clean frontend build. **Not verified**: on-screen appearance of the import panel, or real extraction accuracy against an actual lab report (no live Ollama reaches this sandbox).

## Phase 10 notes: formal Testing & QA pass (2026-08-02)

What "manual QA pass against the full feature checklist" means in a sandbox with no browser: a code-level audit against the project brief's own bullet list (the "Some features of the app include" section at the top of this file's source instructions), not on-screen clicking -- the same honest limitation every other UI-only change in this project has already been logging individually. Walked every core brief item (main chef system prompt, dietary onboarding prompt, body-characteristic-aware planning, trend tracking, knowledge-file grounding, persistent background chat, inventory categories/quantity/expiration, manual inventory edit, vision intake, confirm-deducts-inventory, chat-driven inventory management, kitchen-equipment awareness, full recipe fields, servings scaling, staple/rating, multi-format recipe import, priority-ingredient boosting, expiring/stale-ingredient bias, per-meal guiding tags, Docker packaging, Settings GUI, rebuild-persistent storage, centralized CSS theming) against the current codebase and confirmed each has a real, working implementation -- no gaps found in the original brief's core feature set. (The backlog groups B1-B15 above are all POST-brief enhancements layered on top of that core, tracked and scoped separately.)

**New, genuinely additive test coverage** rather than just re-confirming what individual backlog items already tested in isolation: `tests/test_end_to_end_smoke.py`, a single test that calls FIVE router functions in realistic sequence against one shared `db_session` -- set household preferences, add a household member, add an inventory item, create a recipe using that ingredient, build a meal plan entry referencing it, confirm the entry, and assert the inventory quantity actually dropped by the recipe's exact ingredient amount, then log a health metric and assert the server-computed BMI is correct. Every other test file in this suite (by established, documented convention -- see `test_barcode_lookup.py`'s and `test_recipe_folder_import_confirm.py`'s own comments) exercises one router/service in isolation; nothing before this was a committed, repeatable check that the cross-router flow a household actually lives day to day -- inventory -> recipe -> plan -> confirm -> deduction -- keeps working together as the codebase grows. Also extended the existing `test_zzz_main_import_check.py` route-registration smoke test to assert the new B8.1 `/api/health/import` route registers.

**Full suite state**: 473 backend tests passing (up from 458 at the start of this session), run in the sandbox's balanced ~45s-timeout-respecting batches per its own established pattern (a single unbatched `pytest -q` run exceeds this session's available continuous runtime, same reasoning as the B4.3/B15.1 session's own note on this). Clean `vite build` (306 modules) confirmed after every frontend change this session, most recently as a final end-to-end pass covering all of B7.1-B7.3/B5.2/B8.1/the geolocation fix together. **Not done, and cannot be from this sandbox**: any actual on-screen QA (clicking through cook mode, the PWA install prompt, the bloodwork import panel, the prep-day dropdown, or re-testing the geolocation fix on a real iPad) -- the standing limitation on every UI-facing item in this project's history, not new to this session. Worth the author's own hands-on pass, especially the geolocation fix (the original bug report) and cook mode's Wake Lock behavior on the actual iPad this session was triggered by.

## B7.4 notes: accessibility pass, and the Walmart-import/geocoding round it shipped alongside (2026-08-02)

Picked as "the next feature (no preference)" per the author's compound message that also reported the Walmart-receipt import producing zero visible results and asked for address/zip geocoding on Dining Out -- all three landed in one round; this section covers all three since they were verified and committed together.

**1) Walmart PDF receipt import producing nothing, with no visible feedback.** Root cause was two compounding issues, both fixed:
- `inventory.py`'s `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT` only told the model to skip subtotal/tax/register-noise lines, never actual non-food purchased items -- a real Walmart receipt mixes groceries with household/personal-care/pet/OTC items. Rewrote the prompt with explicit exclusion categories and examples, and an explicit instruction to return `[]` (not prose) when nothing on the receipt is food.
- `InventoryPage.jsx`'s `confirmImportItems`/`confirmVisionItems` had **zero error handling** around the confirm POST -- any failure (network error, validation error, anything) silently did nothing, exactly matching "nothing gets imported, no console error." Wrapped both in try/catch with a visible `error-text` message and a busy/disabled confirm button.
- Added the visibility the author explicitly asked for: an "N items identified from your receipt/photo" line before confirm, an "Added N item(s) to inventory" message after, and a collapsible raw-AI-response `<pre>` block for troubleshooting without needing server logs. New test: `test_receipt_import_prompt_instructs_skipping_non_food_items`.
- Not independently verified against the author's actual PDF in this sandbox (no live Ollama vision model reaches here) -- the fix addresses the two concrete, code-visible failure modes; worth the author re-running the same import to confirm.

**2) Address/zip geocoding for Dining Out**, alongside the existing manual lat/lon and "use my location" (browser geolocation, still author-reported as unreliable -- not re-investigated this round, see below). New `dining_service.geocode()` against OpenStreetMap's free, keyless Nominatim API (`GET /api/dining/geocode?query=...`), returning candidate matches for the user to disambiguate rather than trusting the first hit. `DiningPage.jsx` gained an "Address or zip code" field + Look up button above the existing lat/lon form; picking a candidate fills lat/lon the same way "use my location" does. Nominatim itself 403's this sandbox's shared egress IP (confirmed via `httpx`, distinct from the sandbox's separate network-allowlist blocking that affects Overpass/USDA/PyPI) -- not a code defect, and the 502 error path was verified to work correctly regardless. 5 new tests for `parse_nominatim_response`. **"Use my location" itself was not re-investigated this round** -- the author's wording ("we're still trying to get it to work") suggests the prior fix hasn't yet been retested on the real iPad; flagged back to the author rather than silently dropped.

**3) The accessibility pass itself.** Rather than a superficial single-file pass, did two things with actual verification behind them instead of guesswork:
- **Contrast**: wrote a one-off script computing real WCAG relative-luminance/contrast ratios for every `theme.css` color-token pairing across all 9 themes. Found 26 failures below the 4.5:1 (text) / 3:1 (UI component) minimums, almost all the same shape -- white text hardcoded onto a theme's bright primary/secondary/danger color used as a button or banner fill, which needs a different contrasting color than when that same token is used as a link/text color on a dark background. Fixed by adding theme-specific `--color-on-primary`/`--color-on-secondary`/`--color-on-danger` tokens (computed per-theme, whichever of white/`#1a1a1a` passes) and repointing `.btn-primary`/`.gcal-banner` at them instead of a hardcoded `white`; nudged `daylight`'s own `--color-secondary` slightly darker (4.41->4.84) since it's Chef's own palette, not a third-party one. Deliberately left 4 remaining sub-4.5 combinations alone -- they're official Catppuccin brand swatches, not Chef's own design choices, and altering a named third-party theme's colors felt like the wrong tradeoff; documented inline in `theme.css` above those blocks.
- **Keyboard/focus**: added a global `:focus-visible` outline (using `--color-primary`, not `--color-accent` -- the latter measured only 1.81:1 against the default theme's own background, failing the 3:1 UI minimum, discovered by the same contrast script). Found and fixed the one non-semantic clickable element in the whole frontend via a systematic grep for `<div|span|li|td|tr|img> onClick=`: `RecipeChat.jsx`'s collapse/expand header was a bare `<div onClick>`, unreachable by keyboard and silent to a screen reader; converted to a real `<button>` with `aria-expanded`.
- **Form labels**: grepped every `<input`/`<select` in `frontend/src` and read each one's surrounding JSX to judge whether it had a real accessible name (wrapping `<label>` with text, `aria-label`, or `title`) versus relying on `placeholder` alone (not a reliable accessible name -- it disappears once text is entered and isn't treated as a label by every screen reader) or nothing at all. Fixed genuinely bare fields: `MealPlanEntryRow.jsx`'s per-slot recipe `<select>` and servings `<input>`; `GroceryListPanel.jsx`'s add-item name/qty/unit inputs and category select; `InventoryPage.jsx`'s category filter select; `MealPlanPage.jsx`'s plan-picker select, per-day guidance tags/notes inputs (previously only associated with a plain `<span>`, which isn't a programmatic label), and the generate-preview per-slot select/servings/tags inputs; `RecipesPage.jsx`'s search box; `KnowledgeFilesPanel.jsx`'s description field; `BarcodeScanner.jsx`'s manual-entry field; `ChatWidget.jsx`'s and `RecipeChat.jsx`'s message inputs. Left data-grid-style inputs alone (the recipe-folder-import review table in `RecipesPage.jsx`, the bloodwork-import review table in `HealthPage.jsx`) since they sit inside real `<table>` markup with `<th>` column headers, which a screen reader announces alongside each cell -- consistent treatment with the project's existing `data-label` mobile-stacking convention on read-only table cells, which needed no fix for the same reason.
- **Explicitly checked and found already correct, no change needed**: all 4 `<img>` tags already have appropriate `alt` text; `CookMode.jsx`/`ChatWidget.jsx`/`TimersBadge.jsx` already had correct `aria-label`/`role`/`aria-modal` usage from earlier session work; every checkbox in the app that's wrapped in a `<label>` with visible text (the large majority) already had a valid accessible name with no change needed.
- **Not done / known gap**: this was a targeted pass against concrete, verifiable defects (contrast ratios, keyboard reachability, missing form names) rather than an exhaustive WCAG 2.1 AA audit against every success criterion (e.g., heading-level structure, live-region announcements for async job completion, reduced-motion preferences were not reviewed this round). Worth a follow-up pass if the author wants full AA coverage rather than the highest-impact subset covered here.

**Verification**: 478 backend tests passing (unchanged from before this round's frontend-only accessibility work; the geocoding/import fixes added tests bringing the number up from 473), clean `vite build` (306 modules) run after the full set of changes including the last `RecipeChat.jsx` edit. Not verified: on-screen appearance/actual screen-reader behavior (no browser or assistive-tech tooling reaches this sandbox) -- worth the author's own pass with a screen reader or the browser's own accessibility inspector if this matters enough to fully confirm.

## B4.5 notes: inventory quantity/packaging redesign, and B4.1 barcode-scan re-verification (2026-08-02)

Author-requested directly: "I think we have room to improve the quantity system... perhaps we should separate measurement from packaging as two distinct fields" plus "there's the volume at time of purchase vs volume on hand... wouldn't that skew my price per meal calculations?" -- both concerns were verified as REAL, not hypothetical, by re-reading the actual code paths before changing anything, per this app's own standing discipline.

**The two bugs found.** (1) `InventoryItem.unit` was a compound string mixing a measurement with a container word (e.g. `"8 oz bag"`), produced by `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT`'s own prior rule 5 ("put that descriptor in unit instead, e.g. '8 oz bag'"). `unit_conversion_service.normalize_unit("8 oz bag")` doesn't recognize this as a real unit, so `inventory_service.deduct_by_name`'s unit-conversion fallback silently treated a recipe's "4 oz bacon" as 4 raw units against `quantity=1` (one PACKAGE) -- zeroing an entire pack of bacon after using a quarter of it. (2) `cost_service.compute_ingredient_line_cost` divided `unit_price` by the row's live, depleting `quantity` rather than a stable purchase-time snapshot, so a $6.00-for-2-lb purchase would recompute to $6.00/lb once 1 lb had been used, even though nothing about what was paid changed -- exactly the "skewed cost per meal" the author suspected.

**The redesign.** Four new nullable `InventoryItem` columns (migration `b3f7e9c2a5d1`, full rationale in the model's own docstring): `purchased_quantity` (immutable "on hand at purchase time" snapshot cost_service now divides by instead of the live `quantity`), and three purely-descriptive package fields never read by any arithmetic -- `package_quantity` (size of one package, e.g. 8 for "8 oz bag"), `package_count` (how many packages), `package_descriptor` (the leftover container word, e.g. "bag"). `quantity`/`unit` keep their existing columns but now mean, going forward, "on-hand amount, in a REAL convertible measurement unit" -- not a compound string.

**New shared parser** (`app/services/package_parsing.py`, `parse_package_text`): splits a freeform string like "8 oz bag", "10oz 6 Count pack", or Open Food Facts' "12 x 355 ml" into `(package_quantity, unit, package_descriptor, package_count)`, returning `None` (never guessing) when there's no recognizable leading measurement. Used in three places: the barcode-lookup endpoint (parses OFF's `quantity` field, previously explicitly NOT parsed -- see BarcodeLookupResponse's revised docstring for why that was the right call before this session's data-model change and isn't now), `order_import_service.apply_mapping` (a spreadsheet's own unit column), and `inventory_service.parse_vision_response` (a POST-PROCESSING step on whatever the receipt/vision AI prompts already return).

**Deliberately did NOT touch `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT` or `VISION_PROMPT`.** Both were only just stabilized against the author's real Ollama container earlier this same session-history (see the session log's multi-round receipt-import saga) -- editing either prompt's wording risked re-triggering that exact regression for an unrelated reason. Instead, `parse_vision_response` applies `package_parsing` to whatever compound `unit` text the (unchanged) prompt already produces, fixing the same underlying bug with zero prompt risk.

**Migration backfill** (`b3f7e9c2a5d1`): for every existing row, best-effort splits the current `unit` text the same way; on success, rewrites `unit` to the clean canonical unit and sets `package_quantity`/`package_descriptor`/`package_count`/`purchased_quantity`/`quantity` accordingly, with one explicitly documented assumption -- nothing has been used yet as of the upgrade (there's no historical "how much was used" data to recover). On failure (no recognizable leading measurement, e.g. "bunch", "each"), the row is left completely alone. Verified live against a fresh SQLite DB seeded with the exact strings from the author's own inventory screenshot ("10oz 6 Count pack", "8 oz Bag", "14 oz can each", plus an unparseable "bunch" and a `unit=None` loose-item row) -- every parseable row backfilled correctly, every unparseable row was untouched, both upgrade and downgrade paths run cleanly. Deliberately carries its own frozen copy of the parsing regex rather than importing `package_parsing.py` -- every other migration in this repo is pure schema DDL with no app-code import, and this keeps that same independence (see the migration's own module docstring).

**Frontend** (`InventoryItemForm.jsx`): new "Package count" / "Package size" / "Unit" (datalist-suggested canonical units) / "Container" (free text) row, auto-computing the "On-hand quantity" field as `package_count * package_quantity` until the household directly edits that field themselves (tracked via a ref, not re-overwritten by a later package-field tweak). An "Originally purchased" field appears when editing an existing row, for manually correcting the cost-calc baseline. `InventoryPage.jsx`'s main table now shows a package-breakdown hint line and an "N% on hand" indicator (quantity / purchased_quantity) when both are known, and every AI-import/order-import/barcode confirm path now carries the new fields through to the create payload instead of just the flattened on-hand total.

**B6.1 cost_service fix**: `price_per_match_unit` now divides by `match.purchased_quantity or match.quantity` -- falls back to the old (live-quantity) behavior only for rows with no recorded purchase-time snapshot (pre-migration rows that couldn't be backfilled, or an intake source with no purchase concept), never a hard failure.

**Deliberately NOT built this pass**: merge-on-restock (buying another 8oz bag of an item already in inventory currently always creates a new row rather than adding to the existing row's on-hand quantity) -- a real, reasonable future enhancement, but a bigger feature (matching, differing expiration dates on the two batches, etc.) than "fix the quantity/packaging model" asked for; flagged here rather than silently attempted.

**B4.1 barcode-scan re-verification** (the author's other explicit ask this session: camera-based barcode entry on Android/iOS/iPad). Confirmed this was already fully built 2026-08-01 (`BarcodeScanner.jsx`, ZXing-based, rear-camera preference, manual-entry fallback, HTTPS/secure-context-aware error messages) and wired into `InventoryPage.jsx`'s "Scan barcode" button -- no gap found, no rebuild needed. Updated it this session only to carry the new `package_quantity`/`package_count`/`package_descriptor` fields from a scanned barcode's lookup result into the add-item form alongside the pre-existing name/category/quantity/unit prefill.

**Verified**: 577 backend tests passing (up from 537), including 14 new `test_package_parsing.py` tests against real strings from the author's own screenshot, updated `test_barcode_lookup.py`/`test_cost_service.py`/`test_order_import_service.py`/`test_inventory_import.py` assertions reflecting the new intentional behavior, and a live migration dry-run (upgrade + downgrade + re-upgrade) against a real SQLite DB as described above. Clean `vite build` (306 modules). **Not verified, and cannot be from this sandbox**: on-screen appearance of the new form fields/table hints, or the barcode scanner's real camera behavior on an actual phone/iPad -- same standing limitation as every other UI-facing change in this project's history; this sandbox additionally has no writable `/app` path, so a live curl-against-a-running-uvicorn pass (the usual fallback verification for router-level logic like `create_inventory_item`'s `purchased_quantity` defaulting) wasn't reachable this session either -- covered instead by the unit-test suite and the live migration dry-run.

## B8.2 notes: wearable/health-platform import (2026-08-02)

Picked as one of two "no preference which" next roadmap items this session (the other being B12.2, below).

**Apple Health path (deterministic, no AI call)**: `health_service.parse_apple_health_export` accepts either a raw `export.xml` upload or the `export.zip` the Health app's own "Export All Health Data" produces, and streams it via `xml.etree.ElementTree.iterparse` (clearing each `<Record>` element immediately after reading) rather than a plain `ET.parse` -- a real multi-year export.xml can be hundreds of MB to multiple GB, and loading the whole tree would not be viable on this app's target hardware. Reads `HKQuantityTypeIdentifierBodyMass` (weight -- last sample of the day wins, converting lb->kg per-sample since a household's unit preference can genuinely change partway through their Health history) and `HKQuantityTypeIdentifierStepCount` (steps -- summed across the day, since HealthKit records many small same-day increments). Everything else in the export is ignored.

**Everything else: routed through the SAME free-text Ollama extraction pipeline B8.1's bloodwork import already built** (`WEARABLE_IMPORT_PROMPT`, a sibling of `BLOODWORK_IMPORT_PROMPT`), not a second hand-built parser. This was a researched decision, not a shortcut: live web search (2026-08-02) confirmed Android Health Connect's own native "export" is a proprietary backup ZIP meant for Health-Connect-to-Health-Connect restore, with no documented, stable, third-party-parseable JSON/CSV schema published anywhere -- the same class of gap this app already hit with Walmart's purchase history (B10.3) and declined to build a guessed parser against there either. A household using a third-party Health Connect export tool (several produce CSV) or a Withings export gets a real, working path through the AI extraction; nothing here pretends to natively understand a format that was never actually verified to exist in a stable public shape.

**Schema**: new nullable `HealthMetricEntry.steps` column (migration `c9d4a2f8e6b1`) -- deliberately just a daily integer total, not a fuller activity model (active minutes, workout sessions, heart rate). This table's grain is one row per (member, entry_date), built for occasional point readings, not a real time-series store -- B8.3's own already-written backlog text flags this exact gap for anything denser than a daily total, and this addition stays inside that stated boundary rather than trying to solve B8.3's larger, deferred question as a side effect.

**Backend**: new `POST /api/health/import-wearable` (multipart file upload only, no text-paste option -- unlike bloodwork, this is always a real export file), dispatching on filename/content sniff (`.xml`/`.zip`/a PK-magic-byte zip -> Apple Health path; anything else -> AI extraction path). Runs through the shared background job queue (B11.1) even for the Apple Health path, which makes no Ollama call at all -- a large export.xml can still take real wall-clock time to stream-parse, and this app's standing rule is "anything that isn't instant gets a visible busy indicator," not just "anything that calls Ollama." New `HealthWearableEntryPreview`/`HealthWearableImportResponse` schemas, deliberately reusing the exact same preview-then-confirm discipline and the same `POST /api/health/metrics` confirm endpoint bloodwork import already established -- BMI computation/validation stays on the one real write path.

**Frontend**: `HealthPage.jsx` gained a collapsible "wearable/health-app import" panel (file upload, `useBackgroundJob`-driven) below the existing bloodwork import panel, with a review table (member picker, date, weight in lbs, steps, per-row Add/Discard, bulk "Add all to log") -- and the metrics history table gained a Steps column.

**Verified**: 10 new unit tests (`test_wearable_import.py`) covering last-sample-wins weight aggregation, same-day step summation across multiple records, ignoring unrelated HealthKit record types, the `.zip`-containing-`export.xml` path (including correctly skipping the longer-named `export_cda.xml` clinical-records companion file), a `.zip` with no `export.xml` raising a readable error, an empty export, and the AI-extraction fallback's defensive parsing (garbage input, entries with neither field dropped). Clean `vite build`. **Not verified**: real extraction accuracy against an actual Apple Health export or a real Health Connect/Withings file (no live Ollama reaches this sandbox, and no sample real-world export file was available to test against) -- worth the author's own first real import as the practical verification, same standing pattern as every other AI-dependent import in this app.

## B12.2 notes: iCloud Calendar push sync (2026-08-02)

The other of the two "no preference which" next roadmap items picked up this session.

**Auth**: an app-specific password against a real Apple ID (generated at appleid.apple.com, Sign-In and Security -> App-Specific Passwords) instead of OAuth -- Apple has no public developer OAuth program for third-party CalDAV clients. This is actually LESS setup friction than B12.1's Google flow (no OAuth client to register, no redirect-URI gotcha), confirming the backlog text's own prediction.

**Protocol**: hand-rolled CalDAV (WebDAV PROPFIND/MKCALENDAR + plain PUT/DELETE of `.ics` resources) via `httpx` and the stdlib `xml.etree`, deliberately not the `caldav` PyPI package -- consistent with this project's existing preference for a stdlib-adjacent implementation over a new dependency where the protocol surface actually used is small (same reasoning `calendar_export_service.py`'s hand-built `.ics` export already documents). Discovery (principal -> calendar-home-set -> find-or-create the "Chef Meal Plan" collection) is the one genuinely fiddly part of CalDAV; every event push/delete after that is a single HTTP request.

**The one real architectural difference from B12.1's Google sync**: iCloud's CalDAV resource model addresses one `.ics` file per event by a URL Chef itself controls, so every event's URL is DETERMINISTIC from `MealPlanEntry.id` (`chef-mealplan-entry-{id}@chef.local.ics`) -- unlike Google's server-assigned event ids, this needed no new `MealPlanEntry` column at all, and a PUT is always "create or replace the whole resource" rather than needing a create-vs-update branch. `routers/meal_plan.py` gained iCloud-specific sync helpers (`_enqueue_icloud_plan_sync`/`_enqueue_icloud_entry_sync`/`_enqueue_icloud_event_cleanup`) called alongside the existing Google ones at all four calendar-relevant mutation sites (plan create, plan delete, entry update, entry skip) rather than merged into one provider-generic helper -- the two providers' cleanup inputs differ enough (Google needs pre-collected event ids; iCloud only ever needs entry ids) that a shared abstraction would have been more indirection than it saved.

**Backend**: `icloud_calendar_service.py` (connect/disconnect/status/push/delete/resync, mirroring `google_calendar_service.py`'s shape), `routers/icloud_calendar.py` (`/api/calendar/icloud/status`/`connect`/`disconnect`/`sync-enabled`/`resync` -- no `/authorize`/`/callback` OAuth routes needed), four new settings (`icloud_calendar_username`, `icloud_calendar_app_password` [encrypted], `icloud_calendar_calendar_href` [written automatically], `icloud_calendar_sync_enabled`), and a new `icloud_calendar` entry in `GET /api/system/status`'s `integrations` array (B14.1's pattern) alongside `google_calendar`.

**Frontend**: `SettingsPage.jsx` gained an "iCloud Calendar" card in the Integrations tab (status, Connect/Disconnect, sync toggle, Force resync -- same shape as the Google card, minus the OAuth redirect handling), and `icloud_calendar_username`/`icloud_calendar_app_password` render as normal generic settings rows (the password field auto-masked, same `is_secret` treatment every other secret gets) rather than needing Google's redirect-URI-suggestion special-casing.

**Verified**: 11 new unit tests (`test_icloud_calendar_service.py`) covering config/connection gating, calendar-href discovery against synthetic CalDAV multistatus XML shaped like Apple's own published RFC 4791/RFC 5397 examples (both the create-new-calendar and reuse-existing-calendar branches, with discovery-result caching confirmed on a second call), a 401-credentials-rejected error path, `push_entry`'s deterministic PUT url/body, `delete_event`'s 404-treated-as-success and never-connected-no-op behaviors, and `sync_entry`'s skip-vs-push branching -- every CalDAV call faked via a single monkeypatched `httpx.request` (this module deliberately routes every request through one function for exactly this testability). Clean `vite build`. **Not independently verified against a real iCloud account** -- this sandbox has no route to apple.com/icloud.com domains (same class of restriction as B4.3's fsis.usda.gov finding), so every code path is verified against synthetic XML fixtures, not a live round trip; the author's first real connect attempt is the practical integration test, same standing caveat B12.1's own notes carry for Google.

## Bug fix: barcode camera silently not engaging on iOS, plus iOS certificate-trust profile support (2026-08-03)

Author-reported from a real iPad, after B15.1's HTTPS work and the later geolocation fix: (A) `Settings > General > About > Certificate Trust Settings` showed only an iOS version number, no toggle to enable; (B) Safari would not even offer to let the user through to the site, described as blocked by "the untrusted root CA"; (C) with the device now back on plain HTTP, tapping "Scan barcode" showed no camera preview, no error message, and no camera permission prompt at all, despite the browser's own site setting already granting camera access.

**(C) was a real, fixable code bug, found by reading `BarcodeScanner.jsx`'s effect, not assumed.** Its secure-context guard checked `!navigator.mediaDevices?.getUserMedia` first and only used `window.isSecureContext` to decide *which* error message to show once that check failed. But WebKit (Safari, and every browser on iOS/iPadOS, since Apple mandates WebKit there) keeps `navigator.mediaDevices` and a `getUserMedia` function reference present on the object even over an insecure origin -- it just never resolves or rejects a call made through it. That meant the guard's `if` evaluated false (the API "existed"), so execution fell through into ZXing's `listVideoInputDevices()`, which called into that same never-settling API and hung forever with no visible error -- exactly the reported "nothing happens" symptom. Fixed by checking `window.isSecureContext === false` FIRST, unconditionally, before ever touching ZXing or the mediaDevices object at all -- now a plain-HTTP visit shows the pre-existing "Camera access needs HTTPS (or localhost)" message immediately instead of hanging silently. (`DiningPage.jsx`'s geolocation code was checked for the same class of bug and is not affected -- it already has explicit timeouts from the earlier "stuck on Locating..." fix, so a hang there degrades to a clear error rather than silence; not touched this pass.)

**(A) and (B) are a real gap in how HTTPS trust was designed, not user error.** The app's only trust path was, and still is by default, "click through the browser's own per-origin warning" (Settings > Security's Certificate card, and the WIKI's `https-setup` entry) -- correct and sufficient for an ordinary Safari tab, but it has two real limits this session surfaced: it depends on Safari actually offering a bypass (which the author's report suggests didn't happen, or wasn't found), and it fundamentally cannot extend to a Chef PWA installed to the home screen (B7.3) -- a standalone installed web app has no browser chrome to show that warning in at all, so it needs the device to already trust the certificate at the OS level before the app is ever opened. Nothing in Chef previously gave a household a way to install the self-signed cert as a trusted system profile, which is exactly why Certificate Trust Settings had nothing to show a toggle for -- that iOS screen is empty by design until a certificate has actually been installed as a profile, and the per-origin click-through never does that.

**Built `tls_service.build_mobileconfig()`** (new `GET /api/tls/mobileconfig`, gated by the same auth as every other `/api/tls/*` route): packages the currently active certificate as a standard Apple Configuration Profile (stdlib `plistlib`, no new dependency), using `PayloadType: com.apple.security.root` for the certificate payload -- the specific payload type that makes the Certificate Trust Settings toggle appear at all after the profile is installed. This is additive, not a replacement: the existing per-origin click-through still works and needs no profile; this is for households that hit Safari's bypass not appearing, or that want the warning gone everywhere on the device (including the PWA) rather than once per browser tab.

**Frontend**: Settings > Security's Certificate card gained a "Download certificate for iOS/iPadOS" link (visible once a certificate is active) pointing at the new endpoint, with inline copy explaining what it's for and a pointer to the WIKI for the exact install sequence. The WIKI's `https-setup` entry gained a new subsection: download → Safari's "allow profile download" prompt → Settings app's "Profile Downloaded" banner → Install (passcode, confirm the unsigned-profile warning, which a self-signed cert for a LAN always triggers) → **then**, as a clearly separate final step, Settings > General > About > Certificate Trust Settings → toggle full trust on -- explicitly explaining that this is the toggle the author went looking for and found missing, and why it was empty before. Also documented: removing/replacing the certificate later means removing the old installed profile too (Settings > General > VPN & Device Management), then reinstalling against the new one.

**Verified**: 4 new `test_tls_service.py` tests (plist round-trip via `plistlib.loads`, correct `PayloadType`, DER bytes match the real active cert byte-for-byte, per-call UUID uniqueness) plus 2 router tests (correct media type/`Content-Disposition`, and a 400 with no active cert) -- full backend suite re-run in this session's balanced batches, 583 tests passing (up from 577), all green including the pre-existing 24 (now 28) `test_tls_service.py` tests. Clean `vite build` (306 modules, built to a scratch `--outDir` per this project's standing FUSE `EPERM` workaround). **Not verified, and cannot be from this sandbox**: whether iOS actually accepts and offers to install the generated `.mobileconfig` file, the Certificate Trust Settings toggle actually appearing after install, or the barcode scanner's corrected error message rendering on a real device over real plain HTTP -- same standing limitation as every other iOS-facing change in this project's history (no physical device or apple.com/icloud.com network route reaches this sandbox). The plist structure and `PayloadType` follow Apple's own published Configuration Profile Reference, but the author's own real-device attempt is the practical verification step, same pattern as B12.1/B12.2's own notes.

## B16.1 notes: recipe-import parsing regression fix, and AI prompts as GUI-editable advanced settings (2026-08-03)

Author-reported, with an uploaded PDF ("Pumpkin Chiffon Pie Recipe | Bon Appetit") that reproduced it: recipe import going from "parses wrong" to "totally broken" -- the Recipes page's own error banner read "Import failed: Could not extract a recipe from that input" against a plain, well-formed, two-page recipe PDF with nothing unusual about it. Two asks: (A) fix it -- the author's own hypothesis was that this needed a prompt redesign like the receipt importer got on 2026-08-02; (B) stop needing a code change/container rebuild every time local AI output fails to parse -- expose the import prompts as GUI-editable settings.

**Root cause, found by tracing every consumer of the function that raises that exact error, not by guessing at the prompt.** `finish_recipe_parse` raises "Could not extract a recipe from that input" only when `parse_recipe_response` returns `None`, which only happens when `recipe_service._extract_json_object(raw_output)` returns `{}` (or a dict with no `"title"`). Reading `_extract_json_object` found it was STILL the original, naive implementation: `json.loads()` first, then a fallback `re.search(r"\{.*\}", raw_text, re.DOTALL)` -- a GREEDY regex spanning the FIRST `{` anywhere in the model's response to the LAST `}` anywhere in it. This is the exact bug class already found and fixed once before, for the ARRAY-shaped receipt/vision-intake responses, in `inventory_service._extract_json_array` on 2026-08-02 (see that session's own notes) -- but that fix was never ported to the OBJECT-shaped extractor, and grepping every caller of `_extract_json_object` found it is not recipe-import-only: `health_service.py` (2 call sites), `chat_service.py`, and `meal_plan_service.py` all import and use this exact same function for their own AI-response parsing. A single unfixed function was quietly the shared point of fragility behind recipe import, recipe-chat "propose an edit", health/bloodwork free-text parsing, and meal-plan generation's `new_recipe` proposals all at once -- which lines up with the author's broader "just how fragile and inconsistent this application is" framing better than a recipe-only bug would have.

Why this surfaces NOW specifically: `ollama_client.chat()` requests `think=False` on every call, but per that module's own prior investigation this is not honored by every Ollama server/model/chat-template combination -- and the household's currently configured chat model (`qwen3.6:27b`, swapped in on 2026-08-02, a thinking-capable model) puts a reasoning trace inline in `message.content` routinely. A trace reasoning through "let me structure this as JSON: `{ "title": ... }`" contains its OWN scratch braces; the greedy regex then spans from a brace inside that scratch draft to the real answer's closing brace, producing unparseable garbage, silently collapsed to `{}` -- indistinguishable, to the user, from "the model found no recipe."

**Fix.** New `app/services/ai_json_extraction.py`: `strip_reasoning()` (moved verbatim from `inventory_service`) plus a string-aware bracket-matching scan offered for BOTH shapes -- `extract_json_array()` (the existing, already-proven fix, moved here so both extraction functions live in one shared module instead of the array fix being inventory-only) and the new `extract_json_object()` (same discipline: strip any reasoning trace, then walk every `{` doing a real bracket-matching scan rather than a greedy regex, taking the first candidate that parses to a non-empty dict). Also added for the object case, not present in the original array fix: salvage of a TRUNCATED object (generation cut off mid-`instructions` array, say) by cutting at the end of the last fully-formed top-level `"key": value` pair and closing with `}` -- recovers a recipe's title/description/ingredients even when a long instructions list got cut off mid-generation, rather than discarding a mostly-complete result. `inventory_service.py` and `recipe_service.py` both now delegate to this shared module; `recipe_service._extract_json_object` is kept as a one-line re-export (health_service/chat_service/meal_plan_service all already import it from there by name -- zero call-site churn in those three files).

**Redesigned `RECIPE_IMPORT_PROMPT`** into the same numbered-rules-plus-worked-example format `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT` got on 2026-08-02, even though the actual bug lived in extraction, not prompt wording -- for consistency (one style of extraction prompt app-wide) and defense in depth (a long flowing prose-paragraph prompt was independently confirmed, that same prior session, to raise a capable-but-not-huge local model's odds of bailing out early). Every functional requirement from the prior version is preserved, including the "same ingredient name reused across sections -- crust vs. filling, a `divided` quantity split across two occurrences -- must be listed as separate entries, never merged" rule, which the reported PDF's own filling section (`3/4 cup plus 2 Tbsp. sugar, divided`, then a later `remaining 2 Tbsp. sugar` step) exercises directly; the redesigned prompt's own worked example uses that exact case.

**Substitution mechanics changed too, app-wide, for both `RECIPE_IMPORT_PROMPT` and `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT`**: `{content}`/`{today}` are now filled via plain `str.replace()` at every call site, not `.format()`. This matters specifically because of part (B) below -- a household member editing free text in a Settings textarea has no reason to know `.format()`'s brace-escaping rule, and a prompt containing one stray, unescaped `{` or `}` (a JSON example, anything) used to raise a hard `KeyError`/`IndexError` at request time instead of just behaving oddly. `.replace()` has no such footgun. `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT`'s worked example, previously `{{`/`}}`-escaped to survive `.format()`, is unescaped back to plain single braces as part of this change.

**Part (B): every AI import/extraction prompt is now a DB-backed, GUI-editable `SystemPrompt` row**, exactly the existing `main_chef`/`dietary_onboarding` pattern (same table, same `/api/system/prompts` GET/PATCH, same Settings-page textarea) -- no new plumbing invented. Four new prompt keys: `recipe_import`, `recipe_modify` (the recipe-chat "propose an edit" instructions), `receipt_import`, `vision_intake` (the pantry/fridge photo prompt). Each gets a `get_*_prompt(db)` getter (`recipe_service.py`, `routers/inventory.py`) returning the household's active DB override if one exists, else the module's own hardcoded constant -- so unchecking "Active" in the Settings UI reverts to the shipped default without losing the household's draft edit, the same semantics the existing two prompts already had. `app/seed.py` seeds all four with the exact text their code-level fallback carries, so a fresh install's Settings page shows real, editable prompt text rather than an empty box. Frontend: `SettingsPage.jsx`'s "System prompts" card now only shows the two persona/onboarding prompts; a new "Advanced: import & extraction prompts" card (behind a `<details>` disclosure, matching this page's existing "Advanced: use a certificate from your own CA" pattern) holds the four new ones, with copy explaining most households never need to touch these.

**Verified**: 34 new backend unit tests (`test_ai_json_extraction.py` -- direct coverage of `strip_reasoning`/`extract_json_object`/`extract_json_array`, including a test that reproduces the exact reported failure shape, an inline `<think>` trace containing a scratch JSON draft with its own braces; new tests in `test_recipe_service.py` covering `parse_recipe_response`'s resilience end-to-end plus the four `get_*_prompt` getters' DB-override/fallback/inactive-reverts-to-default behavior; new tests in `test_inventory_import.py` for the receipt/vision getters and `.replace()`-substitution mechanics; new `test_seed_system_prompts.py` for the seeding/idempotency/no-clobber-a-customization behavior) -- 617 tests collected (up from 583), 527 run and green in this sandbox across every non-network-dependent file (the same 6 files already excluded from this sandbox's runs for needing live network access -- `test_dining_service.py`, `test_end_to_end_smoke.py`, `test_google_calendar_service.py`, `test_icloud_calendar_service.py`, `test_recall_service.py`, `test_barcode_lookup.py` -- untouched by this change and not re-verified here). Additionally hand-verified end-to-end against a reconstruction of the actual reported failure: fed `parse_recipe_response` a synthetic response shaped exactly like a real `qwen3.6:27b` reply to the redesigned prompt against the reported PDF's own text (an inline reasoning trace with a scratch JSON draft, followed by the real answer containing the PDF's own divided-sugar case) -- parses correctly, both sugar occurrences kept separate. `vite build` transforms cleanly (306 modules, no JSX errors); the actual output-directory write step hit this sandbox's known FUSE `EPERM`/stale-mount limitation (see the standing note elsewhere in this file), not a code issue -- same class of sandbox limitation already documented for every other frontend change in this project's history. **Not independently verified against a real, live Ollama container** -- as with every other AI-prompt change in this project's history, this sandbox has no route to the author's actual Ollama instance; the fix is verified via synthetic reproductions of the documented real failure shapes (this session's own PDF, plus the `<think>`-trace shapes `ollama_client.py`'s own prior investigation already confirmed as real, live-observed behavior) and the full existing test suite staying green, not a live round trip.

## Architecture fix: the frontend is now the only thing a browser ever talks to (2026-08-03)

Author-reported directly, and framed correctly as an architecture problem rather than two separate bugs: a phone on the LAN (not the machine running Docker) showed "Backend status: unreachable" on the Home page, and the barcode scanner's mobileconfig/secure-context fix from earlier the same day still didn't get the camera working. The author's own diagnosis, quoted because it's exactly right: this app was "designed from a localhost access only perspective," the browser itself called the backend container directly on a second origin/port, and "any device on the local network needs to be able to access the front end application... the front end itself should be the only thing that talks to the back end."

**Root cause, confirmed by re-reading `src/api.js`'s `backendOrigin` before touching anything:** the production build had the BROWSER call the backend container directly at `${hostname}:${BACKEND_PORT}` (a documented "Known simplification: frontend/backend origins" since Phase 3, never revisited). That works from the machine serving the app, but asks every OTHER device to independently reach a second origin and, once B15.1 added HTTPS, trust a second certificate -- either failing looks exactly like "unreachable" or "the camera doesn't work" with no obvious link back to the real cause. This also explains the same-day iOS certificate-trust confusion in hindsight: trusting only one of the two origins left background API calls failing silently, which is the exact "stuck Loading" failure mode the WIKI already warned about but the architecture itself created.

**Fixed structurally, not per-symptom.** New `frontend/server.js` replaces the old `serve` static-file process with a small Node server (plain `http`/`https`, `http-proxy`, `serve-handler` -- the same library `serve` itself is built on, and the same proxying engine Vite's own dev-server proxy already uses) that serves the built SPA AND reverse-proxies `/api/*` and `/health` to the backend over Docker's own internal network. The browser now only ever reaches and needs to trust ONE origin -- the frontend's -- in dev (already true, via `vite.config.js`) and in the production Docker build alike.

**Consequences worked through, not left as loose ends:**
- `src/api.js`'s `backendOrigin` collapses to always `""` -- every existing `${backendOrigin}/api/...`-style call site across the app (recipe images, calendar/JSON-LD/backup/certificate downloads) resolves correctly as a same-origin relative URL with no per-file changes needed.
- **Google Calendar OAuth redirect URI is a real, documented breaking change for anyone already connected**: it used to point at the backend's own origin (Google redirected there directly); it now has to point at the frontend's, since that's the only address a browser -- and now Google -- can reach. `SettingsPage.jsx`'s suggestion logic and the WIKI's `google-calendar-setup` entry were both updated, and the Settings page now shows a warning banner on the Google Calendar card if it detects the saved redirect URI's origin doesn't match the current page's.
- The WIKI's `https-setup` entry lost its single most-missed step ("visit and accept the warning at BOTH origins separately") entirely -- there's only one certificate to trust now. The iOS mobileconfig guidance from earlier today still applies, just against one origin instead of two. The stuck-"Loading"-after-cert troubleshooting note was rewritten -- a 502 from the frontend proxy (backend unreachable over the internal Docker network) is the new failure mode to check for, not a second untrusted origin.
- The backend's own host ports (`BACKEND_PORT`/`BACKEND_HTTPS_PORT`) are kept published in `docker-compose.yml` for optional direct API access/scripting, clearly commented as no longer required for normal app use. New `BACKEND_INTERNAL_HOST` env var (default `backend`, the Compose service name) is what the proxy actually targets, always plain HTTP internally regardless of whether the frontend itself is serving HTTP or HTTPS -- TLS only needs to terminate once, at the edge a browser actually reaches.
- `sw.js` (the PWA service worker) already excluded `/api/*` from caching by pathname, correctly origin-agnostic; `/health` wasn't excluded and would now be same-origin-cacheable too -- fixed alongside this, a genuinely stale cached "backend ok" being exactly the kind of misleading result this app's own status check exists to catch.
- The old `window.__CHEF_CONFIG__`/`config.js` mechanism (which existed solely so the browser could learn the backend's port to call it directly) is retired entirely -- nothing needs it anymore.

**A live-typed port number in the author's own follow-up message caught a real mistake mid-session**: an early pass of the WIKI edits hardcoded `5173`/`8095` as if Chef's own defaults universally applied, when the author's own `.env` already uses a custom `BACKEND_PORT` (8096) for a real port conflict on their machine. Rewritten to consistently point at `.env`'s `FRONTEND_PORT`/`BACKEND_PORT`/etc. as the source of truth, with Chef's defaults shown only as an explicitly-labeled placeholder -- a good general lesson for any future WIKI/README edit in this project: never state a concrete port as if it's the reader's, always frame it as "your `.env` value, N by default."

**Verified**: live end-to-end proxy test in the sandbox (real uvicorn backend + the real `server.js`, not mocked) -- `/health` and `GET /api/inventory` proxied correctly, a JSON `POST /api/kitchen-profiles` created a real row through the proxy, a static asset and an unmatched SPA-fallback path both served correctly (matching `serve -s`'s own documented `rewrites: [{source: "**", destination: "/index.html"}]` behavior, read directly from `serve`'s source before relying on it), and a multipart file upload (`POST /api/inventory/vision-intake`) passed through untouched and was accepted by the backend, confirming streaming/multipart passthrough works with no body-parsing middleware in the way. Separately verified the backend-down case: a clean `502 {"detail": "Backend unreachable..."}`, the proxy process itself stays alive, and static file serving keeps working afterward -- an unhandled `http-proxy` error would otherwise crash the whole frontend process, which was checked and is NOT what happens. Backend test suite spot-checked (main.py import/startup, `test_tls_service.py`, the end-to-end smoke test) rather than the full 583 re-run, since the only backend change this pass was a comment; clean `vite build` (306 modules, unchanged module count) confirms the SettingsPage.jsx/api.js/sw.js edits introduced no syntax errors. **Not verified, and cannot be from this sandbox**: real multi-device LAN testing (a second physical device reaching the frontend's address and confirming the Home page no longer shows "unreachable"), or a real Google Calendar reconnect against the new redirect URI -- both are the author's own next real-world check.

**Found but deliberately NOT touched**: the working tree already contained unrelated, uncommitted changes to `inventory_service.py`/`recipe_service.py`/`seed.py`/`ai_json_extraction.py` (a JSON-extraction-from-thinking-models bug fix, timestamped earlier the same day -- almost certainly a parallel session) -- left completely alone and excluded from this commit by staging an explicit file list rather than `git add -A`, rather than silently absorbing or reverting work this session didn't do and doesn't have context on.

## Hardening: explicit Cache-Control on the frontend's never-hashed entry files (2026-08-03)

Added defensively while re-auditing the camera/barcode path after two rounds of fixes were reported as still not working post-deploy: neither `serve-handler` (checked its source directly) nor the old `serve` CLI it replaced ever set a `Cache-Control` header by default -- only `Last-Modified`, no `Expires`/`Cache-Control` at all -- which leaves a browser free to apply its own heuristic freshness lifetime to `index.html`/`sw.js`/the manifest and serve a stale copy after a rebuild without re-asking the server at all. `frontend/server.js` now sets `Cache-Control: no-cache` (always revalidate) on those three never-hashed entry files, and `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` on Vite's hashed `/assets/*` files (safe -- a content change is a new URL). Not confirmed as THE cause of the still-reported camera failure -- flagged and fixed as a real, independently-worth-having class of "why doesn't my fix show up after redeploying" risk for every future frontend change, not just this one. Verified live: `curl -D -` against a running `server.js` shows the exact expected header on `index.html`, a real hashed asset, and `sw.js`. Clean `vite build` (306 modules, unchanged).

## Audit and remediation pass (2026-08-03)

A full architecture review and code audit was run over the whole codebase
by a fresh reviewer. Findings live in **AUDIT-2026-08-03.md** at the repo
root -- read that file, not this summary, before touching any of the areas
below. It records the root cause of each defect and, for several, the
incorrect theory that had previously been "fixed" in its place.

**The headline finding.** Every structured AI response in this app was
free-form text that a bespoke 250-line parser then tried to rescue.
Ollama has supported JSON-Schema-constrained decoding via its `format`
parameter the whole time, which makes a malformed response
unrepresentable rather than merely unlikely. Three successive rounds of
prompt and parser tuning on the recipe importer -- each documented in this
file as a root cause found -- were all treating the symptom. Extraction now
goes through `app/schemas/ai_extraction.py` and
`ollama_client.chat_json()`; `ai_json_extraction.py` is demoted to a
documented fallback for an Ollama server too old to support `format`.

**Seven P0 defects, all fixed except where noted:**

- Ollama calls had NO timeout (ollama-python's default) on a single serial
  worker thread -- one stalled generation wedged every AI feature in the
  app permanently. Now bounded by a configurable `ollama_timeout_seconds`.
- A stale `job_id` in localStorage permanently disabled the import buttons
  on whichever page stored it, after any container restart, with no way out
  short of clearing site data.
- The barcode scanner enumerated cameras BEFORE requesting permission.
  WebKit withholds device ids until access is granted, so on iOS the id was
  `""`, the decode effect returned early, and `getUserMedia` was never
  called at all -- no prompt, no error, no camera. It failed over HTTPS too;
  the secure-context fix on 2026-08-03 was addressing a different problem.
- Receipt extraction ran with `presence_penalty: 1.5` -- a parameter that
  penalises repeated tokens, applied to a response that is deliberately
  repetitive. It was added while chasing the "captured 4 of 8 receipt
  items" bug and was making it worse.
- The frontend Docker build copied the host's `node_modules` over the
  image's (no `.dockerignore` existed anywhere), so builds were not
  reproducible.
- **Installing a TLS certificate broke every API call.** B15.1 turned
  `BACKEND_PORT` into a redirect-only listener when a cert is present; the
  2026-08-03 proxy rework pointed the frontend proxy at that same port. Every
  API call came back as a 307 to an internal Docker hostname no browser can
  resolve. TLS now terminates once, at the frontend.
- Dining Out evaluated restrictions and then never used the result --
  results were sorted by distance and cut to the nearest 50, which in a
  dense area discards tagged matches before they are ever seen.

**Correctness fixes worth knowing about:**

- `normalize_unit` lowercased before lookup, collapsing `T` (tablespoon)
  into `t` (teaspoon). Every "2 T butter" was silently 2 teaspoons.
- Inventory deduction and grocery-list subtraction fell back to subtracting
  raw numbers when a unit conversion failed. "2 lb chicken" against a
  "500 g" row computed 2 - 500 and dropped chicken off the shopping list.
  Both now refuse rather than guess, and say why.
- SQLite was running with foreign keys OFF (the default, which SQLAlchemy
  does not change) -- every FK in the schema was decorative.

**P1-5, the ingredient resolution layer -- done 2026-08-03 (own session).**
Ingredient identity is this app's join key across inventory, recipes,
grocery lines and price lookups, and it was unbounded substring matching
in both directions taking the first row in arbitrary order (`"egg"`
