- **2026-08-02**: REAL ROOT CAUSE of the receipt-import "0 items" saga, found via a live, controlled
  A/B test against the author's actual Ollama container rather than another guess -- this is the
  entry that finally resolved it, after the sampling-options correction directly below turned out
  to be necessary but not sufficient. The author retried the fixed code and shared real
  `docker compose logs` output: `done=True done_reason='stop' eval_count=2 prompt_eval_count=2043
  content_chars=2 content_preview='[]'`. Read precisely rather than pattern-matched onto a prior
  theory: `done_reason='stop'` (not `"length"`) proves generation was NOT cut off by the context
  window; `prompt_eval_count=2043` confirms the full, untruncated, correct 1423-char receipt text
  reached the model (matches the independently-recomputed expected token count from two entries
  ago); `eval_count=2` means the model generated exactly two output tokens -- it didn't attempt the
  task and fail partway, it answered immediately with the prompt's own literal escape-hatch text,
  `[]`. This ruled out truncation, parsing, thinking-routing, and sampling-parameter mismatch all at
  once -- every prior fix in this saga was independently correct and is still needed, none of them
  caused this. To find the actual cause without guessing again, ran a controlled live experiment:
  wrote a script (reusing the app's own `ollama_client.chat`, so its existing logging did the
  measuring) that sent the SAME real receipt text through two calls with IDENTICAL, already-corrected
  sampling options -- Test A used the current full `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT` (7723 chars) as a control,
  Test B used a deliberately minimal ~1800-char prompt with the same task. The author ran it against
  their real container. Result: Test A reproduced `[]`/`eval_count=2` exactly again; Test B
  immediately began extracting real items (`eval_count=107`, real JSON with real item names). This is
  as close to a proof as this investigation gets: the PROMPT ITSELF, not any code path, was causing
  this specific 9B model to bail out via its own documented "if truly nothing is food, respond with
  `[]`" escape hatch rather than attempt a task it apparently judged too complex to execute. One
  important nuance the same A/B surfaced and did NOT ignore: Test B's minimal prompt also let a
  "Purina Friskies ... Wet Cat Food" line through despite being told to skip "pet" items -- proving
  prompt brevity has a real accuracy cost, not just a free win. Rewrote `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT`
  accordingly: cut the two additions from the earlier "4 of 8 items" fix that are also the two
  sections absent from the minimal prompt that worked -- a "MANY lines... do not stop early... do not
  summarize" meta-paragraph and a "before responding, double check" pre-response checklist -- folding
  the completeness requirement into a single clause in the main instruction instead. Kept the detailed
  non-food exclusion list and field-level (quantity/unit/price/date) guidance fully intact, since the
  cat-food miss shows THAT detail earns its keep. Net result: prompt template shrank from 6307 to 3629
  characters (~42% shorter), rendered-with-real-content from 7724 to 5046 chars -- a reasoned point
  between the two empirically-tested extremes (1800 under-filtering, 7723 bailing out entirely), not
  itself a third tested data point. One test's premise no longer held (the removed meta-paragraph) and
  was replaced with an equivalent-intent assertion against the new phrasing rather than deleted
  outright; the anti-merge test needed no change since its replacement phrasing still satisfies the
  original assertion. 535 backend tests passing (same count -- one test swapped, not added/removed).
  **Not yet verified live**: this exact ~5000-char rendered length hasn't itself been tested against
  the real model -- worth the author retrying the same real PDF import after this deploys and sharing
  the `[ollama_client]` log line one more time, this time hopefully to confirm success rather than
  diagnose failure.

- **2026-08-02**: Correction to the receipt-import sampling-options fix (two entries below this
  one), same day, prompted directly by the author pulling real data from their own Ollama
  container rather than accepting another guess. The prior fix set `temperature=0.1` on the
  unverified assumption that a lower temperature makes a local model more likely to mechanically
  finish a long enumeration -- that assumption was never checked against this model's actual
  documentation before shipping. The author ran `docker exec ollama ollama show qwen3.5:9b` and
  shared the real output: this model's baked-in default parameters are `temperature=1,
  presence_penalty=1.5, top_p=0.95, top_k=20`, plus `context length 262144` (far above this app's
  configured `num_ctx`, confirming num_ctx was never the ceiling for this specific model). Cross-
  checked against Qwen's own official documentation (Hugging Face model card, Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B,
  fetched live) rather than assumed: for non-thinking general tasks, Qwen recommends exactly
  `temperature=0.7, top_p=0.8, top_k=20, presence_penalty=1.5`. Two corrections follow: (1)
  `presence_penalty=1.5` is NOT an aggressive/unusual value some community Modelfile added --
  it's Qwen's own documented recommendation for this model in this mode, so an earlier internal
  suspicion that it was fighting the receipt-import task (raised while planning this fix, never
  shipped) was itself wrong and is retracted here explicitly rather than silently dropped. (2) The
  actual bug was overriding ONLY `temperature` down to 0.1 while leaving `presence_penalty` at its
  baked-in 1.5 -- a combination Qwen never tested or recommends (not their general-task preset of
  0.7/1.5, not their reasoning-task preset of 1.0/2.0). A strong presence penalty discourages
  reusing tokens already generated (relevant here: repeated JSON schema keys once per item, and
  any receipt with two lines of the same product); at near-greedy temperature the model has little
  room left to pick a good alternative once its preferred token is penalized -- a plausible
  mechanism for degenerate or incomplete output, compounding rather than fixing the original
  4-of-8-items symptom. Fixed by setting `_RECEIPT_EXTRA_OPTIONS` to Qwen's own tested general-
  task recommendation explicitly (`temperature=0.7, top_p=0.8, top_k=20, presence_penalty=1.5`)
  rather than trusting this Ollama library build's own baked-in defaults, which may not be
  identical across quantizations/rebuilds. Updated the one test whose premise (temperature must be
  "meaningfully lower" than default) was itself the wrong assumption, rather than leaving a
  passing test locking in an already-retracted idea. 535 backend tests passing (no count change --
  same test, corrected). Not yet verified live: the author's `docker compose logs backend --since
  30m | grep ...` came back with NO `[ollama_client]`/`[inventory.` lines, meaning no receipt-
  import attempt has actually been logged against the fixed code yet -- worth retrying the same
  import after this deploys and sharing those log lines plus the text behind "Show raw AI
  response" for the first genuinely fresh, evidence-backed read of this exact model's real output.

- **2026-08-02**: Fresh-eyes review of the receipt-import "0 items" saga (fifth round; see the six entries below for the full history) after the author explicitly asked for a different model and said, correctly, that iterative guessing had run out of road. Deliberately did NOT change the prompt or the sampling options again -- the one thing that materially changed this round is that the symptom itself carries new information: the "Show raw AI response (for troubleshooting)" link was VISIBLE this time, and that link is rendered only when `raw_model_output` is truthy (verified by reading `InventoryPage.jsx:755` directly rather than taking the prior session's summary on faith). So the model DID answer, non-empty, and `inventory_service.parse_vision_response` turned that answer into zero items. Read the whole path again from scratch (`RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT`, `ollama_client.chat/describe_image/extract_content/_log_response`, `parse_vision_response/_extract_json_array`) and found ONE genuine, reproducible, evidence-independent bug that fits that exact signature: `_extract_json_array`'s fallback was `re.search(r"\[.*\]", raw_text, re.DOTALL)` -- a GREEDY match, spanning the FIRST `[` anywhere in the response to the LAST `]` anywhere in the response. Any square bracket outside the real JSON array silently corrupts that slice into unparseable JSON and the function returns `[]`, which the UI reports as "0 items identified" -- indistinguishable, to the user, from a receipt that genuinely had no food on it. Demonstrated (not theorized) by running the ACTUAL old function against real model output shapes: a correct array followed by `Note: I skipped the non-food lines [paper towels, lint roller].` returned 0 items; a lead-in of `Here are the items [from the receipt]:` returned 0 items; an inline `<think>` trace mentioning `lines [1-15]` returned 0 items; a second array of excluded lines later in the response returned 0 items; and an array truncated mid-generation (what a run that hits the context/`num_predict` limit looks like, `done_reason` "length") returned 0 items even though every element before the cut was complete and perfectly usable. Note that the previous round's own changes make several of these MORE likely, not less: the prompt now spends a long paragraph enumerating non-food categories to skip, which invites exactly the trailing "here's what I skipped" commentary that breaks the greedy regex. Replaced the regex with a real string-aware bracket-matching scan (`_scan_json_array`) that walks each `[` in order, respects quoting/escaping so a bracket inside a `confidence_note` value can't throw off the depth count, and takes the first candidate that actually parses as a list; added `_strip_reasoning` to drop an inline `<think>...</think>` block or a bare orphan `</think>` prefix before parsing at all (the `think=False` flag is only honored by a new enough Ollama server/model template, so an inline trace is not a hypothetical); and added salvage of a TRUNCATED array, so a response cut off mid-list now returns the items it did complete instead of nothing (the same path incidentally recovers a trailing comma before `]`, another routine small-model JSON slip). A response that is genuinely just `[]` still means "no food on this receipt" and is still honored as such. Also added one targeted diagnostic that the existing logging could not provide: `_inventory_import_job` now logs the raw response's head AND tail when a non-empty response parses to zero items -- `ollama_client`'s response log already shows the first 300 chars of content, but it is the END of the response that distinguishes "cut off mid-array" from "complete JSON wrapped in prose," and that distinction is the whole question. 12 new tests, one per real failure shape, all of which fail against the old greedy regex; 535 total backend tests passing (was 523), run in chunks since this sandbox's bash calls each get their own PID namespace and can't hold a long-running background process. No frontend changes. **Explicitly NOT claimed as confirmed**: this is a real bug that produces the exact reported symptom, but no live Ollama reaches this sandbox, so it is not proven to be THE cause of the author's specific failure. Two other candidates remain open and are only distinguishable from the real raw response text: (a) the response was truncated because `temperature=0.1` (added last round) is close to greedy decoding, which Qwen 3's own model card explicitly warns can cause endless repetition -- if so, the salvage path above will now recover most items but the temperature should be reconsidered, since Qwen's documented non-thinking recommendation is ~0.7/top_p 0.8/top_k 20; (b) the model genuinely returned `[]`, i.e. a prompt-adherence problem rather than a parsing one, in which case the raw response is literally two characters. Both are settled in one round trip by the author pasting the text behind the "Show raw AI response" link plus `docker compose logs backend --tail 200`, and by A/B-ing the same prompt against their Ollama container at temperature 0.1 vs 0.7.

- **2026-08-02**: Two direct author follow-ups on the SAME PDF/search after the thinking-mode and 406 fixes above deployed -- both root-caused with real evidence (either extracting the actual PDF locally, or reading OSM's own live wiki) rather than guessed. (1) Receipt import now populated content correctly (`think=False` fix confirmed working) but only identified 4 of 8 real food items from the author's actual 15-line Walmart receipt. Rather than assume a budget/truncation regression, extracted that EXACT PDF's text locally using this app's own `recipe_service.extract_pdf_text` and confirmed the full, correct 1423-character text -- all 8 food items, including a second "Progresso Gluten Free..." line for a different soup flavor -- was genuinely present in what reached the model, nowhere near the ~13,000-char budget. This ruled out a code bug entirely: the model itself, given the complete correct input, stopped partway through a long list and also appears to have treated the repeated "Progresso Gluten Free" product name as an already-covered duplicate rather than two separate purchased lines. This is a real, known local-model completeness limitation on long enumerations, not a truncation bug -- fixed with two changes: (a) explicit new prompt instructions telling the model to evaluate every single line without stopping early and to never merge two distinct lines sharing a product name, placed both near the top (primacy) and as a final pre-response checklist item right before the JSON schema (recency, which tends to weigh more for smaller local models); (b) a new `extra_options` parameter threaded through `ollama_client.chat()`/`describe_image()` (merged with `num_ctx`, never silently overwritten) so a caller can request a lower `temperature` for more mechanically complete extraction without affecting other callers like meal planning that genuinely benefit from creativity -- applied to both receipt intake paths (text/PDF and photo) at `temperature=0.1`. (2) Dining Out's "Look up" failed again, this time with a genuinely different error than before: `504 Gateway Timeout` from overpass-api.de itself (not a 406 -- the User-Agent fix above is confirmed working), meaning the free public Overpass instance was itself overloaded, not a bug in this app's request. Fetched OSM's own wiki live (not guessed) to find a real, currently-listed, global-coverage, no-rate-limit public mirror (`overpass.private.coffee`, formerly kumi.systems) and added automatic fallback: `search_nearby_restaurants` now tries the primary instance first, falling back to the mirror ONLY on a 5xx/timeout/connection failure -- explicitly NOT on a 4xx, since a malformed request would fail identically on the mirror and retrying would just delay surfacing a real bug. 15 new/updated tests (12 for the receipt prompt/temperature fix, 5 replacing/extending the Overpass header test with scripted-fallback scenarios covering 5xx-triggers-fallback, 4xx-does-not, timeout-triggers-fallback, and both-fail-raises) -- 523 total backend tests passing. Not verified: a live retry against the author's actual Ollama instance and a live Overpass/mirror call from the author's real network -- worth the author retrying both the receipt import and a Dining Out search after this deploys.

- **2026-08-02**: CONFIRMED ROOT CAUSE and real fix for the receipt-import "0 items" saga (see the four entries below this one for the full history of dead-end/partial fixes that preceded it) -- resolved using real evidence for the first time, exactly as the diagnostic-logging entry below committed to doing. The author ran the diagnostic build and provided real `docker compose logs backend` output; it showed a `chat()` call completing with NO exception and full normal metadata (`done_reason`, `eval_count`, `prompt_eval_count` all present -- proof tokens WERE generated) yet `message.content` was an empty string (`content_chars=0 content_preview=''`). Rather than guess at what that meant, fetched Ollama's own docs live (docs.ollama.com/capabilities/thinking): "Thinking is enabled by default in the CLI and API for supported models," and Qwen 3 is explicitly listed as one of those models -- this app's default `ollama_chat_model` setting is `qwen3.5:9b`, same family. A thinking-enabled model can route its entire answer into a separate `message.thinking` field instead of `message.content`, which exactly explains the observed symptom: a fully successful call whose real answer never landed where every part of this app was looking for it. Confirmed (not assumed) that the previously-pinned `ollama==0.3.3` client predates the `think` request parameter needed to disable this, by fetching ollama-python's actual `_client.py`/`_types.py` source directly from GitHub and comparing against `pip index versions ollama` (latest available: 0.6.2, which does support `think`). Fixed by bumping `requirements.txt` to `ollama==0.6.2` and passing `think=False` on every `chat()`/`describe_image()` call in `ollama_client.py` -- this app has no UI for showing a reasoning trace, so there's no reason to pay the extra generation time for one, and disabling it removes the failure mode entirely rather than working around it. Caught an important SECOND landmine the same research surfaced before it could bite: on ollama==0.3.3, `client.chat()` returns a plain dict; on ollama>=0.4, it returns pydantic `ChatResponse`/`Message` objects instead (confirmed by reading `_types.py`'s `SubscriptableBaseModel` directly). Every one of this app's Ollama call sites -- 13 in total, across `routers/inventory.py` (x3), `routers/recipes.py` (x2), `routers/chat.py`, `routers/health.py`, `routers/meal_plan.py`, `services/recipe_service.py` (x2), `services/health_service.py`, plus `ollama_client.py`'s own diagnostic logging -- had copy-pasted `response.get("message", {}).get("content", "") if isinstance(response, dict) else str(response)`, a check that would have silently started returning `str(<the whole pydantic response object>)` instead of the real answer at every single one of them the moment the version bump landed, since `isinstance(ChatResponse(...), dict)` is `False`. Replaced all of them with one new `ollama_client.extract_content()` helper, duck-typed on `.get()` (both dict and `SubscriptableBaseModel` support it) so it works correctly for both the old and new client versions, tested once centrally instead of copy-pasted and untested at 13 call sites. Also rewrote `_log_response` the same way, and it now additionally surfaces `done_reason`, `eval_count`, `prompt_eval_count`, and -- the specific new field that would have caught this immediately -- `thinking_chars`/a short `thinking_preview`, so a future thinking-model regression would be instantly visible instead of looking identical to a genuinely empty response. Verified the whole chain: fresh venv install of `ollama==0.6.2` (had to work around two sandbox-only artifacts -- a stale/permission-locked venv from an earlier session, fixed by creating a new one; and this sandbox's own outbound SOCKS proxy env vars breaking the new client's eager module-level `Client()` instantiation at import time, unrelated to the real Docker deployment, worked around by unsetting them only for the local test run), confirmed `think` is a real accepted keyword on the installed 0.6.2 via `inspect.signature`, then ran the full backend suite in batches: 513 tests passing, including 9 new/updated tests in `test_ollama_client.py` locking down `think=False` being sent, `extract_content()` handling both a plain dict and a fake pydantic-shaped object correctly, and -- most importantly -- a dedicated test reproducing the exact bug (a mocked response with empty `content` but non-empty `thinking`) and asserting the log now clearly shows `thinking_chars=170` instead of looking like silence. Also fixed, same session, a second real author-reported bug from the same batch of screenshots: Dining Out's "Look up" button failed with `502 ... Overpass API: Client error 406 Not Acceptable`. Root cause: `dining_service.search_nearby_restaurants`'s POST to `overpass-api.de` sent no headers at all, so httpx's own generic default User-Agent got rejected -- overpass-api.de enforces the same identifying-User-Agent expectation Nominatim's documented usage policy already required (and this app's `_nominatim_headers()` already honored for Nominatim specifically, just never applied to Overpass). Renamed to a shared `_osm_headers()` and applied it to both calls; added a new test that fakes `httpx.AsyncClient` to assert the real header is actually present on the outgoing Overpass request, closing the gap that let this ship unnoticed (the previous test coverage only exercised the two pure halves, never the actual HTTP call). Clean frontend build not re-run (no frontend changes this round). Not verified: a live Ollama call against the author's actual `qwen3.5:9b` (or whatever model they've since configured) to confirm `content` now populates correctly with `think=False` set, and a live Overpass request from the author's real network -- worth the author retrying both the receipt import and a Dining Out search after this deploys.

- **2026-08-02**: The receipt-import "0 items" bug (see the num_ctx and content-budget entries below) was STILL happening after both of those fixes deployed -- the author, rightly out of patience with three straight rounds of unverified guessing, asked directly what evidence would actually resolve it. Correct move made explicitly: stopped guessing and got real evidence instead. First checked this app's OWN code against the actually-installed `ollama==0.3.3` package's real source (`inspect.getsource`, not assumption) to rule out a self-inflicted bug in the num_ctx change -- confirmed `options={"num_ctx": N}` is a legitimate, correctly-typed call for this exact client version, and confirmed `Client._request` raises `ResponseError` on any non-2xx HTTP status, which `job_queue._worker` already catches and surfaces as `importJob.error` in the UI. Cross-checked against the author's own screenshot: no error banner was shown, AND -- a detail worth deriving, not guessing -- the "Show raw AI response" debug link (added two rounds ago specifically for this) was ABSENT this time, where it had been present in an earlier failed attempt. Since that link only renders when `raw_model_output` is truthy, this means the job completed successfully with a literal empty string response, not an exception and not a non-empty-but-unparseable one -- a materially different failure mode than anything reasoned about so far, and impossible to know for certain from outside this sandbox without real logs. Root gap found: this app has ZERO logging anywhere, on any Ollama call, in its whole history -- every debugging round so far had no way to see what Ollama actually received or returned. Added print()-based diagnostics (matching this codebase's own established docker-logs-visible convention from `run_server.py`, not the stdlib `logging` module, to avoid any risk of uvicorn's logging config silently swallowing it) at every layer of the receipt-import path: `ollama_client.chat()`/`describe_image()` now log the model/num_ctx/prompt length going out and the response's keys/content-length/a short preview coming back (or the exception, if one occurs) for EVERY AI call in the app, not just receipt import; `inventory.py`'s `_receipt_text_extraction` logs the extracted PDF text's length vs. the computed budget and whether truncation happened; `_inventory_import_job` logs the raw output length against how many items it actually parsed into. 10 new tests (505 total backend tests passing) verify these fire with the right content in every real scenario this investigation surfaced: a normal response, an empty-content response (the exact reported symptom), an exception, and even an unexpected non-dict response type -- so the fix itself doesn't crash while trying to explain a crash. Clean frontend build (no frontend changes). **What's actually needed now, stated plainly rather than promising another guess**: the author retries the same import once this deploys, then runs `docker compose logs backend --tail 100` (or similar) and shares the `[ollama_client]`/`[inventory.` lines from that attempt -- this will show, for the first time, real ground truth (empty response vs. malformed JSON vs. something else) instead of reasoning about token math from outside the system. Deliberately did NOT ship a fourth speculative "fix" this round -- the honest, load-bearing deliverable here is visibility, not another guess.

- **2026-08-02**: Direct author follow-up question to the num_ctx fix above: "will this be a problem when uploading recipe files, which can be larger than simple receipts?" -- answered by actually checking the numbers rather than reassuring on vibes, and found a real, separate, PRE-EXISTING truncation limit the num_ctx fix hadn't touched: every AI-import prompt (receipt, recipe, bloodwork) hard-capped its raw extracted content at a flat `content[:8000]` characters, completely independent of `num_ctx` -- not coincidentally close to Ollama's OLD 2048-token default (8000 chars / ~4 chars/token), but never actually tied to it, just a leftover guess. Confirmed via `len()` on the rendered prompts that this flat cap was, if anything, backwards for the author's specific question: `RECIPE_IMPORT_PROMPT` traced through `routers/recipes.py` confirmed the AI-prompt path (where this cap applies) is a fallback ONLY for sources with no schema.org JSON-LD -- per B9.3's own JSON-LD-first import order -- meaning it's exactly the messiest sources (a photo, a PDF, pasted text, or a site with no structured markup) that hit this path, and a real recipe blog page's SEO/life-story prose commonly runs well past 8000 characters even after trafilatura extraction. Fixed with a new `ollama_client.content_char_budget()` helper that computes a real character budget FROM the configured `ollama_num_ctx`, minus the calling prompt's own instructional overhead and a response-generation reserve (tuned per prompt -- 3000 tokens for receipts, which can have dozens of line items each needing real JSON space; 2500 for recipes; 1500 for bloodwork) -- replacing the flat `content[:8000]` in all four call sites (`inventory.py`'s receipt import, `recipes.py`'s URL/text import, `recipe_service.py`'s file/folder import, `health_service.py`'s bloodwork import). Verified with real numbers at the new 8192 default: recipe imports now get 18,117 usable characters (was 8000, more than double), bloodwork gets 22,068, receipts get 12,940 -- a direct, concrete answer to the author's question, not just "should be fine now." 5 new tests (497 total backend tests passing) confirming the budget scales proportionally with `num_ctx`, reserves room correctly for overhead/response, and never collapses to zero even under an extreme reserve. No frontend changes. Not verified: real-world behavior against a live model (no Ollama reaches this sandbox).

- **2026-08-02**: Direct author follow-up after deploying the previous fix -- receipt import now returned ZERO items instead of a partial result. Researched (web search) rather than guessed: confirmed Ollama's own default context window is 2048 tokens, and critically, it does NOT error when a prompt exceeds that -- it silently clips the overflow with no warning at all. Checked `ollama_client.py` and confirmed every call in this app (`chat()`, `describe_image()`) has ALWAYS omitted `options`/`num_ctx`, relying entirely on Ollama's bare default. The previous round's fix nearly doubled `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT`'s length (~1300+ tokens of instructions alone, before the receipt's own text) -- comfortably capable of pushing a real import past 2048 with the actual receipt content silently dropped, which would produce exactly the observed symptom (a clean, error-free "0 items" rather than a crash). This is a systemic, standing risk that predates this session -- every Ollama-calling feature in the app (recipe import, meal planning, chat, knowledge grounding) shares the same unset-num_ctx exposure, not just receipt import; this round's growth just happened to be what finally tipped it over. Fixed with a new GUI-editable `ollama_num_ctx` setting (Settings > AI Models, default 8192, `OLLAMA_NUM_CTX` env-fallback for first-run seeding) and a new `ollama_client._num_ctx()` helper (floors at Ollama's own 2048 default, falls back to 8192 on a garbage value) wired into both `chat()` and `describe_image()`'s `options` argument. Deliberately left `embed()` alone -- knowledge-file text is already explicitly chunked before embedding (`knowledge_service.chunk_text`), so it isn't exposed to this same unbounded-prompt risk. 6 new tests mocking `ollama.Client` to assert the real call arguments (not just "doesn't crash") -- confirms `options={"num_ctx": N}` actually reaches the underlying client for both chat and vision calls, plus the setting's default/override/garbage-value/floor behavior (493 total backend tests passing). Clean `vite build` (306 modules) for the one-line Settings-tab-bucket addition. Not verified: whether this was actually the true root cause of the "0 items" report, since no live Ollama reaches this sandbox to confirm -- but it's a real, independently-justified fix regardless (a silent 2048-token clip with zero error surfaced is a legitimate standing bug on its own merits), and directly explains the reported symptom. Worth the author re-running the same PDF import after this deploys to confirm.

- **2026-08-02**: Direct author bug report with a real Walmart order PDF and its actual import result attached: the receipt importer correctly identified 8 food items and their purchase quantities, but never filled in the purchase date, price, or unit columns even though all three are printed right on the receipt. Root-caused against the real PDF text (extracted and read directly) rather than guessed, and found TWO separate, compounding bugs, not one: (1) `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT`'s JSON schema never mentioned "purchased_date" or "unit_price" as fields at all -- the model was never even asked for them, despite `VisionDetectedItem` and the frontend's review table both already supporting these exact fields (populated only by the B10.3 order-history CSV importer until now). (2) `inventory_service.parse_vision_response` -- the shared parser behind every AI-driven import path -- never read those two keys out of the model's JSON either, so even a model that volunteered them would have had them silently dropped before reaching the frontend. Fixed both: extended the prompt's schema and added explicit date/price extraction guidance (a receipt's single order-date header applies to every line; a line's own printed price is the *whole-quantity* price, matching this app's already-established `unit_price` semantics from B6.1, not a re-derived per-unit figure), and added `unit_price`/`purchased_date` (new `_safe_iso_date` helper, never raises on a malformed date) to the parser's output dict. Along the way, found and fixed a THIRD, more subtle bug by manually tracing the actual attached PDF's text against what the screenshot showed: for 3 of the 8 rows (hot dogs, bacon, crackers) the model had pulled a number OUT OF THE PRODUCT'S OWN NAME -- "6" from "...6 Count", "24" from "...24 oz", "7" from "...7 oz." -- and used it as `estimated_quantity` instead of the receipt's actual "Qty 1" for those lines, while leaving `unit` null every time instead of capturing that size descriptor there. Added explicit prompt guidance distinguishing "how many were purchased" from "the package's own size/count descriptor," framed with this app's own pre-existing "never invent a conversion" principle (already documented on `BarcodeLookupResponse.quantity_text` -- "a '500 g' bag is 1 item, not 500," directly analogous to "a '6 Count' hot-dog package is 1 item, not 6"). Verified end to end with a hand-built, prompt-compliant model response run through the real parser and confirmed it reproduces every line of the actual receipt correctly (quantity, unit, price, and date all matching). 6 new/updated backend tests (487 total passing), no frontend changes needed at all -- the review table was already reading `unit_price`/`purchased_date` correctly; the entire bug was a backend extraction gap. Not verified: a live Ollama vision/chat call actually following the new instructions (no live model reaches this sandbox) -- worth the author re-running the same PDF to confirm.

- **2026-08-02**: Two direct author UX bug reports from an Inventory page screenshot. (1) The four toolbar panel-toggle buttons ("+ Add item", "Import receipt/list", "Import order history", "Scan barcode") all swapped their label to a bare, identical "Close" while their panel was open -- with more than one panel open at once (each toggles independently) the toolbar showed several context-free "Close" buttons in a row with no way to tell which one belonged to which panel. Fixed by keeping each button's own distinct label at all times and adding a highlighted `.btn-toggle-active` "pressed" style plus `aria-pressed` instead -- click the same (now-highlighted) button again to close that specific panel. (2) The "Import a receipt or list" card's "Parse text" button and its file-upload button both showed "Parsing..." simultaneously no matter which one was actually clicked, since both derived their busy label from the same shared `importJob` background-job state with no way to tell which action triggered it -- looked like two independent things were parsing at once. Fixed with a new lightweight `importTrigger` state ("text"/"file") set at the moment each action starts, so only the button that was actually clicked shows the busy/"Parsing..." label; the other stays disabled (a second import genuinely can't start mid-job either way) but keeps its normal resting label rather than a misleading one. Frontend-only change, clean `vite build` (306 modules), no backend changes.

- **2026-08-02**: Direct author bug report after deploying the GPS/IP-location round above: Dining Out's search form rejected the "Search radius (km)" field with a native "enter a valid value" error despite a number clearly being present. Root cause was a classic HTML5 `<input type="number">` gotcha, not a real validation failure -- the field had `min="0.1"` and `step="0.5"`, and the browser's native step validation only accepts values reachable as `min + n*step` (0.1, 0.6, 1.1, ...4.6, 5.1...); the field's own default value of `5` was never actually on that list, so submitting with the untouched default (or several other ordinary round numbers) failed silently-to-the-user native validation. Fixed by changing `min` to `0.5` so every half-kilometer round number lines up with the step grid; also grepped every other `type="number"` field in the app for the same min+fractional-step combination and confirmed this was the only one -- everywhere else either uses `step="any"` (immune to this class of bug) or no step at all. Frontend-only change, clean `vite build` (306 modules), no backend tests affected.

- **2026-08-02**: Direct author follow-up after retesting the prior "use my location" fix on the real iPad: the stuck-forever hang was resolved, but two real gaps remained -- it still didn't use the iPad's actual GPS chip, and there was no IP-based fallback option. Root cause of the GPS gap: the prior fix hardcoded `getCurrentPosition`'s `enableHighAccuracy: false` on the theory that a WiFi-only iPad has no GPS hardware and forcing high accuracy would just make the hang-forever risk worse -- true for WiFi-only hardware, but wrong for a GPS/cellular-capable iPad (the author's actual device), since `enableHighAccuracy: false` tells iOS Core Location a coarse WiFi/cell-tower fix is acceptable even when a real GPS fix is available. Fixed `DiningPage.jsx`'s `useMyLocation` to try a real GPS-accuracy fix first (own 20s timeout, so the original hang bug can't recur), falling back automatically to network-based positioning only if that attempt errors or times out (skipping the retry entirely on a denied-permission error, since accuracy options can't change that outcome) -- works correctly for both GPS-capable and WiFi-only hardware without guessing which the user has, and now surfaces the browser-reported accuracy radius in the status line. Separately added the requested IP-based option as a genuine third choice alongside GPS and manual address/zip: new `dining_service.geolocate_by_ip()` against ipwho.is (free, keyless, verified live via `web_fetch` since the sandbox's own outbound IP gets 403'd by it the same way Nominatim does -- documented, standing sandbox-only limitation, not a code defect), new `GET /api/dining/geolocate-by-ip` endpoint, and a new "Use approximate location" button on `DiningPage.jsx`. Important, explicitly documented caveat: this geolocates the BACKEND's own outbound IP, not the browsing device -- for this app's normal self-hosted-on-the-home-network deployment (backend and household on the same connection, no reverse proxy) that's a genuinely useful city-level fix, but would silently report the server's location instead of the user's if ever accessed remotely; the frontend labels it "approximate (network-based)" rather than implying device-level precision. 5 new backend tests for `parse_ip_geolocation_response` (483 total passing), clean `vite build` (306 modules). Not verified: real GPS behavior and accuracy on the author's actual iPad, and a live call to ipwho.is from the household's real network (only verified reachable via `web_fetch`, not via the backend's own `httpx`, in this sandbox).

- **2026-08-02**: Completed B7.4 (accessibility pass) plus two author-reported items from the same compound message -- a Walmart PDF receipt import producing zero visible results/feedback, and a request for address/zip-code geocoding on Dining Out alongside the existing manual lat/lon and still-unreliable "use my location." See "B7.4 notes" section above for full detail on all three. Highlights: fixed a genuine silent-failure bug (`InventoryPage.jsx`'s import-confirm handlers had no error handling at all, so any failure did nothing visible -- exactly the reported symptom), tightened `RECEIPT_IMPORT_PROMPT` to actually exclude non-food purchases, added item-count feedback and a raw-AI-output debug view; added `dining_service.geocode()` against free/keyless OSM Nominatim with a new Dining Out address/zip field; ran a real WCAG contrast-ratio audit across all 9 themes (26 failures found, 22 fixed via new `--color-on-primary/secondary/danger` tokens, 4 left alone as they're official Catppuccin brand colors), added a global `:focus-visible` outline, converted the one non-semantic clickable element in the app (`RecipeChat.jsx`'s header) to a real `<button>`, and added accessible names to roughly a dozen previously-bare form fields across 8 files. 478 backend tests passing (5 new for geocoding, 1 new for the prompt fix), clean `vite build`. Not verified: on-screen appearance, real screen-reader behavior, and the author's own Walmart PDF against a live Ollama vision model -- none reach this sandbox. "Use my location" itself was not re-investigated this round; the author's own wording suggests it may not yet be retested since the prior fix.

- **2026-08-02**: Author-requested follow-up to B15.1: the frontend now also auto-redirects its plain-HTTP port to HTTPS once a certificate is active, closing a gap that was originally shipped as a documented, deliberate simplification ("`serve` has no built-in way to do that, and writing a second Node listener wasn't worth it for a one-time bookmark update"). New `frontend/redirect-server.js` -- a minimal, dependency-free Node `http` listener (no Express) that 307-redirects every request on `FRONTEND_PORT` to the same path on `FRONTEND_HTTPS_PORT`, mirroring the backend's own redirect listener in `run_server.py` (same 307-not-301/308 reasoning: HTTPS can be turned back off, and a browser-cached permanent redirect would keep bouncing to a dead port forever after that). `docker-entrypoint.sh`'s supervisor loop now starts/stops this alongside the main `serve` HTTPS process and independently restarts just the redirect listener (not the whole server) if only it dies. Updated the WIKI's HTTPS entry and `docker-compose.yml`'s comments to drop the now-resolved "known simplification" language. Verified with a real functional dry run (real `serve` binary + a real openssl-generated cert + real `curl`): confirmed the exact expected `307` with a `Location: https://<host>:<https-port>/<original-path>` header, path preserved. Frontend build clean. Config-only + two small new/changed files, no backend changes, so the full backend test suite wasn't re-run for this round.

- **2026-08-02**: Fixed a real, author-reported live regression from the previous session's B15.1 work: a custom `BACKEND_PORT` (the author has one set, since the default 8095 was already taken on their host -- a scenario this project already hit and documented once before, 2026-07-31) silently broke every single API call with a browser-level "Failed to fetch" and zero trace in the container's own logs. Root cause, confirmed against the author's own `docker compose logs` output rather than guessed: B15.1's new `run_server.py` binds the container's OWN internal uvicorn listener to whatever `BACKEND_PORT`/`BACKEND_HTTPS_PORT` says, replacing the old Dockerfile `CMD` that always hardcoded `--port 8095` regardless of the env var -- but `docker-compose.yml`'s port mapping still hardcoded the container (right-hand) side to the literal default, so a custom port meant Docker forwarded `host:<custom port>` to a container port nothing was actually listening on. The exact same latent bug existed for the frontend's `FRONTEND_PORT`/`FRONTEND_HTTPS_PORT` too (same B15.1 `docker-entrypoint.sh` rewrite), just not yet triggered since the author hadn't customized those. Fixed all four `docker-compose.yml` port-mapping lines to use the same env var on both sides of the `:` rather than a literal default on the container side. Also traced back and correctly attributed the author's earlier same-session "failed to fetch" report on the Walmart-PDF receipt import to this identical root cause (not a separate bug in the receipt-import code path, as originally hypothesized before this port mismatch was found) -- every backend API call was failing the same way, the receipt import just happened to be the first one tried. No application code changed, config-only fix; `docker compose up -d` (no rebuild) picks it up once pulled.

Pull what references you need from Fiduciary and implement HTTPS support now. Also, go ahead and implement the next item on the project list." See "B15.1 / B4.3 notes" above for full detail. B15.1 ported Fiduciary's `tls.py`/`run_server.py` cert-management pattern, adapted for Chef's two-container (not single-container) architecture via a shared `chef-tls` volume and independently-restarting backend/frontend supervisors -- new Settings > Security "Certificate" card and a new WIKI "HTTPS / secure context" entry covering the two-origin trust gotcha. Getting `unit_price`'s exact semantics right mattered: it's the price paid for a row's WHOLE quantity as purchased, not a per-unit price, so the real per-unit signal is `unit_price / quantity`, converted into the requested unit via the existing `unit_conversion_service` when units differ. New `GET /recipes/{id}/cost` and `GET /meal-plans/{plan_id}/grocery-list/cost` endpoints, each reporting a computed/partial/no_data provenance plus a per-ingredient breakdown with an explicit note for every unresolved line (same honesty discipline as B1.2's nutrition provenance) -- projected spend excludes already-purchased grocery items. Verified: 4 new unit tests (248 total, all passing) plus a live end-to-end pass running the real `python -m app.seed` entrypoint twice against a fresh temp DB (5 rows created inactive with real content on the first run, nothing duplicated on the second, on-disk copies confirmed present). Not verified: on-screen appearance (no new UI was added by this item, so the existing Knowledge Files page is exercised as-is, same standing limitation as every other UI-facing item this project).

- **2026-08-01**: Completed B2.2 and B5.1 (see their notes sections above), the two backlog items picked as "the top 2 most difficult or long running tasks" per the author's explicit request to make real unattended progress while stepping away for the evening -- both stayed within the already-connected Chef folder and only needed web search/fetch for methodology research, so no new permissions were requested or needed. B2.2 (HEI-2020-inspired diet-quality score): fetched the real USDA/HHS scoring-standards table live rather than working from memory, then found two genuine, load-bearing data gaps that made a literal implementation impossible honestly -- Refined Grains/Added Sugars need data this app doesn't have (excluded from the score, not faked), and the 9 adequacy components need a USDA Food Patterns Equivalents crosswalk this app doesn't have (substituted with a documented, approximate ingredient-keyword classifier plus averaged reference gram weights). Every approximation is stated in the response's own `methodology` field, and the feature is never presented as "your Healthy Eating Index score" anywhere in the UI. Found and fixed a real classifier bug via a failing test ("whole wheat bread" double-counting toward both whole- and refined-grain groups). B5.1 (leftovers modeling): a single nullable self-referential `MealPlanEntry.leftover_of_entry_id`, set on the later entry pointing back at its cook event's origin -- deliberately not a grouping-table concept the backlog text never actually asked for. Wired into the two real consumers found by rereading the existing code first: grocery-list aggregation (skips a leftover entry's own ingredient contribution) and confirm-time inventory deduction (skips deducting a second time for the same cook event), while deliberately leaving nutrition/diet-quality counting unchanged since a leftover meal is still a meal eaten. Verified both features with real, hand-checkable live numbers against a running local server (grocery totals dropping from 4x to exactly 3x base quantities once linked; inventory deducting once at the origin and staying unchanged on the leftover's own confirm) rather than relying on unit tests in isolation. 21 new backend unit tests total (244 in the repo, all passing), clean frontend builds for both features (worked around the FUSE `dist/`-cleanup EPERM by symlinking the mounted `node_modules` into a `/tmp` scratch build directory with its own isolated `outDir`, a faster and more reliable variant of the earlier "build from a `/tmp` copy" workaround since it skips the slow/fragile full `node_modules` copy entirely). Not verified: on-screen appearance of either feature's UI (no browser available, the standing limitation on every UI-only change in this project).

- **2026-08-01**: Completed B11.1 (see notes section above), a direct author bug report rather than a backlog pick: uploading a PDF for inventory parsing showed only a text-label change with no other busy indicator, switching tabs and back showed a blank page with no memory of the in-progress parse, and a direct question about whether Chef shared Fiduciary's "one AI task silently kills another" failure mode. Root cause turned out more severe than that framing suggested: several `async def` FastAPI handlers called blocking Ollama/network I/O directly, freezing the ENTIRE app's event loop (every request, not just the one in flight) for the call's full duration -- found by checking each route's actual `def`/`async def` declaration, and, while converting `recipes.import_recipe`, discovering the same bug class also applied to `trafilatura`/`httpx` URL-fetch calls, not just Ollama. Asked the author how far to take the fix (ingestion-only, everything unified, or in between); answer was unambiguous: "comprehensively address this issue now before the project gets bigger." Built a new in-memory serial job queue (`job_queue.py`, architecture ported from Fiduciary's own job-queue solution to the same underlying problem) and converted all six AI-consuming endpoints (inventory vision-intake + import, recipe import + recipe-chat, persistent chat send, meal-plan generation, plus a fire-and-forget variant for knowledge-file indexing) to enqueue instead of blocking. Found and fixed a real, unrelated bug mid-conversion: `chat_about_recipe` had picked up a duplicate stacked route decorator (an editing slip) that would have registered the same endpoint twice. Frontend: a new `useBackgroundJob` hook (localStorage-backed, resumes polling on remount -- the actual fix for the reported blank-page bug) wired into every converted endpoint's UI, plus a new app-wide `JobsBadge` component (mounted outside `<Routes>`, always visible) showing the running job's label and an ETA derived from historical duration. Verified: 9 new backend unit tests (223 total, all passing) against the real background thread, not mocks; a live end-to-end pass proving the exact reported scenario -- a recipe import, a chat send, and a meal-plan generation submitted back-to-back all landed in the same shared queue and processed strictly serially without any one aborting another, draining cleanly back to idle afterward; clean frontend build across all six touched/new files. Also fixed three more `/app`-hardcoded default paths (`DATABASE_URL`, `SECRETS_KEY_FILE`/`SECRETS_KEYRING_FILE`, `SESSION_SECRET_FILE`) discovered while standing up a live server outside Docker for the first time this project -- all were already env-overridable, just never previously exercised that way. Not verified: on-screen appearance (no browser available) and real Ollama generation quality (no live network reaches this sandbox).

- **2026-08-01**: Completed B5.4 (see notes section above), and separately flipped a stale checkbox on B9.6 (repo hygiene): re-checking it found `.gitignore` was already extended to `frontend/dist*/` back in commit `4ea2a1c` on 2026-07-31, alongside other sanity-check fixes that session -- the checkbox itself was just never flipped afterward, no code change was actually needed. Picked B5.4 next since B10 (the author-requested group) was fully closed out by B10.1, and B5.4's own backlog text already called it "low effort, high daily-use payoff" with no external-network dependency. New `meal_plan_service.guess_grocery_category()` -- a keyword-based aisle guesser deliberately held to a lighter standard than `allergen_service`'s matching, since a wrong guess here is a minor inconvenience, not a safety issue -- reuses the same six-value category taxonomy `InventoryItem` already has rather than inventing a separate "aisle" concept. A real inventory-item name match's own category always takes precedence over the guess, confirmed live. `GroceryListPanel.jsx` now groups the list into shop-ordered sections (produce/fridge/freezer/spice/pantry/other/uncategorized), and manual adds gained the same guess-when-blank behavior. Verified: 12 new unit tests (214 total, all passing, including updating two pre-existing tests whose exact-dict-equality assertions needed the new `category` key added rather than left silently broken), a live end-to-end pass confirming every category bucket guesses correctly, the inventory-match-wins precedence, and manual-add's guess-or-override behavior, plus a clean frontend build. Not verified: on-screen appearance.

- **2026-08-01**: Completed B9.1 (see notes section above), picked as the next item once B10.1 closed out the entire author-requested B10 group -- fell back to the B1-B9 backlog and picked the lowest-effort remaining item with no external-network dependency. Found the item's own backlog text was stale (it claimed no `tests/` directory existed at all, when in fact 202 committed tests already existed from every session since B1.1) and corrected it in place rather than leaving a misleading description standing. Added `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (backend pytest job on Python 3.12 matching the Dockerfile's base image, frontend `npm ci`+`vite build` job on Node 20) and a new `backend/requirements-dev.txt` pinning `pytest` out of the production dependency list. Verified what could be verified honestly: YAML validity, an exact package-version match between this sandbox's test venv and `requirements.txt`, and a clean 202/202 pytest run using that exact dependency set. Explicitly could NOT verify a from-scratch `pip install` (this sandbox's `pip` has no PyPI route at all, a new finding -- the standing "no external network" constraint turns out to cover PyPI too, not just Overpass/USDA/Ollama) or a from-scratch `npm ci`+build (the FUSE-mounted working folder blocks removing the existing `node_modules`, so only a reinstall-over-existing and repeated builds against the untouched tree were exercised) -- both noted plainly as unverified rather than assumed fine, worth watching on the workflow's first real run.

- **2026-08-01**: Completed B10.1, the dining-out finder (see notes section above) -- the last remaining B10 (author-requested) item, closing out the whole group. Re-verified OSM's `diet:*` tag coverage live rather than trusting the prior session's note, confirming only 2 of this app's 10 allergen-taxonomy entries (gluten, milk) have any real OSM tag at all; every search result surfaces that gap explicitly (`allergens_with_no_data_source`) rather than treating a missing tag as an implicit all-clear. New `dining_service.py`/`routers/dining.py` query the free, keyless OSM Overpass API and evaluate results against the household's existing B3.1/B3.2 restriction fields, never asserting a restaurant is "safe." Discovered, by reading the existing meal-plan code before building anything, that a recipe-less entry already behaves correctly everywhere (no deduction, excluded from grocery/nutrition aggregation) -- so the only new schema was a purely descriptive `is_eating_out` flag (migration `f3b8d0a6c1e4`) reusing the existing entry-PATCH endpoint, avoiding the new-entry-type the backlog's own text guessed would be needed. Found and fixed two real serialization bugs during live verification that unit tests alone hadn't caught: `create_meal_plan` and the shared `_to_entry_read` response-builder both omitted `is_eating_out` when constructing their respective objects, so the flag silently reported `false` over the API even while the database column held the correct value -- only an actual create-then-fetch-then-PATCH-then-fetch round trip against a running server surfaced this. Frontend: new `DiningPage.jsx` (manual lat/lon plus best-effort geolocation, radius input, honest per-allergen tag rendering, send-to-meal-plan action) and a direct "Eating out instead" toggle on `MealPlanEntryRow.jsx`. Verified: 14 new unit tests (202 total, all passing), the full migration chain applying cleanly, a live end-to-end pass confirming both bugs are fixed and the dining endpoint's error path returns a proper 502 (no live Overpass reaches this sandbox), and a clean frontend build. Not verified: on-screen appearance, and `search_nearby_restaurants`'s real Overpass call, since no live network reaches this sandbox.

- **2026-08-01**: Completed B10.2 (see notes section above), the last tractable B10 item after B10.3 (B10.1, the dining-out finder, needs real design work and live external network services this sandbox can't exercise, so it's the one item left open in this group). Checked with the author directly before building, via AskUserQuestion, whether "mirror Fiduciary" meant literal parity with its multi-user/MFA/recovery-key auth system or the much lighter "optional single-password gating" B9.4's own original text asked for -- confirmed lightweight. Built a session-cookie-based single-shared-password gate (`auth_service.py`, `routers/auth.py`, new `argon2-cffi`/`itsdangerous` dependencies) defaulting OFF, a centralized `auth_gate` ASGI middleware covering every `/api/*` route rather than a per-endpoint dependency (deliberately avoiding the exact "checked on only some endpoints" mistake Fiduciary's own auth.py docstring says its predecessor made), and ported only the genuinely domain-neutral piece (the login rate limiter) near-verbatim. Found and fixed two real bugs while wiring this in, both caught live rather than assumed: an invalid `allow_origins=["*"]` + `allow_credentials=True` CORS combination that would have silently broken session cookies in production (fixed via `allow_origin_regex`), and a Starlette middleware-ordering bug where `SessionMiddleware` needed to be added AFTER `auth_gate`'s own registration, not before, for `request.session` to be populated when the gate checks it. Also shipped B4.4's required-minimum in-app expiration digest: `inventory_service.get_expiring_digest()` (reusing existing urgency-scoring logic) backing a new `GET /api/inventory/expiring-digest` endpoint and an `ExpiringDigestBanner.jsx` mounted app-wide (not just on the Inventory page) so expiration awareness actually reaches the user. Push/email notifications and full MFA/multi-user auth are explicitly, not silently, out of scope -- documented in both this session log and the code itself. Verified: 23 new unit tests (188 total, all passing) plus a thorough live end-to-end pass covering the full auth lifecycle (enable, login, logout, wrong-password rejection, rate-limit tripping, disable requiring the current password, and the CORS/cookie fix specifically) and the expiring-digest split against real seeded data; clean frontend build. Not verified: on-screen appearance of the login screen, Security card, or banner.

- **2026-08-01**: Completed B10.3 (see notes section above), picked as the next item after B4.2 since of the three remaining author-requested B10 items it was the only one with no external-network dependency and a concretely scoped recommended build already on record from prior research. Re-verified live that Walmart still publishes no consumer purchase-history API or export button, and found that even the specific open-source browser extension people use for this doesn't publish a stable column schema -- confirming that shipping a hardcoded "Walmart" column-mapping profile would be an unverified guess, which this app's own discipline forbids. Built a fully generic CSV/XLSX order-history importer instead (`order_import_service.py`, new `openpyxl` dependency): keyword-based column-mapping suggestion, deterministic parsing into the same `VisionDetectedItem` preview shape every other intake source uses, and a new `OrderImportProfile` model so a household can save their own confirmed mapping once and reuse it. New `InventoryItem.unit_price` field (migration `e7a1c9f4b2d6`) captures price data for the first time in this app, partially unblocking B6.1 (cost tracking) as the backlog predicted. Frontend reuses B4.2's exact editable preview table, extended with Price/Purchased columns, plus a new upload-then-map-columns card and a saved-profile dropdown. Verified: 17 new unit tests (174 total, all passing) including a real column-mapping collision case ("Unit Price") caught by a test rather than assumed away; a live end-to-end pass covering profile CRUD, auto-guess vs. saved-profile vs. explicit-mapping precedence, an unsupported-file 400, and the full confirm-and-persist round trip for both CSV and XLSX; clean frontend build. Not verified: on-screen appearance, and behavior against a real (not synthetic) retailer export file.

Kept as a second, deliberately separate intake source alongside the existing `/vision-intake` (pantry snapshot) rather than merging them, since the two answer different questions and need different prompts, but both land in the same preview-then-confirm shape. New `POST /api/inventory/import` dispatches on file type (image -> vision describe, PDF -> text-extract-then-chat with an explicit empty-text guard for scanned receipts, else UTF-8-decode-then-chat) plus a bare-text path; new `POST /api/inventory/import/confirm` shares a new `_bulk_create_items()` helper with the existing vision-confirm endpoint. Frontend: a new "Import receipt/list" card on `InventoryPage.jsx` with a genuinely editable per-row preview table (name/category/quantity/unit/expiration/include-checkbox/remove), upgrading past the read-only list pattern the vision flow uses, since receipt imports produce more lines and more guesses likely to need correction. Verified: 4 new unit tests (157 total, all passing) covering prompt-template safety and receipt-shaped parsing; a live end-to-end pass covering every error branch (no input, undecodable file, blank-PDF-no-text-layer, Ollama-unreachable-502) plus the full confirm-then-list round trip; clean frontend build. - **2026-08-01**: Completed B10.5 (see notes section above), picked as the next item after B1.4 since B10 (author-requested) items carry priority weight and B10.5's stated blocker -- density-sourcing from B1.1's food-database layer -- was already satisfied. Added `unit_conversion_service.convert_for_display()` (the shared Imperial/Metric/Weight rendering layer B5.3 and B10.5 were always meant to share), USDA `foodPortions`-derived density parsing (`food_data_service._parse_usda_density`, verified against USDA's real JSON schema via search rather than assumed), a new nullable `RecipeIngredient.density_g_per_ml` column (migration `d4f9c2a8e1b3`), and a `GET /api/recipes/{id}?unit_system=` query param applied after servings scaling, never touching the persisted recipe. A genuine, documented side benefit: `compute_ingredient_grams` (B1.2) now also accepts a volume-quantity ingredient with a known density, so recipes written entirely in cups/tablespoons can newly reach "computed" nutrition status instead of always landing on "ai_estimated." New `default_unit_system` DB setting, same instant-apply-and-persist pattern as this session's Appearance theme picker. This also closes out B5.3's two previously-deferred pieces (the toggle UI and density-sourcing), marked complete in its own line item. Verified: 31 new unit tests (153 total, all passing) plus a live end-to-end pass covering original/metric/imperial/weight rendering, the display_unavailable flag, servings+unit-system composing correctly together, and the new setting's persistence. Frontend build clean. Not verified: on-screen appearance, and `_parse_usda_density` against a real (not fixture) USDA payload, since no live USDA network reaches this sandbox.

- **2026-08-01**: Completed B1.4 (see notes section above), picked as the next backlog item after the theming work per the author's "continue with the next item on the list" -- the top of the highest-priority backlog group (B1, nutrition data integrity) and one of the options already on the table two sessions ago. New `dri_service.py` computes per-member daily nutrient targets from age/sex/height/weight/activity_level using sourced, cited 2025-2030 DGA/IOM guidance (checked live rather than assumed, including catching that the 2025-2030 DGA -- released Jan 2026 -- raised its protein guidance to a debated 1.2-1.6 g/kg range from the older 0.8 g/kg RDA); returns `None` + `missing_fields` rather than ever guessing from incomplete member data. New `meal_plan_service.compute_nutrition_summary()` sums per-day/week totals from non-skipped recipe entries, each contributing its recipe's per-serving nutrition exactly once regardless of `entry.servings` (a documented, deliberate one-serving-per-person-per-meal simplification), with an honest entry-count-vs-contributing-count split so recipes lacking nutrition data don't silently vanish or get blended in as zeroes. New `GET /meal-plans/{plan_id}/nutrition-summary` endpoint combines both. Frontend: new `NutritionSummaryPanel.jsx` on `MealPlanPage.jsx` (day/week totals table plus per-member targets and a week-vs-7-day-target percentage). Verified: 27 new unit tests (120 total, all passing) plus a live end-to-end pass covering a full-data member, a data-missing member, a recipe with nutrition and one without, and multi-day totals -- all behaving as designed. Frontend build clean. Not verified: on-screen appearance (no browser available).

- **2026-08-01**: Added multi-theme support (see notes section above), the author's direct request after noticing Fiduciary's Settings page has a theme picker. Investigated Fiduciary's actual mechanism first via a research subagent (a useful correction: its frontend is a single vanilla-JS file, not React) rather than assume it matched Chef's stack, then ported the `data-theme`-attribute CSS-variable-override mechanism into Chef, adapted to a DB-backed setting (`ui_theme`, one new `SettingSpec` entry, no schema change) instead of Fiduciary's localStorage-only persistence, per this project's own "persistent across container rebuilds" requirement -- localStorage is kept only as an instant-apply cache for flash-of-wrong-theme avoidance via a new inline pre-hydration script in `index.html`. Ported Fiduciary's four themes (`amber`/`cobalt`/`highcontrast`/`daylight`) verbatim from its source CSS. Fetched Catppuccin's actual palette live from catppuccin.com/palette (not from memory) and added all four flavors (`catppuccin-latte/frappe/macchiato/mocha` -- author explicitly chose all four over just the flagship Mocha when asked), with a documented, uniformly-applied token mapping since Catppuccin's naming doesn't align with Chef's semantic vars. New `frontend/src/themes.js` registry; new "Appearance" swatch-card picker on `SettingsPage.jsx`; 8 new `:root[data-theme]` blocks in `theme.css`. A genuinely nice property surfaced during the port: Chef's existing `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-x) N%, transparent)` usage (from B1.2/B3.1's badges and warning banners) meant none of those derived tints needed Fiduciary's manual per-theme rgba-literal restating -- they recompute live from whichever theme is active for free. Verified: clean frontend build with all 8 theme selectors surviving minification; a live end-to-end pass confirming `GET`/`PATCH /system/settings/ui_theme` persists correctly against a freshly migrated+seeded database. Not verified: actual visual appearance of any theme in a browser (no browser available, same standing limitation as every other UI-only change this project). Proceeding to the next backlog item per the author's request to continue after this.

- **2026-07-31**: Completed B3.1 and B3.2 together (see their shared notes section above), the author's explicit pick over B1.4/Walmart-import/other options for the next backlog area. Built `app/services/allergen_service.py`, a deterministic (non-LLM) keyword/regex allergen check against a 10-entry taxonomy (FDA/FALCPA-and-FASTER-Act's nine major allergens plus gluten as its own category), with word-boundary anchoring, `"-free"` negation handling, and (B3.2) a gluten-observance-level setting plus oats-type cross-contact flagging. New `HouseholdPreferences.restricted_allergens`/`.gluten_observance_level` columns (migration `c3d8a1f5b7e2`), wired into all three points the backlog named -- recipe import preview, every recipe read, meal-plan generation preview -- plus a fourth the backlog implied but didn't spell out: meal-plan entry confirm, which now returns 409 with match details on a hard conflict unless the caller explicitly acknowledges it. Fixed two real regex bugs caught by testing (a naive `.search()` missing the second keyword hit in a multi-keyword ingredient name; a missing `\b` letting "nut-free" falsely suppress an unrelated "almond" tree_nuts match inside "peanut-free almond butter") and one general (non-allergen-specific) bug in `api.js`, where a structured 409 error body was being silently flattened to `[object Object]` before any caller could read it. Frontend: new shared `RestrictionWarnings.jsx` component reused across `RecipeDetailPage.jsx`, `RecipesPage.jsx`'s import review, `MealPlanPage.jsx`'s generation preview, and `MealPlanEntryRow.jsx`'s confirm-conflict dialog; `HealthPage.jsx` gained an allergen checkbox grid and observance-level select fetched from a new `GET /household/allergen-options` endpoint so frontend and backend taxonomies can't drift apart. Verified: 25 new unit tests (93 total, all passing); a live end-to-end pass against a real local uvicorn instance covering household-preferences persistence/validation, a test recipe with both a hard match ("wheat flour") and a cross-contact-only ingredient ("rolled oats") alongside a correctly-NOT-flagged "gluten-free vanilla extract", and the full 409-then-acknowledge-and-succeed confirm flow; a clean frontend `npm run build` with no transform errors. Not independently live-verified: the import-preview and generation-preview endpoints specifically (both need live Ollama the sandbox can't reach; covered instead by the shared unit tests of the functions they call) and actual on-screen appearance of the new UI (no browser available).

- **2026-07-31**: Completed B10.6 (print a recipe -- see its own notes section above), picked as the next backlog item since it had zero unresolved dependencies and was the lowest remaining effort, unlike B10.5 which needs a density-sourcing design first. Added `@media print` to `theme.css` plus a `.no-print` utility class applied to `RecipeDetailPage.jsx`'s navigational/interactive chrome (back link, servings/rating/staple controls, edit/print/delete buttons); the app-wide header/nav and the recipe-scoped chat widget are hidden unconditionally on print rather than needing per-instance tagging. Added a "Print recipe" button calling `window.print()`. Verified the build succeeds and the media query survives minification; explicitly flagged that actual print output was never visually checked (no browser available), same open verification gap as B10.4's responsive pass.

- **2026-07-31**: Completed a first pass of B10.4 (responsive mobile layout -- see its own notes section above), the next item per the sequence agreed with the author after B1.2/B1.3 and the BACKEND_PORT fix. Found and fixed a real, independent bug while surveying the codebase before writing any CSS: `.form-row`'s flex/gap styling was accidentally scoped to `.item-form .form-row`, so every form outside `RecipeForm`/`InventoryItemForm` (meal-plan generation, chat widgets, knowledge-file upload, grocery add-item, recipe detail meta row) had no real row layout at all -- generalized to a plain `.form-row` rule after checking all 10 usage sites. Added one `@media (max-width: 640px)` block to `theme.css` covering: universal form-row stacking, a CSS-only (data-label attribute + `::before`) conversion of the inventory table to stacked cards, meal-plan entry rows stacking full-width, the chat widget becoming a full-screen overlay, a horizontally-scrollable nav, and basic touch-target sizing. Verified the build succeeds and that the media query/data-label selectors survived minification, but explicitly flagged that nothing was checked in an actual browser at a mobile viewport -- this is a reasoned first draft, not a visually-confirmed one.

- **2026-07-31**: Fixed a real deployment blocker the author hit (see its own notes section above): port `8095` was already taken on their host, and changing `BACKEND_PORT` in `.env` alone didn't work because the production frontend build talked to the backend at a hardcoded port baked in at build time. Replaced the build-time constant with a runtime-config file (`frontend/docker-entrypoint.sh` writes `dist/config.js` from the container's live `BACKEND_PORT` env var on every start, mirroring the backend's own entrypoint pattern) that `src/api.js` reads instead -- changing `.env` and running `docker compose up` (no `--build`) is now enough. Also added `frontend/public/config.js` as a real default (caught, via an actual curl against a live dev server rather than assumption, that without it `npm run dev` would serve Vite's SPA-fallback `index.html` for the unmatched `/config.js` request and the browser would try to execute that HTML as JavaScript) and made `vite.config.js`'s dev-proxy target fall back to `BACKEND_PORT` instead of a hardcoded `8095`. Updated README's "Known limitation" section and `.env.example` accordingly. Verified the whole chain by building the frontend, running the entrypoint script against the built `dist/` with a custom port, confirming the bundle references `window.__CHEF_CONFIG__` at runtime (not a folded-in constant), and serving the result with a plain HTTP server to check the exact bytes end to end.

- **2026-07-31**: Completed B1.2 and B1.3 together (see their shared notes section above), the next items per the sequence agreed with the author after B1.1 and the shared unit-conversion layer. B1.3: reconciled the three AI surfaces that ask for recipe nutrition (`recipe_service.RECIPE_IMPORT_PROMPT` had 7 keys, `RECIPE_MODIFY_INSTRUCTIONS` had no explicit list, `meal_plan_service.MEAL_PLAN_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` had only 4) onto one shared 9-key set (`food_data_service.NUTRITION_KEYS`, matching what USDA/OFF resolution already produces) via a new `NUTRITION_PROMPT_HINT` string substituted into all three templates; investigated and deliberately did not add `added_sugars_g`/`soluble_fiber_g` since neither source cleanly distinguishes them from totals. B1.2: `Recipe.nutrition_provenance` (migration `8f2c4b6a1d9e`), new pure `compute_ingredient_grams`/`compute_recipe_nutrition` functions in `food_data_service.py` that sum resolved ingredients' cached per-100g nutrients scaled to actual gram weight (mass units only -- volume units still need a density source nothing in this codebase provides yet, a known limitation shared with B10.5's still-pending weight-mode toggle), a new `POST /api/recipes/{id}/compute-nutrition` endpoint, and provenance bookkeeping on the normal create/update paths (an AI guess and a manual edit share the "ai_estimated" bucket; an update only resets provenance when the nutrition dict actually changed, avoiding a subtle bug where RecipeForm's always-resent nutrition fields would otherwise silently demote a computed recipe back to "ai_estimated" on every unrelated edit). Also fixed a real, independently-existing bug found during the code survey: `RecipeForm.jsx` only ever round-tripped 4 of the up-to-9 nutrition keys, silently dropping the rest on every save -- replaced with a `NUTRIENT_FIELDS`-driven fieldset covering all 9. `RecipeDetailPage.jsx` gained a computed/partial/ai_estimated badge and a "Compute from ingredients" button. Verified: 15 new unit tests (68 total in the repo, all passing) covering every provenance branch with hand-checked arithmetic; full migration chain re-applied cleanly; a live local-uvicorn pass (hand-simulating a USDA/OFF resolution via direct DB writes, since the sandbox has no network route to either API) confirmed "partial" with a real tbsp-of-olive-oil unit-conversion gap and "computed" once that ingredient was switched to grams, both with hand-verified summed numbers, plus the unchanged-vs-changed-nutrition PATCH provenance behavior and a 404 case; frontend `npm run build` clean and a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass confirmed `nutrition_provenance` round-trips through the actual proxy path and both touched components transform cleanly via Vite's dev pipeline.

- **2026-07-31**: Wired the B5.3/B10.5 unit-conversion layer into the two real call sites that had been comparing/summing raw numbers across mismatched units (see the updated B5.3/B10.5 notes section above for full detail). `inventory_service.deduct_by_name()` now converts a deduction quantity into the matched item's own unit when both are known and differ (fixed a real bug: deducting "8 oz" from an item logged in pounds previously subtracted 8 from the pound quantity directly); `meal_plan_service.aggregate_ingredients()` now merges same-name ingredient lines across convertible units instead of only an exact unit match; `meal_plan_service.subtract_inventory()` now converts on-hand inventory into the grocery line's unit before comparing (fixed a second real bug: a "2 lb" need against a "500 g" on-hand row was previously compared as raw numbers, silently treating it as covered). All three fall back to the pre-existing behavior when no conversion is available, so this is additive, not a regression. 15 new tests added (53 total in the repo), plus a live curl pass confirming the exact expected post-deduction quantity through the real HTTP endpoint. Next per the agreed sequence: B1.2/B1.3 (nutrition summation + provenance, prompt key reconciliation) or B10.4 (responsive UI); B10.5's frontend toggle still needs a density-sourcing helper designed first.
