- **2026-07-31**: Added backlog group **B10 (author-requested)** after the author reviewed the capstone findings -- six items, flagged as carrying higher priority weight than inferred items since they are stated wants: a dining-out/restaurant finder with a configurable radius and diet filtering (B10.1); notifications and authentication mirrored from the Fiduciary project rather than invented, absorbing B4.4 and B9.4 (B10.2); Walmart integration and order-history import (B10.3); a real mobile UI alongside desktop (B10.4); an Imperial/Metric/Weight toggle on a viewed recipe (B10.5); and recipe printing (B10.6). Two integration questions were researched rather than assumed. **Walmart publishes no public consumer purchase-history API** -- developer.walmart.com's Order Management/On Request Reports/DSV APIs are seller-side and require a Marketplace account, and Walmart's site has no export button; the working consumer paths are third-party browser extensions exporting to XLSX/CSV. Recommendation recorded: build a generic order-history CSV/XLSX importer with a per-retailer column-mapping profile (Walmart first) rather than depending on any single export path, explicitly no scraping and no credential handling. **No clean free API exists for celiac-grade restaurant safety data** -- Find Me Gluten Free has no public API (only scrapers), so the recommendation is OpenStreetMap's Overpass API and its `diet:*` tagging namespace as the primary free source, Tavily (already keyed in this app) for enrichment, and Google Places/Yelp only as optional user-configured keys. Also recorded a dependency-sequencing note at the top of the backlog: B1.1, the shared B5.3/B10.5 unit-conversion layer, and B10.4 are each load-bearing for several other items and are cheaper done early than retrofitted. Corrected two details in the B1-B9 items during verification: `recipe_service.py` and `meal_plan_service.py` request different nutrition key sets from the model (7 keys vs 4, neither including saturated fat), and `frontend/dist.old/`+`dist.stale2/` are not gitignored while the vite timestamp files are. No code changed this session.

- **2026-07-31**: Capstone assessment -- competitive/market research pass, then a gap analysis against the current app. Research was done first and deliberately without reading Chef's own code, so findings would not be anchored on what already exists; written up in the new "Capstone research" section (incumbent meal planners and what each is best at, nutrition data services and their licensing/cost implications for a self-hosted repo, encodable nutrition standards, celiac/allergen tooling, inventory capture mechanisms, cooking-time UX, recipe interchange formats, health-data integration, and a grocery-pricing reality check). Repopulated the previously-empty Backlog with 9 themed groups / 31 candidate items (B1-B9). The headline finding is B1: `Recipe.nutrition` is a free-form JSON dict populated entirely by LLM estimate with no authoritative food-composition source anywhere in the codebase, so cholesterol steering, calorie counts, and BMI-aware portioning all currently rest on an unverified number -- USDA FoodData Central plus Open Food Facts (both free, both self-host-friendly) is the recommended fix. Other notable gaps: the knowledge/RAG layer works but ships with an empty corpus, so the brief's "grounded in nutritionist knowledge" requirement is unmet out of the box (B2.1); dietary restrictions are free text with no deterministic safety check and no cross-contact awareness, which for a celiac primary user is a safety gap rather than a feature gap (B3.1/B3.2); inventory lacks the two capture mechanisms the whole food-waste category has converged on, barcode and receipt OCR (B4.1/B4.2); leftovers and batch/prep-day planning are absent despite "leftovers welcome" being in the brief (B5.1/B5.2); there is no cook mode, no timers, no PWA, and essentially no ARIA attributes (B7); and the test suite every phase was verified with was never committed to the repo (B9.1). No code changed this session -- research and tracker only.

- **2026-07-31**: Extended the recipe variants feature (see notes above) after the author asked a direct follow-up: does the chat-commit mechanism support overwriting the original recipe or a specific variant, not just always creating a new one? Answer: the recipe-scoped chat already did (it operates on whatever recipe/variant page is open, and already offered both save paths); the persistent chat widget didn't (it was deliberately variant-only). Added a `mode` field ("variant", still the default, or "overwrite") to the persistent chat's `recipe_update_proposal` action, with `CHAT_CONTEXT_TEMPLATE` guidance on which phrasing implies which mode and an explicit fallback to "variant" (the safer choice) for anything ambiguous or unrecognized. `ChatWidget.jsx` branches `executeAction` on the mode and adds a native `window.confirm()` speed bump specifically before an overwrite executes, matching the app's existing convention for destructive actions. Verified via the same live-`TestClient`-with-mocked-Ollama approach as the base feature: overwrite mode correctly PATCHes the target recipe's content in place without disturbing its own `variant_count`/`parent_recipe_id`, an unrecognized `mode` value safely coerces to "variant" rather than being trusted, and both `ChatWidget.jsx` code paths (POST-variant and PATCH-overwrite) were simulated end-to-end. Frontend build clean.

- **2026-07-31**: Completed the recipe variants feature (see notes above), in response to a direct author question ("can chat-modified recipes ever be committed, not just ephemeral?") rather than a pre-queued backlog item. Confirmed via code inspection the capability didn't exist before building it. Author chose "both" chat surfaces and a variant/version system over always-overwrite, explicitly prioritizing never accidentally serving a recipe out of sync with dietary needs. New self-referential `Recipe.parent_recipe_id`/`variant_label` (migration `349c7fd3ed4c`, `op.batch_alter_table` for SQLite FK support) -- a variant is a full independent Recipe row, not a diff/version-history entry, the simplest structure satisfying the stated goal. Recipe-scoped chat (Phase 4.1) extended to optionally propose a full edited recipe (`RECIPE_MODIFY_INSTRUCTIONS`/`parse_recipe_chat_response`), still preview-then-confirm. Persistent chat (Phase 7) gained a `recipe_update_proposal` action that is deliberately variant-ONLY (never overwrite-in-place) since that surface's one-click confirm has no review form -- the riskier overwrite path is only reachable through the recipe-scoped chat's fuller `RecipeForm`-based review step; also added a lightweight recipe id/title catalog to the persistent chat's context so it can reference real recipes without inventing ids, same discipline as the existing entry_id/meal_plan_id guard. New `GET /{id}/variants` endpoint and `parent_recipe_title`/`variant_count` on every `RecipeRead`. Frontend: `RecipeChat.jsx` gained a review card (variant-vs-overwrite radio choice, editable variant label, reusing `RecipeForm` via a new `submitLabel` prop) that navigates to a new variant or reloads the recipe in place on overwrite; `RecipeDetailPage.jsx` shows a parent link, variant-label tag, and variants list; `ChatWidget.jsx` gained the new action's execute case and a "View saved variant" link on confirm. Verified end-to-end via a live `TestClient` against a fresh seeded DB with mocked Ollama, covering both chat surfaces' propose/save flows (variant and overwrite), the variant count/list/parent-title round-trip, the recipe catalog actually appearing in the generated system prompt text, and a deliberately hallucinated action type being silently dropped; frontend `npm run build` clean (worked around the documented FUSE-mount `EPERM`-on-stale-`dist/`-delete issue with `os.rename` instead of `rm -rf`).

- **2026-07-31**: Completed the knowledge/RAG backlog item (see notes above), reconnecting the Fiduciary project folder to read `knowledge.py`/`store.py` directly rather than working from memory of it. New `KnowledgeChunk` table + `KnowledgeFile.indexed_embed_model` column (migration `5ee4a7ad013b`); `knowledge_service.py` gained chunking (`chunk_text`), cosine similarity, indexing (`ensure_indexed`), and retrieval (`search_knowledge`); a new `ollama_embed_model` setting (default `bge-m3`) and `ollama_client.embed()`. `health_service.build_knowledge_context()` now takes a `query` and returns top-k retrieved excerpts instead of the whole active corpus truncated to a fixed budget. Wired into meal-plan generation (a synthetic query built from dietary restrictions/goals) and, closing a real gap rather than just upgrading an existing mechanism, into persistent chat for the first time (the user's actual message as the query -- chat previously had zero knowledge-file grounding). Adapted rather than 1:1 ported from Fiduciary: no content-hash-keyed cross-file dedup tables, since Chef's knowledge files are explicit user uploads with immutable content rather than files discovered by scanning a watched folder; no model-invoked tool-calling, since nothing else in this codebase uses native tool-calling and Ollama tool-calling reliability is unverified; no background-thread reindexing, since Chef's corpus is small enough for synchronous indexing to be fine. Knowledge-file upload now eagerly indexes (best-effort) and the list UI shows a chunk-count/indexing-status tag. Verified extensively with a from-scratch script run directly against a live test DB (32 checks): pure-function tests for chunking/cosine similarity; indexed a 5-file corpus with deliberately distinct topics using a deterministic stand-in embedding (no real Ollama in the sandbox); confirmed topically-targeted queries correctly rank relevant files above irrelevant ones at a small k; confirmed both integration points' resulting knowledge_context actually appears in the literal generated prompt/system-prompt text and is meaningfully shorter than the full corpus; confirmed model-change invalidation, deactivation-excludes-from-retrieval, and cascade-delete-removes-chunks all work correctly; separately confirmed via live curl that upload eagerly indexes and delete cleans up chunk rows. Backend/frontend both build clean; a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass confirmed the new `chunk_count` field and `ollama_embed_model` setting surface correctly with no additional frontend changes needed for the latter.

- **2026-08-02**: Fixed an author-reported live bug (real iPad testing) and completed the author's full prioritized punch list in order ("1, 3, 4, 2"): the B7 cook-mode/PWA cluster, B5.2, B8.1, then Phase 10. Bug: Dining Out's "Use my location" stuck permanently on "Locating..." -- root cause was `getCurrentPosition()` called with no `timeout` (default `Infinity`), which a WiFi-only iPad's WiFi-based (no-GPS-chip) positioning can hang on indefinitely with neither callback ever firing; fixed with an explicit timeout, a JS-side fallback timer, and error-code-aware messaging that no longer always blames HTTPS now that B15.1 shipped -- see its own notes section above. B7.1 (cook mode): full-screen step-at-a-time overlay with Wake Lock, per-step done-tracking persisted to localStorage, an ingredients/step-list drawer, and inline temperature conversion. B7.2 (step timers): regex-parsed durations from instruction text, a localStorage-backed background timer store independent of any one page (mirrors the chat widget/job-badge "stays alive across navigation" pattern), a three-beep chime plus optional browser Notification on completion. B7.3 (PWA): manifest + service worker (app-shell caching only, API calls never cached), a programmatically-generated chef-hat icon set, registered guarded by secure-context support. B5.2 (prep-day/batch-cooking): scoped down from a literal shared-component/inventory-linking model (a much bigger schema change) to a generation-prompt heuristic -- an optional `prep_day` field steers the model to batch-cook reusable components on one day and favor fast-assembly recipes reusing them on others, tagged with existing `make_ahead`/`quick` tags, no new persisted linking concept. B8.1 (bloodwork import): same preview-then-confirm architecture as recipe import, extended past the backlog's CSV/PDF framing to also accept a photo of a printed report; runs through the B11.1 job queue as the first genuinely new AI-consuming endpoint added since that backlog item, so it never needed a follow-up blocking-I/O conversion the way six pre-existing endpoints did. Phase 10 (QA): a code-level audit against the original project brief's full feature list (no gaps found), plus a new `test_end_to_end_smoke.py` that chains FIVE routers in one realistic household flow (prefs -> inventory -> recipe -> plan -> confirm -> deduction -> health metric + BMI) -- the first committed test exercising that cross-router path together rather than each router tested in isolation. 473 backend tests passing (up from 458), all re-run in batches this session; clean `vite build` (306 modules) reconfirmed after every frontend change. Not verified from this sandbox: any actual on-screen behavior (cook mode, the PWA install prompt, the bloodwork import panel, the geolocation fix itself) -- worth the author's hands-on pass, especially on the same iPad that reported the original bug.

- **2026-08-02**: Fixed an author-reported recipe-import usability bug, reproduced from a screenshot plus the actual PDF that triggered it (see "Fix: recipe-import review form usability + ingredient-parsing accuracy" section above for full root-cause detail). Two of the three reported symptoms ("excessively small field sizes," "ingredients seem amazingly wrong") turned out to be one CSS layout bug -- `RecipeForm.jsx`'s ingredient-row inputs had no flex-basis/min-width, so the row wrapped mid-ingredient on desktop widths between the 640px mobile breakpoint and full width, making one parsed ingredient look like two broken ones. Fixed with explicit per-field classNames + flex sizing in `theme.css`. Cross-checked the parsed data against the source PDF's actual text (extracted independently with both `pypdf` and `pdfplumber`) and confirmed the ingredient parser also has a real, separate accuracy problem: it invented a wrong unit conversion for one ingredient, bled a "divided" modifier from one same-named ingredient onto a different one in another section, and dropped stated quantities for two others. Hardened `recipe_service.RECIPE_IMPORT_PROMPT` against exactly those three failure modes (no unit conversion, no guessing, no cross-section modifier bleed). Also added an explicit `::placeholder` style so an under-populated import's empty fields read as "needs review" rather than blending into a wash of grey text, addressing the third symptom ("grey on white... difficult to see"). `npm run build` clean (306 modules, built from a `/tmp` scratch copy per the FUSE-mount note). Explicitly not verified: no reachable Ollama instance from this sandbox to confirm the hardened prompt actually fixes this specific recipe's extraction, and no browser to visually confirm the layout fix -- flagged for the author's hands-on re-test of the same PDF.

Completed Phase 5 (see notes above): AI-assisted weekly meal-plan generation grounded in inventory urgency/priority (reusing Phase 3's scoring), household preferences, and kitchen equipment; a new kitchen-profiles CRUD router that no earlier phase had built; preview-then-confirm plan creation with automatic new-recipe creation for slots the catalog can't fill; a grocery list derived from planned ingredients minus current inventory, kept in sync as entries are edited/confirmed/skipped; confirm/skip actions with inventory deduction on confirm; and a matching Meal Plan page (generate form with per-day guidance, editable review step, weekly grid, grocery list panel). Verified extensively: unit tests for every pure service function, a live curl sequence against a fresh local backend with mocked Ollama covering generation (including a deliberately hallucinated recipe id, correctly nulled out), plan creation, grocery-list math (hand-verified quantities), confirm/skip/regenerate/PATCH-resync, 404s, and cascade delete; frontend `npm run build` clean; full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass through the actual proxy path. Completed Phase 6 (see notes above): household preferences and per-member profile management, health-metric logging with server-computed BMI, rolling-window trend computation over weight/BMI/cholesterol, both wired into Phase 5's meal-plan generation prompt so body metrics actually steer plan selection (verified by asserting the injected text appears in the real generated prompt, not just that a context dict was built); nutritionist knowledge-file upload/extraction/grounding (also injected into generation, same verification approach); a matching Health page with member management, a metric-logging form, dependency-free inline-SVG trend charts, and a knowledge-file panel. Fixed a fragile (accidentally-working) name collision in `main.py` between the `/health` endpoint function and the newly-imported `health` router module. Verified via unit tests for every pure function (BMI, trend windowing including the fallback-to-earliest-when-nothing-in-window case, knowledge-excerpt truncation), live curl against a fresh local backend, direct inspection of the actual generated prompt text, a clean frontend build, and a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass through the actual proxy path. Author requested one deferred feature before Phase 7: optional recipe dish images (manual upload plus auto-capture during import), fully scoped and recorded in the Backlog section above rather than implemented immediately. Completed Phase 7 (see notes above): persistent, session-based chat history (`ChatMessage.actions` JSON column added via migration); implicit sessions derived from distinct `session_id` values rather than a separate table; context grounding that injects the real, current meal-plan entries and inventory items (with their actual ids) into the system prompt, verified by inspecting the literal generated prompt text; a chat-action-proposal system where the model can propose inventory/meal-plan changes but nothing is ever executed by chat code itself -- confirming an action calls the exact same pre-existing endpoint the rest of the app already uses, including two new small name-based inventory endpoints (`/deduct`, `/update-by-name`) added because chat needs to resolve natural-language ingredient names to rows; a `ChatWidget` mounted once at the `App.jsx` shell level, outside `<Routes>`, so it stays alive across route navigation with history restored from the server on page reload. Verified extensively: unit tests for response parsing and action coercion; a live curl sequence against a fresh local backend with mocked Ollama covering plain replies, each action type, malformed-response fallback, a deliberately hallucinated meal-plan id, history round-trip, and session listing/deletion; confirming each proposed action for real through its actual endpoint and hand-verifying the resulting state, including confirming the hallucinated id correctly 404s rather than silently succeeding; frontend `npm run build` clean; a full live Vite-dev-server + real-backend pass through the actual proxy path, including confirming in the served dev source that the widget mounts outside `<Routes>` as intended.
